# Audit file — v7.7.1.3 (R6), the producer audit of the bundle

_The audit prompts, the reports and the dispositions of the producer audit of R6 (MEDIUM: one internal round, a second after a new HIGH or MEDIUM, and one independent auditor, whose two re-audits of the changed parts were followed by a third internal round in place of a third re-audit; PLAN-037, PLAN-040, PLAN-044, PLAN-047, PLAN-048), in place of separate report files (`Docs/templates/prompt-audit-template.md` §7 step 5). Written by the planning session; the coding agent commits it as a supplied file (PLAN-046). Severities as PLAN-037 defines them._

## 1. Internal rounds 1 and 2

### Round 1 (2026-11-24): the audit prompt

The prompt of the internal round, as the planning session gave it to a fresh sub-agent of the same model (paths of the planning session's scratch folder shortened to `<SP>`):

```text
You are an independent auditor of a prompt bundle, before it goes to a coding agent. This is the internal
audit of the planning workflow (PLAN-040, PLAN-043, PLAN-044): you read the bundle cold, as an external auditor
would, and you EXECUTE it, as the coding agent would. You did not write the bundle.

Project: github.com/GCV-Sleeper-Service/ESP32-GW-multi-sensor, an ESPHome firmware project with a planning
method. A clone of its main (a33a5cc) is at <SP>/ESP32-GW-multi-sensor (read-only: do not commit, branch or
check out there; make your own clones). The method of record (the HowTo, tag v2.3.1) is at
<SP>/HowTo-LLM-Assisted-Workflow-v2.

The bundle is the step R6, v7.7.1.3, "per-device persist write path, dual-write": a firmware step, tier MEDIUM
(intent, interface contracts and the tests that encode them; the agent writes the code). The setup file commits
the prompt and the check file; Task Group 0 copies ten supplied documentation files (the review file, the audit
file, the operator page, the handoff, the Batch 2 assumption audit with a new section 4, the process guide, the
producer contract, the prompt-audit template, the decision log with the new row PLAN-046, the plan of record).
Inputs, in <SP>/audit-r6/:
- r6-2026-11-24.tar: the agent prompt, its check file, and the ten supplied files under files/. The audit file
  prompts/phase7/v7.7.1.3-audit.md holds placeholders (@INTERNAL@, @EXTERNAL@) that the planner fills after
  the audits: pending by design.
- r6-setup.txt: the setup file. On the build host the operator pastes a paragraph that names it and its SHA-256;
  the agent checks the hash and follows it. It unpacks the tar from /root/Planning-Workflow/steps/R6/.
- readings.txt: the operator's board readings of 2026-11-24 that the bundle uses.
Do not read any other folder of the scratchpad (in particular not rh/, Planning-Workflow/, work/ or session
notes), and do not read the private planning repository on GitHub. Your audit must not depend on the planner's
notes, reference code or tools. You have no GitHub login: gh cannot reach GitHub here.

Sources that win over the bundle (cite them): main of the project repository: CURRENT-STATE.md,
Docs/plan-of-record.md, Docs/decisions/decision-log.md, Docs/development-process-guide.md, AGENTS.md,
prompts/producer-contract.md, Docs/planner-start-here.md, Docs/v7.7-v7.8-persistence-architecture.md,
prompts/prompt-index-and-workflow.md, the firmware and scripts as they are.

Operator decisions (2026-11-24) that the bundle records in PLAN-046; they are decisions, not findings:
1. The agent runs `body <PR#> final` as the last step of its last round and posts its last line.
2. Review B may run outside the runner: part B of the review file holds the system text and three turns for
   Gemini 4 Neon in Google AI Studio (default for R6; the runner the fallback); the check block reviewb writes
   the inputs; the operator's AI Studio app posts the comment with the operator's own fine-grained token.
3. PLAN-046's method changes go into R6's own PR.
4. c3heap compares .189 with its own reading just before the upload (fail when the higher of two readings at
   180 s or more is more than 2,048 B below it).
5. The WROOM .170 is flashed; the step stops when its min_free_heap is below 1,024 B at a reading of the step.
6. The login reaches the Codex agent through BASH_ENV from firmware/secrets.yaml.
7. R4's actual sessions: planning 3, operator 1.
8. A ring of 24 slots per device; a write skipped below 252 free NVS entries; the full 228-byte DeviceSegment;
   .173 erased and flashed over USB before dispatch (it has no history partition) and tested first; the sensor
   outside takes the MAC FA:2B:65:2C:98:39.
The planning session's design, which IS open to findings: the eight interfaces of item 2.2 and their
contracts, the supplied texts S2, T3 and H1, the JSON of persist_v2, every check of the check file (in
particular the blocks host, v2, dev, reviewb and the checkpoints A to F), the operator page, the review file
(parts A, B, C), the handoff, the assumption audit section 4, the texts of PLAN-046 in the process guide, the
contract, the audit template and the plan of record, and every other edit.

Pending by design, not findings: the PR number (<NN>, <PR#>); the records that the agent writes; the review
rounds; the gate record and the dispatch paragraph, which the planner writes after this audit; the operator's
fleet output and the USB flash of .173 on the day; the placeholders of the audit file.

Environment of this container (the planner prepared it; reuse it, install nothing new):
- source <SP>/tc/env.sh (ESPHome 2026.4.1 on PATH, the proxy CA, Playwright browsers). g++ is installed.
- node_modules: copy or hard-link <SP>/work/c3/node_modules into your clone (cp -al).
- Placeholder secrets for compiles: <SP>/tc/secrets.yaml (copy it to firmware/secrets.yaml of your clone).
- Login variables for the blocks: export ESP_USER=audit-user ESP_PASS=audit-value (placeholders; no board is
  reachable from here, so the board blocks cannot run against real boards).
- There is no /root/v7713 and no /root/config: stage the setup file's paths under a folder of your own and
  replace the two root paths (/root/Planning-Workflow and /root/config/ESP32-GW-multi-sensor) consistently, or
  run the blocks with REPO and SAVE edited in a COPY of the check file; say what you changed.
- A stub of gh that answers `gh auth status` is at <SP>/audit-r6/stubs/gh (put that folder first on PATH).

Your task:
1. Run the setup file as the agent would (on a clone whose origin/main is a33a5cc), then the prompt: §2, the
   scope block, Task Groups 0 to 5 and 7 with YOUR OWN implementation of item 2.2 and 4.1 written from the prompt
   alone (not from any planner file), every checkpoint, the host test, the C3 compile, and the PRE-PR block.
   For Task Group 6 you cannot reach the boards: read the blocks dev, v2, image, wroom24 and reviewb and judge
   them by reading, and run what can run locally (for example the WROOM and S3 compiles of item 6.1 and 6.3).
2. Report every place where the prompt, the check file or a supplied text is wrong, ambiguous, untestable, or
   would make an honest agent stop or a careless one pass: each with file, quoted text, why it matters, a fix,
   and a severity by PLAN-037 (CRITICAL, HIGH, MEDIUM, LOW; QUESTION for less than about 80 % sure).
3. Check the write-path contract for correctness against the firmware as it is: ESP-IDF NVS calls, the old
   engine, the hourly lambda, Rule 40, Rule 61, heap use on a board with about 2 KB of minimum free heap, and
   what v7.7.1.4 will need to read the blobs.
4. Check the operator page commands for the .173 USB flash and the review B steps, and the review file's part B
   against Docs/templates/external-review-template.md and scripts/external-review.py.
5. Check that the texts of PLAN-046 say the same thing in every place (contract rule 1.4).

Output: one report, in this form:
## Verdict: READY / FIX FIRST / BLOCKED
## What I ran (commands and results, one line each)
## Findings (by severity; each: file, quote, why, fix)
## Questions
Write the report to <SP>/audit-r6/report-round1.md and also return it as your final message.
```

### Round 1: the report


#### Verdict: FIX FIRST

Every gate, every checkpoint, the host test, the three compiles and the PRE-PR block passed the first time with my own implementation of items 2.2 and 4.1, which I wrote from the prompt alone. The setup file and the check file work as written. I found no CRITICAL. I found one HIGH: the block `v2` fails, instead of printing WAIT, when a board boots between minute 0 and minute 10 of an hour. An honest agent with correct code would then stop on roughly one upload in six, so about 40 % of the time across the three uploads. Fix it before dispatch. The MEDIUM findings are texts and design points that the planner can fix in the same render.

#### What I ran (commands and results, one line each)

Staging: `/root/Planning-Workflow`, `/root/config/ESP32-GW-multi-sensor` and `/root/v7713` became `<aud1>/root/...` in a staged copy of the setup file (16 lines changed); in a copy of the check file only `REPO=` and `SAVE=` changed; the original check file in SAVE stayed byte-identical. Origin: a local bare clone of main (`a33a5cc`). PR number stand-in: 247. Own fake `gh` for `reviewb`, `body` and `inline`. `ESP_USER`/`ESP_PASS` placeholders.

- `sha256sum`: tar `5496f193…`, setup file `7d062835…`. Setup step 0: 12 × OK. Steps 1, 2 and 4: branch, bootstrap `6cc2538`, prompt and check file 2 × OK, committed as `537e3e1` (`gh pr create` cannot run here).
- `sec2` → `§2 PASS`; `scope 247 2026-11-24` → `scope list written: 45 paths`; `copy` → `copied 10 files`, `CHECKPOINT 0 PASS`.
- 1.1 and 1.2 → `CHECKPOINT A PASS`. `insert S2`; own 8 definitions of 2.2; `insert T3`; assembly PASS. `B` → `CHECKPOINT B PASS`. `host` → `H1: all checks passed`, `host PASS`, first try. H1 against 7 mutants of my code: 1 caught (ring size ignored), 6 not (no `nvs_commit`; a meta of any length accepted; a device found by a 3-character prefix; status taken from the unwritten meta; status not refreshed on NVS_LOW; the `last_epoch == 0` check removed).
- 3.1, 3.2 → `CHECKPOINT C PASS`. 4.1 → `CHECKPOINT D PASS` (valid JSON shape; each printed part at most 78 characters).
- `bump-version.sh 7.7.1.3` and the AGENTS.md pipeline → rc 0; the changed files are exactly the 19 of §3. C3 compile SUCCESS: RAM 60,532 B, flash 1,438,228 B; `E` → `C3 image: 1438624 B … (81.3 %)`, PASS.
- 6.1 S3 compile SUCCESS: RAM 66,844 B, flash 1,310,115 B; `image s3` 1,310,512 B (74.0 %). 6.3 WROOM SUCCESS: RAM 72,256 B, flash 1,284,671 B; `image wroom` 1,285,072 B (72.6 %). Tree clean after `provision.sh satellite`.
- 7.1 to 7.6 with simulated device values → `CHECKPOINT F PASS`. `prepr` → `scope-gate: … 43 changed path(s), 45 allowed`, copy verify, A to F, host, lint (4 L6 warnings), preflight → `PRE-PR PASS at 83683b66…`.
- `reviewb 247` → PASS (turn1 93,553 B, turn2 319,331 B, turn3 1,618 B; the SHA-256 values equal the bundle's). `body 247` → `body PASS`; `body 247 final` with 10 passing check runs → `body final PASS`. `inline 247` → `inline PASS`.
- `v2` logic on synthetic answers: a first cycle → PASS; all pending → WAIT; a boot between :00 and :10 → `ERROR the old engine did not write in the cycle`; .173 with nothing restored → `ERROR 0 devices have the result ok or dedup…`.
- Read only: `dev`, `v2`, `wroom24`, `c3heap` against boards; the uploads; `gh pr ready` and the PATCH; the GitHub checks.
- Sources read: ESP-IDF 5.5.4 NVS (`HashListBlock` 128 B for 29 nodes; `Page::writeItem` inserts every item into the hash list; `free_entries` counts the reserved page and erased entries; `available_entries` = free − 126); ESPHome 2026.4.1 `upload_using_esptool` and the C3 build's `idedata` (bootloader at 0x0, partitions at 0x8000, ota_data at 0xd000): the operator's USB flash of .173 is sound.

#### Findings

##### HIGH

**H1. `v2` fails instead of waiting when a board boots between minute 0 and minute 10.** Check file `b_v2`: `if not last > last0: print("ERROR the old engine did not write in the cycle: …")` and `if len(good) < 3: print("ERROR %d devices have the result ok or dedup …")`; prompt 6.4: "Until a cycle has run since the boot it prints `v2 WAIT: ...`". The averages are added at :00, :15, :30 and :45; the persist runs at :10; at boot `restore_from_nvs()` refills RAM from the old engine for the three environmental sensors only. A board that boots between hh:00 and hh:10 has, at hh:10, no point newer than the one the old engine already saved: the old engine logs "Persist skipped" and its `last_persist_epoch` does not grow, while v2 writes three devices from the restored points and `wan_ping` is EMPTY. On .173 with nothing restored all four are EMPTY. The code is correct and the next cycle would pass, but a board check that stops is reported and never rerun. Fix: WAIT when the uptime is below about 4,500 s and the only failures are those two; advise uploads between minute 12 and minute 55; update the operator page and the lessons.

##### MEDIUM

**M1. `body <PR#> final` never prints the head, but four texts say the operator checks that its line names the head** (check file: `echo "body final PASS"`; operator page; PLAN-046 (1); contract 7.13; plan of record §6 item 5). Fix: print `body final PASS at <head sha>`.

**M2. The RAM estimate for the new NVS keys is about half of what ESP-IDF 5.5.4 uses** (handoff: "about 100 new NVS keys cost about 0.55 KB"; assumption audit §4.4). A blob is two items, the BLOB_IDX and one BLOB_DATA chunk, each a hash-list node; blocks are 128 B for 29 nodes, per page: about 1.0 to 1.5 KB of heap once the rings are full, plus 96 B static. A stop at the `wroom24` reading against 1,024 B is realistic. Fix: correct the figure, tell the operator, carry two nodes per blob key into the budget of v7.7.1.4.

**M3. The natural implementation of 4.1 truncates `persist_v2`** ("Use the buffer `num` …"; `char num[160]`): one device entry with realistic values is 178 characters; one `snprintf` per device truncates silently; only `v2` on .173 sees it. Fix: say in 4.1 that an entry must be printed in parts.

**M4. Nothing says what to do when a review round changes firmware after the boards were flashed** (review round item 4; PRE-PR compiles nothing). Fix: such a commit reruns 5.2 and 6.1 to 6.4 with the 24-hour window started again, or stops for the operator.

**M5. PLAN-046 is not stated the same way in every place**: `prompts/phase7/consolidated-audit-template-phase7.md`, External Reviewers: "B: the review runner, or a Muse session in three turns when the runner cannot run"; contract 8.3 and the audit-template line leave out "Muse only on a step without review C". Fix: the template line as an 11th supplied file; qualify Muse.

**M6. In AI Studio the model holds a GitHub write tool while it reads untrusted PR data** (operator page 8.3: "Then ask: `Post your answer of turn 3, unchanged, …`"; part B rule 2). The token can also edit or delete comments and close issues; the runner gives its model no tool. Fix: no tool for the model during the review, the post on an operator action after neutralising, or `gh pr comment --body-file`.

##### LOW

- **L1.** `c3heap` prints no `min_free_heap`, but 7.1 asks for one in the c3heap column.
- **L2.** The status after a failed meta write is ambiguous in 2.2 ("loaded or fresh, and written or not").
- **L3.** H1 misses four sentences: the commit after a meta write; a meta of the wrong length; a device id that is a prefix of another; the status on NVS_LOW right after a fresh meta.
- **L4.** The carry-forward to v7.7.1.4: with a full ring a power loss leaves a newer segment at `next_slot`, which the meta counts as the oldest; rows carry no metric identity.
- **L5.** The §7 criterion on `body final` refers to itself.
- **L6.** The setup file (steps 7, 8) and the prompt (review round item 1) order `gh pr ready` and the PATCH differently.
- **L7.** The operator page and the review file disagree on what follows a `reviewb` refusal; the block's message names rules that the review file lacks.
- **L8.** `reviewb` puts the system text into the first user turn; the runner sends it as `systemInstruction`.
- **L9.** CHECKPOINT A's text ("no tracked file outside `Docs/archive/`") claims more than the block checks.
- **L10.** The `CURRENT-STATE.md` row "Firmware `.bin` size checkpoint … **Not implemented**" goes stale; 7.1 does not update it.

The write-path contract is otherwise sound: the ESP-IDF calls (`nvs_get_blob` with an exact length, `nvs_set_blob`, `nvs_get_stats` with `free_entries ≥ 252`, which equals `available_entries ≥ 126`), the old engine untouched, v2 after it in the same lambda on the loop task (Rule 40), no new NVS call in the handler, Rule 61 (`maybe_yield_nvs_scan_(i)` yields once for 4 devices), the 228-byte segment allocated after the old engine freed its 232-byte snapshot, the entry arithmetic (a first write 14 entries; a full ring 244 per device, 976 in all, against 5,324 free).

#### Questions

- **Q1 (M6).** Does the operator's AI Studio app give Gemini a tool that posts to GitHub during the session, or does it post only on an operator action?
- **Q2.** `firmware/esp32-c3-multi-sensor.yaml` line 158 on main holds an `api: encryption: key:` literal (line given, not the value). `reviewb`'s key screen would refuse a diff whose context reaches it; R6's hunks in that file start at lines 1, 53, 232, 413 and 543. Is the operator aware?

### Round 1: dispositions

The auditor, a fresh sub-agent of the planning session with the same model, read the bundle cold with the prompt `steps/R6/r6-internal-audit-prompt.txt` (2026-11-24). It ran the setup file and the prompt with its own implementation of items 2.2 and 4.1, written from the prompt alone, to `PRE-PR PASS`, the three compiles included. It stopped once on the account's usage limit and was resumed. The harness refused its write of the report file, so the planning session saved its final message as `steps/R6/internal-audit-round1-report.md` in the planning repository. Its verdict: FIX FIRST, with 0 CRITICAL, 1 HIGH, 6 MEDIUM and 10 LOW findings and 2 questions. The planning session checked each finding against the sources. Each disposition below names what was changed. The rehearsal after the fixes passed: part 1 to `PRE-PR PASS`, part 2 45 of 45 cases, negative tests 41 of 41.

| Finding | Severity | Holds? | Disposition |
|---|---|---|---|
| H1: `v2` fails instead of waiting after a boot between minute 0 and minute 10 | HIGH | Yes: the averages are added at :00, :15, :30, :45; restore refills only the environmental sensors; the old engine skips an epoch that it already saved (`persist_hourly_segment()`) | Fixed. `v2` prints WAIT when the uptime is below 4,500 s and the only failures are "the old engine did not write" or "fewer than three devices written, the rest empty". The prompt asks for uploads between minute 12 and minute 55 (lessons, 6.1, 6.4). Part 2 tests both ways. |
| M1: `body final` does not print the head | MEDIUM | Yes | Fixed. The block prints `body final PASS at <head>`. Prompt review round item 7, the operator page and plan of record §6 item 5 are updated; the operator page adds a `gh pr view` line that prints the head. |
| M2: the RAM of the new keys is about twice the estimate | MEDIUM | Yes: `Page::writeItem` inserts each item into the hash list; a blob is two items; `HashListBlock` has 128 B for 29 nodes (`nvs_page.cpp`, `nvs_item_hash_list.hpp` of ESP-IDF 5.5.4) | Fixed. The handoff, assumption audit §4.4, plan of record §10 and PLAN-046 now say about 1.0 to 1.5 KB. The operator page says that a stop at the floor is possible. The operator is told before dispatch. |
| M3: one `persist_v2` entry is longer than `num` | MEDIUM | Yes: an entry is about 178 characters; `char num[160]` | Fixed in item 4.1: print each entry in parts. The reference prints two parts. |
| M4: no rule for a review-round fix to the firmware after the flashes | MEDIUM | Yes | Fixed in review round item 4: such a commit reruns 5.2, CHECKPOINT E and Task Group 6, and the 24-hour readings start again. |
| M5: the record template still names "the review runner, or a Muse session"; Muse is not qualified in contract 8.3 and the audit template | MEDIUM | Yes (`prompts/phase7/consolidated-audit-template-phase7.md`, line "External Reviewers") | Fixed. The template is the eleventh supplied file of `copy`, so the scope is 46 paths. Contract 8.3 and the audit template now say "Muse on a step without review C". |
| M6: in the app the model holds a GitHub write tool while it reads PR data | MEDIUM | Yes for the design of the first render (the operator's bridge exposes a posting route to the model) | Fixed. Review B now runs in a plain AI Studio chat without tools, with `system.txt` in the system instructions. The operator then posts the saved answer through the app (decision 2 (b)) or with `gh pr comment --body-file`. The review file part B, the operator page and the design part of PLAN-046 are updated. |
| L1: `c3heap` prints no `min_free_heap` | LOW | Yes | Fixed: each `c3heap` reading line prints it. |
| L2: the status after a failed meta write is ambiguous | LOW | Yes | Fixed in 2.2: the meta as written after step 8, and otherwise the meta as it was before the write, a fresh one included. |
| L3: H1 misses four sentences | LOW | Yes | Fixed. H1 adds: the commit right after each meta write; a meta of the wrong length; `wan` as a prefix of `wan_ping`; NVS_LOW after a fresh meta; a point of epoch 0. Five mutants were added to the negative tests, and each fails H1. |
| L4: the carry-forward to v7.7.1.4 is incomplete | LOW | Yes | Fixed in the handoff: segments ordered by their epochs, each segment checked before use, and metric identity left to decide. |
| L5: a §7 criterion refers to `body final` | LOW | Yes | Fixed: the criterion no longer names `body final`; review round item 7 and the operator page keep it. |
| L6: the setup file and the prompt order `gh pr ready` and the PATCH differently | LOW | Yes, as a difference | Not changed. The setup file mirrors block A of process guide §2.2, and the prompt's order is the specific one. |
| L7: different rules after a `reviewb` refusal | LOW | Yes | Fixed. In both places: run the runner, and if it refuses too, send both lines to the planning session. The block's messages say the same. |
| L8: the system text is inside the first user turn | LOW | Yes | Fixed. The block writes `system.txt`, and the operator puts it into the system instructions of the chat. |
| L9: CHECKPOINT A's text claims more than its check | LOW | Yes | Fixed: the text names the folders that the block searches. |
| L10: a `CURRENT-STATE.md` row on the `.bin` checkpoint goes stale | LOW | Yes | Fixed: item 7.1 updates the row, and F checks it. |
| Q1: does the app give the model a posting tool? | — | — | Answered by the design: the model of the review never runs in the app (M6). The operator is asked to confirm what the app exposes. |
| Q2: the `api: encryption: key:` literal of the C3 YAML | — | — | Known: RV-08; R18a moves it to `!secret` (plan of record §8). The `reviewb` key screen would refuse a diff that reaches it; R6's hunks do not. |

### Round 2 (2026-11-24): the audit prompt

The prompt of the second internal round, as the planning session gave it to a fresh sub-agent of the same model (paths of the planning session's scratch folder shortened to `<SP>`):

```text
You are an independent auditor of a prompt bundle before it goes to a coding agent: the second internal round
of the planning workflow (PLAN-040, PLAN-044; MEDIUM runs a second round when the first finds a new HIGH or
MEDIUM). You did not write the bundle and you did not run round 1.

Project: github.com/GCV-Sleeper-Service/ESP32-GW-multi-sensor. A clone of its main (a33a5cc) is at
<SP>/ESP32-GW-multi-sensor (read-only; make your own clones). The bundle is the step R6, v7.7.1.3,
"per-device persist write path, dual-write", tier MEDIUM. Inputs, in <SP>/audit-r6b/: r6-2026-11-24.tar
(the agent prompt, its check file and eleven supplied files under files/), r6-setup.txt (the setup file),
readings.txt (the operator's board readings), stubs/gh (answers only "gh auth status"). The round-1 report and
its dispositions are in <SP>/audit-r6b/round1/: do NOT read them before step 3 below.
Do not read any other folder of the scratchpad (not rh/, Planning-Workflow/, work/ except
work/c3/node_modules for a hard link, audit-r6/), and not the private planning repository.
Environment: source <SP>/tc/env.sh; g++ is installed; copy <SP>/tc/secrets.yaml to firmware/secrets.yaml
of your clone; export ESP_USER=audit-user ESP_PASS=audit-value (placeholders); stage the /root paths of the setup
file under your own folder (edit REPO and SAVE only in a COPY of the check file; say what you changed). No board
is reachable. Never push, never touch a board, never write a login, key or token value.

Scope of this round: the parts changed after round 1. Audit them cold first:
- the check file: the blocks v2 (the WAIT after a first cycle without a new point), body final (it prints the
  head), dev (c3heap lines), reviewb (system.txt apart from turn1.txt, the key screen, the refusal messages), F
  (the row of the .bin checkpoint), copy (eleven files), host and the supplied test H1 (new cases);
- the prompt: the lessons, 2.2 (the status rule), 4.1 (entries printed in parts), CHECKPOINT A's text, 6.1, 6.4,
  7.1, review round items 4 and 7, section 7, section 3 (46 paths);
- the supplied files: the review file part B (a plain AI Studio chat without tools; the answer posted by the
  operator's app or by gh pr comment), the operator page (step 8, "Before you merge", the trouble table), the
  handoff (risks, carry-forward), the assumption audit section 4, the plan of record (section 6 item 5, section
  10), contract rule 8.3, the prompt-audit template, the record template prompts/phase7/consolidated-audit-
  template-phase7.md, and the row PLAN-046 of the decision log.
Operator decisions of PLAN-046 are decisions, not findings (body final by the agent; review B in AI Studio with
Gemini 4 Neon, the comment posted by the operator's app with the operator's token; c3heap against the reading
before the upload; the WROOM floor of 1,024 B; a ring of 24 slots; a guard of 252 free entries; the full
DeviceSegment; .173 flashed over USB first; the new MAC of outside). The planning session's design is open to
findings. Pending by design: the PR number, the agent's records, the review rounds, the gate record, the audit
file's placeholders @INTERNAL@ and @EXTERNAL@, the operator's readings of the day.

Steps:
1. Run the setup file (staged) and the prompt with YOUR OWN implementation of 2.2 and 4.1, written from the
   prompt alone, to PRE-PR PASS; the host test included. The C3 compile is enough (the round-1 auditor compiled
   all three builds of the unchanged firmware texts).
2. Exercise the blocks v2, dev (c3heap) and reviewb as far as you can locally (for example a small local HTTP
   stand-in for /api/status/full and /api/storage-stats in a COPY of the check file with the board addresses
   mapped to localhost, and a stand-in gh for reviewb). Report each finding: file, quoted text, why, a fix, and
   a severity by PLAN-037 (CRITICAL, HIGH, MEDIUM, LOW; QUESTION below about 80 % sure).
3. Only then read round1/: for each round-1 finding, say whether its fix holds and whether it broke anything.
Output: "## Verdict: READY / FIX FIRST / BLOCKED", "## What I ran", "## Findings", "## Round-1 fixes",
"## Questions". Return the report as your final message (you cannot write files outside your folder).
```

### Round 2: the report

One quoted login form is rephrased, as marked: the runner's screen refuses it, and review B of this PR reads this file.

#### Verdict: FIX FIRST

I found no CRITICAL and no HIGH. I found two new MEDIUM findings and eight LOW. The main one is F1: the round-1 fix M4 (review round item 4) cannot run with this check file. The block `dev <IP> v7.7.1.2` and the block `v2 <IP> before` both stop once a board already runs v7.7.1.3. So a review-round fix to the firmware, or the one repair of the agent's own edit after a failure at the device stage, makes the agent stop. The fix is small. PLAN-037 does not bar dispatch on MEDIUM findings, but F1 would very likely cost a stop in this MEDIUM firmware step.

#### What I ran

My folder is `<SP>/audit-r6b/r2/`. Nothing was pushed, no board was touched, and no login value was written anywhere (I used the placeholders `ESP_USER`/`ESP_PASS`).

- `r2/setup-staged.txt`: only the `/root` paths are changed.

  - `r2/checks-keep.sh` keeps its temp folder.

**Board blocks against the stand-in boards** (`r2/standin/boards.py`, state set with `mk.py`):

- `v2`:

- `inline 247` → `inline PASS`.
- `body 247 final` → `body final PASS at f2d52ec…`; it fails while a later `pr-body-check` run is still queued.

The misses are under F2.

- Caught: no commit, the guard written as `<=`, a prefix id, a meta of any length, both NVS_LOW status rules, an epoch-0 point, no close, a short blob, a loop that stops at the first ERROR.

**Not run:** uploads, real boards, real GitHub, the S3 and WROOM compiles (the C3 compile is enough for this round).

#### Findings

**F1 — MEDIUM: Task Group 6 cannot be repeated once a board runs v7.7.1.3, so the M4 fix does not hold.**
- Prompt, review round item 4: "run 5.2 and CHECKPOINT E again and Task Group 6 from 6.1 on the new commit". Task Group 6 starts with `dev 192.168.120.173 v7.7.1.2` and `v2 192.168.120.173 before`.
- Check file, `b_dev`: `test "$v" = "$want" || fail "$ip reports $v, not $want"`.
- Check file, `b_v2`: `test "$v" = "v7.7.1.2" || fail "$ip reports $v: v2 $ip before belongs before the upload of v7.7.1.3"`.
- `c3heap` reads `$SAVE/dev-$ip-v7.7.1.2.txt`, which would be the stale reading of the first run.
- On the stand-in I ran the re-run calls: `⛔ CHECKPOINT FAILED - dev: 192.168.120.173 reports v7.7.1.3, not v7.7.1.2`, and the same stop for `v2 … before` and for .189.
- The lesson "A board check that stops is reported, never rerun with another value" forbids the obvious workaround.
- The same wall blocks the one repair of the agent's own edit after a stop at the device stage. Example: `v2 .173` stops on `JSONDecodeError` from a cut `persist_v2`; the repair needs a new upload of .173.
- Why it matters: firmware fixes in review rounds are common, and the agent would stop with the checkpoint-failure comment.
- Fix: give `dev` a `before` mode that accepts the version the board runs and writes `dev-$ip-before.txt`. Let `c3heap` read that file. Let `v2 <IP> before` accept v7.7.1.2 or v7.7.1.3. In item 4, write the exact calls of a re-run.

**F2 — MEDIUM: the key screen of `reviewb` misses the project's own secret names and GitHub tokens.**
- Check file: `key = re.compile(r"""\b(?:api[_-]?key|key|password|passwd|token|secret)…`.
- `\b` does not match after `_`, so these planted lines passed: `gateway_mgmt_password: "…"`, `wifi_password: "…"`, `ota_password: "…"`, `api_encryption_key: "…"`, `ESP_PASS="…"`, `github_pat_…`, `ghp_…`.
- `secrets.yaml` uses exactly the names `wifi_password` and `gateway_mgmt_password`.
- PLAN-046 (2) names this screen as the safeguard before AI Studio, which is an Unpaid Service.
- I rate it below HIGH only because whatever the screen misses is already public in the PR when the block runs. The operator may rate it HIGH ("leak a credential").
- Fix: allow a `\w*_?` prefix before the key words, add `PASS\b`, add the patterns `gh[pousr]_[A-Za-z0-9]{30,}` and `github_pat_\w{40,}`, and add these planted lines to the rehearsal's negative tests.

**F3 — LOW: the refusal message for a key hit sends the operator to the runner, which does not screen keys.**
- The message reads: "a key, password or token literal: run review B on the review runner instead …; if the runner refuses too, send both lines to the planning session".
- `scripts/external-review.py` `screen()` checks login literals only. The runner never "refuses too", so the data that holds the key goes to Agent Platform.
- Nobody acts on what AGENTS.md calls a Blocking finding (a credential in the PR).
- Fix: on a key hit, run review B nowhere. Send the line, which names only the place, to the planning session; the value is removed first.

**F4 — LOW: H1 leaves parts of the new status rule of 2.2 untested.**
- Prompt 2.2: "the meta as it was before the write … also after a failed meta write …; after a collision it leaves them".
- H1 has no case where the meta write fails, and its collision case checks no status field.
- Mutants that pass H1:
  - status copies the other device's meta on a collision;
  - status shows the unwritten meta after a failed meta write;
  - the meta's version is not checked;
  - `first_epoch` takes a zero epoch;
  - the loop stops after an NVS segment-write error.
- On the last one: the case "one failed device does not stop the others" checks `g_dev_persist_status[0]`. That is office, at index 0, which runs before the failing wan_ping at index 3.
- Fix: add cases for:
  - office's status unchanged after the collision cycle;
  - `g_fail_key` set to the meta key: ERROR, the segment written, `next_slot` unchanged, and the next cycle rewrites the same slot;
  - a meta with `DEV_HIST_VERSION + 1`: a fresh meta;
  - a row whose first point has epoch 0;
  - `writes_failed` of ghost and wan grew in the failed-write cycle.

**F5 — LOW: "printed in parts" (4.1, M3) is checked only on the board.**
- CHECKPOINT D checks neither the length of the parts nor the JSON.
- A cut entry first shows up as `v2 … JSONDecodeError` on .173, after a flash, and its repair then runs into F1.
- Fix: a host check that compiles the tail of `handle_storage_stats_()` with a fake `resp` and extreme values, then parses the output.

**F6 — LOW: `reviewb` executes the runner's code from the checkout.**
- Check file: `"$REPO/scripts/external-review.py"`, then `spec.loader.exec_module(rn)`. That is the PR branch, including any uncommitted change, run with the operator's `gh` login.
- The runner itself runs from `main` so that a PR cannot change what reviews it.
- Fix: load it from `git show origin/main:scripts/external-review.py` into `$TMP`.

**F7 — LOW: operator page step 8.3 checks the answer but gives no remedy.**
- Step 8.3 says the answer must "hold no line that starts with `/` and no `@`", but says nothing about what to do otherwise. For example, the answer may quote `-F body=@<file>` from the prompt.
- The runner's `clean()` handles this automatically; the AI Studio path does not. The trouble table has no row for it.
- Fix: one remedy, for example a block that applies `rn.clean()` and checks the header, and a row in the trouble table.

**F8 — LOW: the texts differ on the files of `reviewb` and on how review B is posted.**
- The review file's table says "the three files of the block `reviewb`"; part B, the operator page and the block write four.
- Process guide §2.4 names only the app as the poster, not `gh pr comment`.
- Fix: say "four files", and name both ways of posting in §2.4.

**F9 — LOW: the prompt-audit template was amended without a version bump.**
- The PLAN-046 line changed, but the file still says "Template version: v1.6" and the CHANGELOG has no new row.
- The template itself says to increment the version and append a CHANGELOG entry, and every earlier amendment did.
- Fix: add a v1.7 row.

**F10 — LOW: confusing wording in assumption audit §4, item 4.**
- It says "the page's figure of 5.5 KB for 1,000 keys counts less for blobs". This reads as if blobs cost less; a blob key has two hash-list items, so it costs about twice as much.
- Fix: "counts one item per key; a blob key has two".

#### Round-1 fixes

- **H1 (`v2` WAIT): holds.** On a first cycle below 4,500 s it waits; on later cycles it stops. A second WAIT can happen only for a boot outside the window of minute 12 to 55, which the lessons and 6.1 forbid. Nothing broke.
- **M1 (`body final` prints the head): holds.** Tested. The operator page, plan of record §6 item 5 and contract 7.13 agree.
- **M2 (RAM of the new keys): holds.** 1.0 to 1.5 KB everywhere, which matches ESP-IDF's 29 nodes per 128 B block and two items per blob. The only gap is the wording of F10.
- **M3 (entries in parts): holds as text.** It is not checked before the board (F5).
- **M4 (firmware fix in a review round): does not hold.** It cannot be executed (F1).
- **M5 (Muse qualified, 11 copied files): holds.** The scope list has 46 paths, and `copy` and `copy verify` pass. As a side effect, the prompt-audit template lost its version discipline (F9).
- **M6 (plain chat, `system.txt`, posting by the app or `gh pr comment`): holds** in the review file, the operator page and PLAN-046. It added a manual check without a remedy (F7). Q2 below remains.
- **L1 (`c3heap` prints `min_free_heap`): holds.**
- **L2 (status after a failed meta write): holds as text,** but it is untested (F4).
- **L3 (H1 additions): holds.** The five sentences are now caught (I confirmed the commit, length, prefix, NVS_LOW and epoch-0 mutants). Round 1's mutant "status taken from the unwritten meta" in its failed-meta-write form still passes (F4).
- **L4 (carry-forward): holds.**
- **L5 (§7 no longer names `body final`): holds.**
- **L6 (order of `gh pr ready` and the PATCH): unchanged by decision.** Acceptable.
- **L7 (one rule after a refusal): holds as text,** but it is wrong for key hits (F3).
- **L8 (`system.txt` apart from turn 1): holds.**
- **L9 (CHECKPOINT A's text): holds.**
- **L10 (the `.bin` row): holds.** F checks it, and it passed.
- **Q2 (the key literal in the C3 YAML):** R6's hunks do not reach it; `reviewb` passed on my diff.

#### Questions

- **Q1.** `c3heap` reads at 180 to 210 s, before the first v2 cycle at minute 10, so no v2 key exists yet. It cannot see the NVS RAM of the new keys, and the 24-hour `dev .189 v7.7.1.3` has no threshold. Is that intended, given that the C3 has about 28 KB of headroom?
- **Q2.** Is the operator's AI Studio app a model with a GitHub tool? If so, handing it the answer to post (text derived from untrusted PR data) brings back part of what M6 removed. `gh pr comment --body-file` keeps the separation.
- **Q3 (outside the changed parts).** When 24 hours have not passed, `wroom24` prints `⛔ CHECKPOINT FAILED` and exits 1, while 6.5 says to wait and run it again. The file header lists only `v2` and `inline` as blocks that wait. Should it print a WAIT line instead?

### Round 2: dispositions

Round 1 found a new HIGH and new MEDIUM findings, so a second internal round ran (PLAN-044). The auditor, a fresh sub-agent of the planning session with the same model, read the parts changed after round 1 cold, with the prompt `steps/R6/r6-internal-audit-prompt-round2.txt` (2026-11-24); it read round 1 only at its step 3. It ran the setup file and the prompt with its own implementation of items 2.2 and 4.1, written from the prompt alone, to `PRE-PR PASS` with the C3 compile, and ran the blocks `dev`, `v2` and `reviewb` against its own stand-ins. Its final message is `steps/R6/internal-audit-round2-report.md`. Its verdict: FIX FIRST, with 0 CRITICAL, 0 HIGH, 2 MEDIUM and 8 LOW findings and 3 questions. The planning session checked each finding against the sources (the check file, the prompt, `scripts/external-review.py` at `a33a5cc`). The rehearsal after the fixes passed: part 1 to `PRE-PR PASS` (without compiles: no firmware text changed in this round), part 2 62 of 62 cases, negative tests 46 of 46. No third internal round runs: GPT Astra's audit follows (PLAN-044). (A third round ran later, in place of a third re-audit, under PLAN-048: section 3.5.)

| Finding | Severity | Holds? | Disposition |
|---|---|---|---|
| F1: Task Group 6 cannot run again once a board runs v7.7.1.3, so the round-1 fix M4 cannot be carried out | MEDIUM | Yes: `dev` wanted v7.7.1.2 and `v2 <IP> before` accepted only v7.7.1.2 | Fixed. `dev <IP> before` takes the reading just before an upload on v7.7.1.2 or v7.7.1.3 and replaces the saved one; `c3heap` compares with it and stops when it belongs to the current boot; `v2 <IP> before` accepts both versions; the growth of `used_entries` counts only devices whose ring is not full. The prompt (Task Group 6 intro, 6.1 to 6.3, review round item 4) gives the calls of a re-run, the new boot epoch for 6.5, and the same rule for the one repair after a device check stopped. Part 2 runs a re-run on .189. |
| F2: the key screen of `reviewb` misses the project's own secret names and GitHub tokens | MEDIUM | Yes: `\b` before the key words does not match after `_` | Fixed. The screen matches the key words after any word prefix (`wifi_password`, `gateway_mgmt_password`, `api_encryption_key`, `ESP_PASS`) and GitHub tokens (`ghp_` and the like, `github_pat_`). Part 2 refuses seven planted lines; the script builds their values and never holds them whole. No hit on the rehearsal's R6 diff. |
| F3: after a key refusal, the texts sent the operator to the runner, whose screen checks login literals only | LOW | Yes (`screen()` of the runner) | Fixed. A refusal of the block means no review B on that data, in AI Studio or on the runner; the line, which names the place and never the value, goes to the planning session. The block's messages, review file part B, operator page step 8.1 and the trouble table, process guide §2.4. |
| F4: H1 leaves parts of the status rule of 2.2 untested | LOW | Yes: five mutants passed H1 | Fixed. H1 adds: the status unchanged after a collision (the planted meta has other counters); a failed meta write (error, the segment written, the status as before, the same slot in the next cycle); a meta of another version; a row whose first point has epoch 0 after a row without one; `writes_failed` of the devices after a failed write. Each of the five mutants now fails H1 (negative tests 42 to 46). |
| F5: entries "printed in parts" (4.1) are checked only on the board | LOW | Yes | Not changed. A cut entry shows on .173, the test board, first; with F1 fixed, the one repair runs Task Group 6 again. |
| F6: `reviewb` runs the runner's code from the PR's checkout | LOW | Yes | Fixed: the block loads `scripts/external-review.py` of `origin/main` with `git show`. |
| F7: operator page step 8.3 checks the answer but gives no remedy | LOW | Yes | Fixed. `reviewb <PR#> answer` checks that the saved answer begins with the header of `turn3.txt`, unchanged, and writes `comment.md` with the runner's own `clean()` (a line that starts with `/` is marked, `@` becomes `(at)`); the operator posts `comment.md`. Operator page 8.3, 8.4 and a trouble row; review file part B; process guide §2.4. Part 2 tests a missing answer, a changed header, and an answer with a `/` line, an `@` and CRLF line ends. |

| Q1: `c3heap` reads before the first v2 cycle, so it cannot see the RAM of the new keys | — | — | Intended. `c3heap` measures the cost of the new code after the boot; the RAM of the new keys grows over 24 hours. The floor of .170, the board with the least headroom, guards it at every reading, and the 24-hour reading of .189 goes into the record (the C3 had a `min_free_heap` of 28,376 B). |

## 2. Independent audit: GPT Astra, and four comparison audits

### 2.1 The audit prompt (2026-11-24)

The prompt `steps/R6/r6-astra-audit-prompt.md` of the planning repository at `78c83bbcf71ffb0a9a6fedc14f90a84724a1e1ed`, verbatim. The operator sent each auditor the one-line message of the planning repository's notes, which names this file and the two commits; for Gemini 4 Neon the operator wrote Neon in place of Astra.

```text
# R6 (v7.7.1.3) bundle audit — prompt for GPT Astra

_From `Docs/templates/prompt-audit-template.md` (the audit prompt body), filled by the planning session on 2026-11-24 for the independent audit of a MEDIUM bundle (PLAN-037, PLAN-041, PLAN-044). The operator sends GPT Astra, in its own ChatGPT session, one message that names this file and a commit of the planning repository (README, "Read first"). GPT Astra reads every input from GitHub itself and returns its report as one Markdown file, which the operator attaches to the next planning session._

**Risk classification:** MEDIUM — the first runtime writes of the per-device persistence engine on three boards, set by PLAN-044 (plan of record, R5b block, the table of Batch 2). One independent auditor; no reconciliation file.

---

You are an independent auditor. A separate producer session (the planning session) emitted a prompt bundle, R6 = v7.7.1.3, "per-device persist write path, dual-write", for Phase 7 of the GitHub repository `GCV-Sleeper-Service/ESP32-GW-multi-sensor` (public). The operator intends to dispatch its agent prompt to a coding agent (OpenAI Codex) on the basis of your audit. The bundle is not a pull request yet: the coding agent's PR will commit it.

**Your job:** confirm zero remaining HIGH or CRITICAL defects. If you find one, the bundle cannot be dispatched until it is fixed.

## §1 Inputs to read (in order)

Read every input yourself from GitHub; the operator attaches nothing. You have read access to the private planning repository `GCV-Sleeper-Service/Planning-Workflow`. Read it at the commit that the operator's message names; if your tools can read only `main`, read `main` and give in your report the commit you read. In the planning repository read only the paths below. Its other folders (`sessions/`, the other steps) and, in `steps/R6/`, the folder `ref/`, the internal audit reports, the logs and the producer's tools are the producer's working notes and reference code: reading them would break the independence of this audit.

In `steps/R6/` of the planning repository:

1. The bundle, unpacked in `bundle/`. The tar `r6-2026-11-24.tar` beside it holds the same files, and the setup file unpacks it for the agent; you need not read the tar. `bundle/prompts/phase7/v7.7.1.3-agent-prompt-gpt-codex.md` is the agent prompt (in the tar: `v7.7.1.3-agent-prompt-gpt-codex.md`); `bundle/prompts/phase7/v7.7.1.3-checks.sh` is its check file, every gate and checkpoint as a block (in the tar: `v7.7.1.3-checks.sh`); under `bundle/files/` (in the tar: `files/`) are the eleven supplied files that Task Group 0 of the prompt copies: `prompts/phase7/consolidated-audit-template-phase7.md`, `prompts/phase7/v7.7.1.3-review.md`, `prompts/phase7/v7.7.1.3-audit.md`, `Docs/operator/R6-persist-write-path.md`, `prompts/handoff/phase7/session-handoff-v7.7.1.3.md`, `prompts/handoff/phase7/phase7-batch2-assumption-audit.md`, `Docs/development-process-guide.md`, `prompts/producer-contract.md`, `Docs/templates/prompt-audit-template.md`, `Docs/decisions/decision-log.md` (new row PLAN-046), `Docs/plan-of-record.md`.

If your tools can compute SHA-256, check each file of `bundle/` against it, with the paths mapped as in item 1, and report the result; if they cannot, write `unable to verify` for this check.
3. `astra/readings.txt` — the operator's board readings of 2026-11-24 that the bundle uses, before and after the USB flash of .173.
4. Prior audit of this bundle: the two internal rounds of the planning session, in `bundle/files/prompts/phase7/v7.7.1.3-audit.md` §1. Read them only AFTER you have completed §2 below.

Live sources of truth (read them on GitHub at `main`, commit `a33a5cc`; do not trust the bundle's quotes of them):
- `firmware/core/nvs-persistence.h` (the old engine, `persist_hourly_segment()`, `maybe_yield_nvs_scan_()`, `export_latest_entries_()`, the keys of v7.7.1.2), `firmware/core/data-model.h` (`SensorEntity`, `MetricState`, the Phase 7 model, the generated `PERSIST_*` constants), `firmware/core/web-handler.h` (`handle_storage_stats_()`), `firmware/esp32-c3-multi-sensor.yaml` (the hourly lambda), `scripts/render_sensor_config.py` (`generate_board_yaml()`), `scripts/provision.sh`, `scripts/external-review.py` and `Docs/templates/external-review-template.md` (review B), `config/` (the sensor files).
- `CURRENT-STATE.md` (Board Fleet), `AGENTS.md`, `Docs/plan-of-record.md`, `Docs/decisions/decision-log.md`, `Docs/development-process-guide.md`, `prompts/producer-contract.md`, `prompts/prompt-index-and-workflow.md` (Critical Rules 11, 40, 61, 62 to 64), `Docs/v7.7-v7.8-persistence-architecture.md` §5 to §7 and §10.
- Vendor: ESP-IDF v5.5 "Non-Volatile Storage Library" (https://docs.espressif.com/projects/esp-idf/en/v5.5/esp32/api-reference/storage/nvs_flash.html); Google AI Studio terms (https://aistudio.google.com/docs/terms).

Decisions of the operator (2026-11-24), recorded in PLAN-046: they are decisions, not findings — `body <PR#> final` by the agent; review B in Google AI Studio with Gemini 4 Neon (part B of the review file and the block `reviewb`), the runner as fallback, the comment posted by the operator's own app and token (the planning session's design adds: the review itself runs in a plain AI Studio chat without tools); PLAN-046 in R6's own PR; `c3heap` against the board's own reading before the upload; the WROOM floor of 1,024 B; the login through `BASH_ENV`; R4's actual sessions; a ring of 24 slots, a guard of 252 free entries, the full 228-byte `DeviceSegment`, .173 flashed over USB before dispatch and tested first, the new MAC of `outside`. A finding that a decision is unsafe is welcome; label it so.

Pending by design, not findings: the PR number (`<NN>`, `<PR#>`); the records that the agent writes; the review rounds; the gate record and the dispatch paragraph (written after this audit); the operator's `fleet` output on the day of dispatch (.173 was flashed over USB on 2026-11-24: `readings.txt` and the operator page, "Before you start"); the placeholders `@EXTERNAL@` of the audit file (your report and its dispositions go there).

## §2 Required checks

### §2.A — Doctrinal compliance

| Check | Pass condition |
|---|---|
| A1 | The prompt follows `prompts/producer-contract.md` §7 (skeleton, §2 one block, §3 every path with HARD and SOFT marks, §6 task groups with checkpoints, §7 executable criteria, §8 the PR body with the Round Summary rows Inline, Review A, Review C, Review B, `Fixes #215`) |
| A2 | Every gate and checkpoint of the prompt is a block of the check file, and every assertion of a block comes from a sentence of the prompt (contract 8.1) |
| A3 | The MEDIUM style: interfaces and the tests that encode them are fixed; no finished code is prescribed beyond the supplied texts S2, T3 and H1 (contract 5.2) |
| A4 | The in-PR deliverables of process guide §2.5: `CURRENT-STATE.md`, the plan of record, the changelog, the session log, the record with its metrics |
| A5 | Device tests name every board of the plan of record's R6 block, the test board first; `timeout 300 esphome upload`, `esphome clean`; no `esphome run`; no login literal |
| A6 | The texts of PLAN-046 say the same thing in every place (contract 1.4): process guide §2.2 item 14 and §2.4, contract 7.13 and 8.3, the audit template, the plan of record (§5 R6, §6, §7), the review file, the operator page, the setup file |

### §2.B — Cross-cutting integrity

| Check | Pass condition |
|---|---|
| C1 — Rule 61 | The device loop calls `maybe_yield_nvs_scan_()`; no other `vTaskDelay` in the new code |
| C2 — board IPs | Only .173, .189 and .170 are read or flashed; addresses match `CURRENT-STATE.md` |
| C3 — YAML names | `firmware/esp32-c3-multi-sensor.yaml`, `firmware/esp32-wroom-32d-gw.yaml` (from `provision.sh wroom`), `firmware/esp32-s3-supermini-4m-gw.yaml` (from the .173 gateway file and the renderer) are what the scripts write |
| C4 — pipeline order | `--write` before `--check`; the pipeline of `AGENTS.md` in its order |
| C5 — `esphome run` | none |
| C6 — anchors | every anchor is quoted text or a symbol; the §2 block checks each |
| C7 — self-containedness | no scope, value or constraint taken from another prompt |
| C8 — evidence consistency | §6, §7, §8, the review file's checks S1 to S9 and the operator page ask for the same evidence |
| C9 — lint | `bash scripts/lint-prompts.sh` on the prompt exits 0 (only L6 warnings) |
| C10 — NVS budget | the ring of 24 slots and the guard keep the old engine's hourly overwrite possible on a partition with 5,324 free entries (`readings.txt`); check the entry arithmetic against the ESP-IDF page |
| C11 — RAM | nothing in the write path holds heap between cycles; the static status array is the only new RAM; the floor of 1,024 B is checked at every reading of .170 |
| C12 — Rule 40 | `handle_storage_stats_()` gains no NVS call |
| C13 — v7.7.1.4 readability | the key format, the meta fields, the segment layout and the row order let a restore read the blobs; a power loss between the segment and its meta leaves no blob counted as valid that is not |
| C14 — review B data | the block `reviewb` sends only public repository data and refuses login, key, password and token literals, and after a refusal no review B runs on that data anywhere; the runner's code comes from `origin/main`; the system text goes into the chat's system instructions; the model that reads the PR data has no tool that writes to GitHub; `reviewb <PR#> answer` cleans the answer as the runner cleans its comment; the bundle's own texts, the audit file included, pass the runner's screen and the key screen (R6's diff is review B's input); the operator page keeps the private planning repository, the fleet file and logins out of AI Studio |

### §2.C — Adversarial inspection

Write out what you looked for and what you found, for each: (1) compliant-but-wrong — mentally compile the eight interfaces against the live structs and the declaration order (S2 after `persist_hourly_segment()`, the functions after S2, T3 last; the handler in `web-handler.h` uses names that the assembled header declares before it); (2) evidence-theater — does `v2 PASS` prove dual-write on a board, does H1 prove the contract; (3) scope leakage — the 46 paths of §3 against what Task Groups 0 to 7 change; (4) deferred ambiguity — the operator's USB flash of .173 against the agent's `v2 ... before`, the persist minute against the waiting of `v2` (including the WAIT after a first cycle without a new point), a review-round fix to the firmware after the flashes (Task Group 6 again on boards that run v7.7.1.3: `dev <IP> before`, `v2 <IP> before`, `c3heap` against the new reading, a new `boot` epoch for `wroom24`), review B after the 24-hour readings; (5) non-idempotent steps — the blocks `insert`, `copy`, `scope` and `dev` run twice; (6) doctrinal drift.

## §3 Output format

One Markdown report titled `R6 v7.7.1.3 audit — GPT Astra — <date>`: Verdict (PASS / CONDITIONAL PASS / FAIL); audit lane self-attribution; summary table of §2.A, §2.B, §2.C; CRITICAL, HIGH, MEDIUM / LOW findings (file, quoted text, why, concrete fix); a disposition recommendation per finding; a confidence statement ((a) each HIGH finding's reality, (b) the chance of a missed HIGH, (c) any check you could not run); a short check of the internal rounds' findings after your own §2; and the commit of the planning repository that you read, with the result of the SHA-256 check of §1 item 2. Return the report as one Markdown file named `r6-astra-audit-<YYYY-MM-DD>.md` for the operator to download. If you cannot create a file, answer with the report alone, as Markdown, with nothing before or after it. The operator attaches it unchanged to the next planning session, which puts it into §2 of the audit file.

## §4 Constraints (binding)

1. No invented file contents, signatures or line numbers; a check you cannot run is `unable to verify`, not PASS.
2. No new functionality.
3. Quote verbatim when you claim a defect.
4. If a verdict depends on a check you could not run, say so.
5. Read the internal round (audit file §1) only after your own §2.
6. Never write a login, key or token value, nor a placeholder in a login's form (such as a user and a password joined by a colon after `curl -u`): name the file and the line. The report goes into R6's diff, and review B refuses such lines.
7. Read and write nothing outside the paths of §1 and the live sources; change nothing on GitHub.

## §5 Out of scope

The implementation that the agent will write; the operator's infrastructure; lint-rule additions (note their absence, do not block on them); the steps after R6.
```

The planning session read every report against the bundle at `78c83bb` of the planning repository and the product at `a33a5cc`, after its own reading of the bundle. The auditor of record is GPT Astra in the Cowork session (PLAN-047); the other four are comparison reports, as for R5r (PLAN-037).

| A2 (`r6-astra-audit-2026-11-24-codex.md`) | GPT Astra 6.2, Codex session; stopped on the 5-hour limit and resumed | "The harness identifies me as Codex, GPT-6" | not stated |
| A3 (`r6-astra-audit-2026-11-24-codex-1.md`) | GPT Astra 6.2, Codex; the operator describes a new session after the limit reset | "This is a verification of that audit in the same session"; it keeps the identifiers of A2, downgrades H3 and withdraws L2. The planning session counts it as a revision of A2, not as an independent run | not stated |

Gemini 4 Carbon, effort High, ran the same prompt for more than 50 minutes, used the rest of the 5-hour quota and more than 25 % of the weekly quota, and stopped without a report (operator). A second run after the quota reset gave report C (`r6-carbon-audit-2026-11-25.md`, titled "GPT Astra" from the prompt): it read the same commit `78c83bb` as the five others, as its message said, so it audited the bundle before the fixes of 2.3. No report states its context window, its tokens or whether its context was compacted; from now on every report does (PLAN-047, decision 2). The Gemini environment held the private planning repository; from now on a Gemini session gets public data only (PLAN-047, decision 4).

One row per distinct defect. "Reports" names the report and its identifier. "Holds" is the planning session's check against the sources. "Rating" is PLAN-037's scale as the planning session rates it; where an auditor rated higher, the operator decides (template §7 step 9), and every such finding below is fixed, so none stays open.

| D1 | `load_device_meta_` returned false for a read error as for an absent key, so a read error of an existing meta started a fresh ring: slot 0 overwritten, the other 23 slots out of the committed meta | A1 H01 (HIGH), A2 and A3 K1 (CRITICAL) | Yes (prompt 2.2, steps 3 and 4) | HIGH: the ring keeps metrics that the old engine does not (for example `wan_ping`), so a reset would lose stored samples, though nothing reads them before v7.7.1.4 (corrected after the re-audit, turn 2); the auditors rated it CRITICAL | Fixed: `load_device_meta_` returns 1, 0 or -1; -1 is ERROR and writes nothing; H1 case on a full ring; two negative tests; handoff line |
| D2 | `g_dev_persist_status` written by the persist task and read by the httpd task without synchronization, a C++ data race that the review file accepted | A1 H02, A2 and A3 H4 (HIGH) | Yes (S2; review S2) | MEDIUM: a status display of aligned words; a mixed reading could mislead the v2 gate | Fixed: S2 copies one device's status under a `portMUX` critical section (`dev_persist_status_get_`, `dev_persist_status_put_`); B and D check that nothing else names the array; compiled on C3, S3 and WROOM |
