Opinion of the planning session, from one run each (weak evidence): GPT Astra stays the first auditor; its Cowork run was the most complete and the best calibrated. Gemini 4 Neon found real defects that three Astra runs missed and none that are false, at a small part of a 5-hour quota, but it missed every HIGH contract and gate defect: a useful second auditor from another model family, never the only one. Muse produced two false findings and much noise: review C only. Gemini 4 Carbon (report C, 2026-11-25): the fewest real defects and no HIGH, at the highest cost: it audits no bundle for now. The operator recorded this as PLAN-047, decision 1, on 2026-11-25.

### 2.5 The reports, verbatim

Each report as the operator received it; only its headings are four levels lower, so that they sit under this section. Report C came on 2026-11-25, after the dispositions of 2.3 were written; 2.3 and 2.4 add it.

#### Report A1: GPT Astra 6.2, ChatGPT Cowork session (auditor of record)

#### R6 v7.7.1.3 audit — GPT Astra — 2026-11-24

**Verdict: FAIL. Hold dispatch.** Six HIGH findings remain. A metadata read error can reset a device’s valid history. The status display has a C++ data race. The board gates can pass below the WROOM heap floor, reuse evidence from an earlier boot, or report dual-write using stale measurements. The final PR check accepts skipped required jobs, and the Review B screen misses ordinary password literals. These are defects in the instructions and checks supplied to the coding agent. No device failure or credential disclosure was observed.

**Audit lane.** Independent external MEDIUM producer audit, GPT Astra lane. Harness attribution is OpenAI Codex, GPT-6. I performed the checks in this session and inspected the producer’s two internal reports after completing my own §2 assessment.

| Source | Revision and verification |
|---|---|
| Planning repository | `GCV-Sleeper-Service/Planning-Workflow` at `78c83bbcf71ffb0a9a6fedc14f90a84724a1e1ed`. The Git tree under `steps/R6/bundle/` contains exactly 13 files. I computed SHA-256 over the downloaded Git blob bytes. **13/13 match** step 0 of `r6-setup.txt`, including the audit file. I mapped the two top-level tar names to `bundle/prompts/phase7/` and the eleven other paths to `bundle/files/`. The S2, T3 and H1 fenced texts also match their three embedded hashes. This verifies the unpacked bundle. The tar itself was optional and was not inspected. [Audit instructions][instructions] · [Setup manifest][setup] |
| Product repository | `GCV-Sleeper-Service/ESP32-GW-multi-sensor` at `a33a5cc9a78214cb214cdb228df71e138edc993d`. GitHub reports the repository is public. I used this revision for the firmware, configs, scripts and doctrine. [Product snapshot][product] |
| Operator and vendor evidence | The operator’s November 24 readings distinguish .173 before and after the USB flash. Afterward it reported v7.7.1.2, 524,288 history bytes and available NVS statistics. The earlier zero figures describe a different installation. I checked the NVS arithmetic and durability guarantees against ESP-IDF v5.5, and the AI Studio data-use statements against Google’s terms. [Board readings][readings] · [ESP-IDF][idf] · [Google terms][google] |

##### Required checks

PASS below means the specified property was verified within the stated scope. A fixture result describes the actual check logic with substituted inputs. It is not a measurement from an ESP32.

###### §2.A — Doctrinal compliance

| Check | Result | Evidence |
|---|---|---|
| A1. Contract §7 | PARTIAL | The preamble, §§1–9, single §2 invocation, enumerated scope, HARD/SOFT marks, named device checks, PR sections, four ordered round rows and `Fixes #215` are present. The gate does not prove every anchor. Acceptance criteria can disappear from the final body without failing its check. H05, M03. |
| A2. Gates trace to the prompt | FAIL | The blocks for §2, scope, copy/0, A–F, host/H, device and image measurements, PRE-PR, inline, body and Review B exist. Some predicates add requirements absent from the prompt, and others enforce less than the prompt says. H03–H05, M03. |
| A3. MEDIUM prescription | PASS | S2 fixes status types and constants. T3 and H1 fix tests. The eight function bodies and the handler implementation remain the agent’s work. The two one-line call-site insertions specify where the interface is invoked. |
| A4. In-PR deliverables | PASS for inclusion | Task Group 7 calls for current state, the ladder row and next step, changelog, session log, consolidated record, the four reporting categories and five process metrics. The supplied record’s device labels need correction. M04. |
| A5. Board procedure | PASS for the executable procedure | Only .173, .189 and .170 are OTA targets. .173 must pass first. Each upload has `timeout 300`, the builds use `esphome clean`, and the board-reading commands obtain credentials from the environment. The record template has a conflicting example. M04. |
| A6. PLAN-046 consistency | PARTIAL | The decision row, process guide §2.2 item 14 and §2.4, contract 7.13/8.3, audit template, plan §§5–7, setup and review instructions agree on agent-run final verification and the R6 Review B lane. The plan records R4 as three planning sessions and one operator session. The heap rule and full-ring evidence are not enforced as written. The setup and agent prompt order PR readiness differently. H03, H04, L01. |

###### §2.B — Cross-cutting integrity

| Check | Result | Evidence |
|---|---|---|
| C1. Rule 61 | PASS for specification | §2.2 requires one call to `maybe_yield_nvs_scan_(i)` per device and forbids another delay. The live helper yields at positive multiples of two. Check B verifies presence, while H1 replaces the helper with a no-op. Neither proves the per-device call sequence in a future implementation. |
| C2. Board IPs | PARTIAL | The allowlists and upload commands match the live fleet. The generic record names .191, a production aggregator outside R6. M04. |
| C3. YAML names | PASS | The C3 default goes to `firmware/esp32-c3-multi-sensor.yaml`. The WROOM and S3 gateway configs specify board IDs `esp32-wroom-32d` and `esp32-s3-supermini-4m`. The renderer’s non-C3 path produces `firmware/esp32-wroom-32d-gw.yaml` and `firmware/esp32-s3-supermini-4m-gw.yaml`. [Renderer][renderer] · [Config files][config] |
| C4. Pipeline order | PASS for instructions | Task Groups 1–4 put the applicable writes before checks. Task Group 5 invokes the AGENTS pipeline through header generation. PRE-PR subsequently runs preflight. The pinned linter’s three proximity warnings do not identify a reversed command in these sequences. [Product instructions][agents] |
| C5. Prohibited run command | PASS | `esphome run` occurs in prohibitions. No R6 deployment command invokes it. |
| C6. Anchors | FAIL | Core fragment and lambda anchors are checked, several by whole-file comparison. The current-state editing anchors are not all verified before work, and the later gate uses absolute line positions. M03. |
| C7. Self-containedness | PASS | The eight interfaces, fixed texts, targets, thresholds, paths and evidence instructions are in R6. Prior prompts are identified as historical evidence. |
| C8. Evidence consistency | FAIL | The gate accepts board and review evidence weaker than §§6–8, the review checklist and operator page promise. H03–H05, M04. |
| C9. Lint | PASS, executed | The pinned `bash scripts/lint-prompts.sh prompts/phase7/v7.7.1.3-agent-prompt-gpt-codex.md` exited 0 with only L6 warnings, on lines 159, 280 and 656. The check file also passed `bash -n`. |
| C10. NVS budget | PASS on the supplied readings | A 228-byte segment needs 10 entries and a 60-byte meta needs 4. Four full rings therefore cost nominally `4 × (24 × 10 + 4) = 976` entries. From 5,324 free, 4,348 remain, 4,096 above the guard. A 232-byte legacy segment likewise takes 10. Updating an existing key releases its previous entries. ESP-IDF includes reserved entries in `free_entries`, so 252 is a pre-write threshold, not 252 guaranteed writable entries after each operation. This is capacity arithmetic, not a guarantee against flash errors. [NVS blob accounting and statistics][idf] |
| C11. RAM and WROOM floor | FAIL | The contract releases the one heap segment and handle on all paths. The four status records are the only prescribed persistent application array. ESP-IDF also retains heap for its index as keys accumulate. The producer’s 1.0–1.5 KB estimate is not a board measurement. Two actual pre-upload gate paths omit the 1,024 B floor. H02, H03. |
| C12. Rule 40 | PASS for the change | The new status object uses RAM. The pinned handler already opens the history namespace, loads old metadata and calls `nvs_get_stats`. Check D compares the new NVS call count to that baseline. Review S2’s wording “no NVS call in an HTTP handler” overstates the requirement. The R6 condition is that the change adds no NVS call. [Live handler][handler] |
| C13. Restore readability and power loss | PARTIAL | Both key formats fit the 15-character NVS limit. The pinned structs give a 60-byte meta, 24-byte header and 228-byte segment. Row order is reproducible with the same manifest. Operational read errors can discard the ring index, and H1 does not establish crash durability for two separate keys. H01, M01. |
| C14. Review B | FAIL | Preparation reads the public product PR, takes the runner module from `origin/main`, and uses its wrapping, filtering and cleaning functions. The instructions assign `system.txt` to system instructions, use a tool-free review chat, and stop every lane after a refusal. I ran the actual login and additional key screens over all 13 supplied texts, including the audit file, with zero matches. The screens still miss literals, and output and snapshot checks differ from the runner. H06, M02. |

###### §2.C — Adversarial inspection

| Inspection | Result | What I checked and found |
|---|---|---|
| 1. Compliant-but-wrong | FAIL | I traced both ring helpers, ID lookup, segment builder, meta load/save, device write and cycle driver against the live structs. `SensorEntity::metric_states` contains `MetricState::history`. The exporter copies the latest samples oldest first. The live model precedes persistence, which precedes the handler. S2 follows the old writer, the eight definitions follow S2, and T3 ends the file. I ran the H1 type extractor and a GNU C++20 syntax check with the eight declarations. A compiling implementation can still satisfy the reset-on-read-error contract and race the HTTP reader. H01, H02. |
| 2. Evidence-theater | FAIL | H1 covers row selection, wrap, dedup, collision, the space guard and selected write failures. Its store changes immediately and commit always succeeds. Board counters and total partition usage corroborate activity but do not identify an individual flash blob or prove two engines advanced in the same recent cycle. The final PR gate also has a false PASS. H04, H05, M01. |
| 3. Scope leakage | PARTIAL | I reconciled the paths for setup, the eleven copies, hand edits, version/pipeline outputs, session log and record. Overlaps such as the plan of record count once. The actual scope block produced 46 distinct paths twice. The five listed sensor configs each contain the old outside MAC once. The template’s .191 reference creates a scope conflict in the evidence instructions. I did not regenerate the complete future implementation diff. M04. |
| 4. Deferred ambiguity | FAIL | The .173 USB prerequisite is supported by the post-flash readings and rechecked before OTA. Minute 10, quarter-hour samples, and a WAIT for a first cycle with no new point are consistent. All-pending can wait indefinitely. Both baseline blocks accept v7.7.1.3 for a repeat and the C3 compares with its new pre-upload reading. The WROOM can reuse its old boot. The reviewer file puts B after A/C and the 24-hour readings, but a firmware fix from B invalidates that evidence. H03, H04, M04. |
| 5. Repeated operations | PARTIAL | In a disposable local fixture, `scope` produced the same 46 paths twice, each `insert` succeeded twice without duplicating its block, and `copy` twice followed by `copy verify` passed. The copy fixture used the supplied bytes for the eight replacements and exercised initial creation of the three new files. Repeating `dev before` deliberately overwrites evidence. Without a boot transition on .173/.170, repeated success does not identify a new deployment. H03. |
| 6. Doctrinal drift | FAIL | PLAN-046’s thresholds, tool-free review lane and ownership of final verification are identifiable. Several executable blocks depart from those rules, and two reused templates retain instructions from earlier steps. H03–H06, M03, M04, L01. |

##### CRITICAL findings

None identified. The faults below are sufficient to withhold dispatch. The active v7.7.1.2 restore path still reads the legacy engine.

##### HIGH findings

###### H01. A metadata read failure authorizes overwriting a previously valid ring

**Evidence.** Agent prompt §2.2 requires “a fresh meta when `load_device_meta_` gives false” and specifies “otherwise it returns false and leaves `*meta` unchanged.” The Boolean also covers an operational failure of the read. The next successful write can replace slot 0 and publish `valid_segments = 1` for a device whose ring previously held 24 valid segments. The remaining slots still exist but are no longer reachable through that meta. The same failure prevents verification of ownership before writing. H1’s fake read models a missing key and length errors, but has no injected storage read failure. [Agent prompt, lines 243–254][pmeta] · [ESP-IDF NVS API][idf]

**Disposition: fix before dispatch.** Distinguish a valid meta, confirmed absence, a deliberately resettable incompatible format, and an operational read error. On an operational error, report ERROR for that device and perform zero writes for it. Validate the cursor/count invariants before trusting otherwise compatible metadata. Add a populated-ring H1 case that fails one read, compares the complete meta and slot data before and after, and confirms later devices still run.

###### H02. The published per-device status has a C++ data race

**Evidence.** Review S2 explicitly says “The status array is written by the persist path and read by the handler without a lock”. S2 declares `static DevicePersistStatus g_dev_persist_status[PERSIST_DEVICE_ARRAY_LEN] = {};` with ordinary mutable fields. The hourly task writes them while the HTTP task can read them. Concurrent conflicting ordinary C++ accesses have undefined behavior. Tolerating mixed observations does not make the accesses safe. H1 runs serially. [Review S2][rstatus] · [Supplied status layout][status]

**Disposition: fix before dispatch.** Specify safe publication in S2 and §4.1. For example, publish and copy one small status record under the same ESP32 task-safe synchronization mechanism, then release it before formatting or sending the response. Preserve the WROOM memory budget and keep NVS and network I/O outside the protected section. Update the resource check for the chosen mechanism.

###### H03. The WROOM pre-upload gates accept low heap, and a repeat can reuse the previous boot

**Evidence.** The acceptance criterion says “at least 1,024 B in every reading of the step”. Yet `b_dev` applies the floor only when `"$want" = "v7.7.1.3"`, and `b_v2` returns from its `before` branch ahead of the floor check. With a 900 B minimum, the real blocks returned `dev PASS` for pre-upload readings on both allowed versions, and `v2 PASS (before)` on v7.7.1.3. The ordinary post-upload floor check correctly failed at 900 B. In a separate same-version repeat with adequate heap, identical pre/post boot epochs and a 90,000-second uptime yielded `dev PASS`, `dev PASS`, then `wroom24 PASS`. The latter can therefore certify 24 hours that elapsed before the intended reflash. Only the C3 path requires a different boot. [Board checks][dev] · [24-hour block][w24] · [Acceptance criteria][accept]

**Disposition: fix before dispatch.** Check every .170 status reading immediately, before saving a baseline or returning PASS/WAIT. Tie post-OTA evidence for each board to a boot later than its saved pre-upload observation, allowing documented clock tolerance. Start the WROOM 24-hour window only from that verified post-OTA boot. Save both versions, boot epochs and the upload/build evidence for each attempt.

###### H04. Yesterday’s activity can satisfy today’s dual-write check

**Evidence.** Task 6.4 says “the old engine wrote in the same cycle”. The block compares `last > last0` with the **pre-upload** epoch, and accepts devices with `writes_ok` of one since boot. At 24 hours it reuses the same baseline. When the rings are full, the required growth in used entries is zero. In a 25-hour fixture representing full rings on a repeat deployment, four devices with one successful write each and later dedup results, unchanged used entries, and an old-engine epoch that had advanced once after baseline produced `v2 PASS`. Those values can be unchanged since the first check. The predicate also permits a fourth device with an unrecognized result if the other three qualify, and only prints several JSON fields that §4.1 makes mandatory. In a second 25-hour fixture, all four devices still pending produced `v2 WAIT`; that branch has no deadline. [Task 6.4][boards] · [Device evidence predicates][v2]

**Disposition: fix before dispatch.** Save the first successful post-OTA observation for each boot. At the later check, use a fresh expected persist interval and compare the old epoch, per-device epochs/counters, and ring position before and after it. Permit dedup/empty under the stated no-new-data rule, but report the observation as inconclusive when it cannot establish the required progress. Bound WAIT by scheduled opportunities, including the first no-point cycle. Validate the required fields, ranges and result enum for every device. Keep the full-ring overwrite exception and state it in §6.4, where the text currently requires growth “for each such device”. This uses the existing status fields.

###### H05. The final merge check accepts required jobs that never ran and reviews that never posted

**Evidence.** The final checker accepts `("success", "skipped", "neutral")`. It rejects unticked criteria it finds, but requires no minimum set of criterion rows. The final round-row check accepts a filled row naming a commit even when the corresponding external comment is absent. I exercised the actual final block with all ten named jobs present but skipped, no external review comments, no acceptance boxes, and an omitted closing line. It printed `body final PASS at <commit>`. The block verified the head while leaving the required checks and reviews uncertified. A separate GitHub job or manual check may catch individual omissions. [Final-body block][bodycheck] · [Mandatory criteria and PR skeleton][accept]

**Disposition: fix before dispatch.** Require success for the specifically mandatory jobs and seven expected browser variants. Verify actual A/C/B review records and the commits they identify. Check the complete required criterion set, its final state, and the closing issue line. Keep the current head comparison. Add independent negative fixtures for a skipped mandatory job, a missing review and a missing criterion.

###### H06. The Review B screen passes ordinary password literals and does not screen the answer before posting

**Evidence.** The block promises to refuse “a key, password or token literal”. I exercised the actual additional key expression and the pinned runner’s login detector together. Ordinary quoted password assignments with synthetic values of 8 and 12 characters passed both detectors. No value was sent or reproduced here. In the return path, the entire content check after the header is `comment = rn.clean(answer) + "\n"`. The runner’s `clean()` neutralizes mentions and command-like lines. The live runner separately screens model output before publishing. A correct header does not protect the public PR against sensitive text introduced in an answer. All 13 current bundle texts passed the input screens; this finding concerns the controls on the later PR, comments and answer. [Manual input and output paths][manual] · [Trusted runner][runner] · [AI Studio terms][google]

**Disposition: fix before dispatch.** Refuse non-reference literal assignments to known password or credential fields independently of a presumed minimum length. Use the same credential policy on the answer and on the final text to be posted, in addition to the existing cleaning. Cover normal project configuration forms and short literals with locally generated fixtures. Keep refusal messages limited to the source and line, and retain the existing no-fallback-on-refusal rule.

##### MEDIUM and LOW findings

| ID | File and quoted evidence | Consequence and recommended disposition |
|---|---|---|
| M01. Two-key crash durability | Agent prompt §2.2 says “after a written segment, and only then, the meta”. H1’s commit stub ends in `return ESP_OK;`. The handoff already acknowledges “a newer segment at `next_slot`, which the meta counts as the oldest”. [Contract and H1][pmeta] · [Handoff][handoff] · [ESP-IDF][idf] | The API guarantees durability after a successful commit. Set-call order and the fake store do not establish a two-key transaction. At full wrap the previous meta already counts the overwritten slot. Sorting valid headers addresses that specific ordering case. **Fix the proof and contract.** Establish segment durability before publishing its meta, specify how a failed commit is handled, and exercise partial-ring and full-ring restart states with staged writes and injected commit failure. Specify whether an intact newer slot may be recovered after a torn update. This can be tested without implementing R7/R8 restore. |
| M02. Manual review can attest to a different PR snapshot | `reviewb` records `head, base, base_ref` once, then reads the live PR diff, reviews and comments. Its answer check consults the saved `turn3.txt`. In contrast the pinned runner calls `confirm_position(pr_url, token, pr)` and checks its comment length. [Manual preparation][manual] · [Runner][runner] | A push during collection can combine the old header with a newer diff. A previously screened package also cannot vouch for newly added comments fetched by a later fallback. **Fix before relying on Review B.** Recheck head/base around collection and before accepting/posting the answer. Invalidate prior output after a new failed attempt. Apply the credential check to the exact fallback inputs, enforce the runner’s posting-size limit, and say explicitly on the operator page that only the four generated materials belong in AI Studio. Keep private planning files, the fleet file and login material out. |
| M03. Checks impose textual requirements and miss editing anchors | Check E requires `$C3YAML does not name v7.7.1.3 twice` as its failure condition and an image newer than the assembled header. Check F uses `sed -n 3p "$CS"` and `sed -n 4p "$CS"`. The prompt says “Every anchor in this prompt is quoted text or a symbol, never a line number”. [Gate definitions][checks] · [Agent prompt][prompt] | Task 5 describes a version bump, a successful compile and a fitting image. It supplies neither an exact YAML occurrence count nor a timestamp rule. §2 skips the current-state headings/rows required by 7.1. A harmless movement of a heading can stop a late checkpoint. **Align prompt and checks.** Check named version fields, describe the freshness requirement, and verify each later editing anchor in §2 using its text. Make the yield sequence observable in H1 if the checkpoint is to certify one call per device. |
| M04. The evidence template and repeat-review instructions can mislabel measurements | The consolidated template specifies `S3 (.191)` and `Peak heap during history serve`. Task 7.1 fixes the column title at `v7.7.1.2 (before the upload)`. The review file says B is “After reviews A and C are assessed and the 24-hour readings are in”. [Record template][record] · [Agent prompt][prompt] · [Review file][review] | .173 is the only S3 under test, the blocks do not produce all generic metrics, and a repeat starts on v7.7.1.3. If B itself requests a firmware fix, its existing review also predates the replacement 24-hour observations. **Correct the R6 evidence instructions.** Use .173 and the actual pre-upload version, mark unavailable generic metrics N/A, preserve the initial and repeat records, and send B the replacement observations and changed firmware before final sign-off when its fix invalidates them. |
| M05. A permitted helper has a partition-wide erase path | Task §5 says “do NOT erase a key”. Task §2.2 mandates `open_history_nvs_(&handle, NVS_READWRITE)`. The live helper calls `ensure_history_nvs_ready_()`, which can call `nvs_flash_erase_partition(HISTORY_PARTITION_LABEL)` on two initialization errors. [Live helper][nvopen] · [Agent prompt][prompt] | This is inherited recovery behavior. R6 adds an invocation, including when the old writer returned early for lack of data. The ban on erase spellings in *new* text and the H1 open stub do not ensure preservation of the old partition. **Clarify the boundary before dispatch.** Make the v2 open fail without destructive recovery, leaving legacy recovery ownership unchanged, or obtain an explicit disposition accepting that existing side effect. Exercise an initialization failure in the host fixture. |
| L01. Two instructions still disagree on PR readiness and record location | Setup steps 7–8 put `gh pr ready` before “Write the PR body”. Review-round item 1 puts the PR-body update first and says “Then run” before its ready command. Contract 9.8 says “§8 of the record”, while the supplied template puts the metrics under “## §4 — Prompt Quality Score”. [Setup][setup] · [Producer contract][contract] · [Record template][record] | The agent can make the PR ready with a placeholder body, and the record has no numbered §8. **Correct at the next render.** Use one body-before-ready sequence in setup and prompt, and reference the metrics table by its actual heading. |

##### Check of the internal rounds

I inspected the two reports and disposition tables in §1 of the supplied audit file after the independent checks. Their descriptions of producer rehearsals are useful evidence of what those auditors ran. I did not count their compiles, mutation totals or simulated deployments as my own. [Internal rounds and dispositions][internal]

| Earlier findings | Independent disposition |
|---|---|
| Round 1 H1, M1–M4 | The first no-point cycle now waits, the final line names the head, the handoff discloses NVS index RAM, 4.1 requires split prints, and firmware fixes call for reflashing. I reproduced the first-cycle WAIT. The all-pending deadline, same-boot retest, late progress and final-job problems remain distinct. H03–H05. |
| Round 1 M5–M6, L1–L5, L8–L10 | Confirmed the eleventh copied file, Muse qualification, separate system instructions, tool-free chat, both C3 heap values, the revised H1 cases, full-ring recovery note and revised checkpoint wording. The admission of mixed status observations still leaves H02. H1’s failed meta **set** test does not simulate a failed **commit**. |
| Round 1 L6–L7 and questions | L6’s disagreement still exists. Mirroring a generic setup text leaves two literal orders for the agent; L01 is a small fix. Round 2 F3 correctly superseded the earlier instruction to use the runner after a refusal. The known baseline literal identified by the auditors is at `firmware/esp32-c3-multi-sensor.yaml`, line 158. No claim is made that the future PR diff contains it. The permissions of the operator’s posting app were not tested here. |
| Round 2 F1–F4 | Both versions are now accepted before upload, and full rings are exempt from net entry growth in the block. The more specific credential-name checks, no-fallback rule, and collision/failed-write cases are present. The shorter-literal, boot-provenance and stale-progress counterexamples still pass. H03, H04, H06. |
| Round 2 F5–F10, Q1–Q3 | Deferring the complete JSON check to .173 is reasonable for this bundle; host syntax alone cannot certify the payload. The manual lane loads runner code from `origin/main`, uses the runner’s cleaner, specifies four files, and documents the posting remedies. The audit template is v1.7, and the NVS RAM wording was corrected. Early C3 measurement, the app-permissions question and the WROOM WAIT below 86,400 s were accurately distinguished. Identical cleaner behavior does not establish identical credential screening or snapshot provenance. |

**Revision check.** The report itself passed both bundle screens as plain Markdown and as added diff lines. When this report and its dispositions are embedded in the supplied audit file, its hash changes. Regenerate the dependent check-file and setup hashes, then repeat the manifest check, affected gate fixtures and both screens on the exact dispatch revision.

##### Confidence and verification limits

| HIGH finding | Basis and remaining uncertainty |
|---|---|
| H01 | Direct consequence of the mandatory Boolean return and reset branch. Loss of reachability requires a failed read followed by a successful write. I did not induce a flash fault. |
| H02 | The supplied fields are ordinary C++ objects, and review S2 expressly prescribes unsynchronized cross-task access. Timing and observable effects on particular devices are unknown. |
| H03 | Reproduced the below-floor prechecks and the same-boot 24-hour PASS using the actual blocks with local status fixtures. No claim about the current heap or boot of a physical board. |
| H04 | Reproduced stale full-ring PASS and 25-hour all-pending WAIT. The output of an actual future board may be healthy; the predicates cannot distinguish the demonstrated cases. |
| H05 | Reproduced `body final PASS` with skipped jobs and missing review evidence. Any independent protections in the operator’s GitHub settings were outside this audit. |
| H06 | Ran both input detectors on benign generated fixtures. Verified the answer path calls the cleaner without the runner’s output screen. No secret was transmitted and no real review comment was posted. |

**Chance of a missed HIGH.** A further HIGH remains plausible, especially at the boundary between NVS failure recovery, task concurrency and actual board timing. Source inspection and fixtures gave direct evidence for the reported gate failures. They do not measure how often physical faults occur.

**Unable to verify end to end:** the build-host §2 gate, the future eight function bodies and linked H1/T3 execution, complete regeneration/preflight, the three production-toolchain builds, OTA and 24-hour measurements, live GitHub review/CI results, and the future AI Studio or runner exchange. These require an implementation, a dispatched PR, or the operator’s boards. The **FAIL verdict does not depend on any of those unavailable runs**.

#### Report A2: GPT Astra 6.2, Codex session

#### R6 v7.7.1.3 audit — GPT Astra — 2026-11-24

**Verdict — FAIL. Hold dispatch.** A metadata read error can reset an existing device’s history. The WROOM gates accept a below-floor reading before an upload, and the 24-hour gate accepts storage evidence that has been unchanged since the first hour. These findings apply to the pinned bundle. All 13 setup hashes match.

**Audit lane.** Independent MEDIUM producer audit, GPT Astra lane. The harness identifies me as Codex, GPT-6. I completed the independent checks before reading the producer’s two internal rounds. Findings below use PLAN-037 severity. None requests reversal of the operator’s decisions on ring capacity, free-entry guard, board order, heap floor or review venue.

##### CRITICAL finding

**K1 — A failed meta read can discard a healthy device ring.** Prompt 2.2, lines 243 and 249, says “otherwise it returns false and leaves `*meta` unchanged” and “a fresh meta when `load_device_meta_` gives false”. A temporary storage read error on a populated ring therefore takes the same path as an absent key. If subsequent writes succeed, slot 0 is replaced and the committed meta advertises one valid segment. On a full ring, one existing slot is overwritten and the other 23 fall outside the newly committed history. The old engine covers the environmental series and cannot recover every per-device metric. H1 exercises absent and incompatible meta, but its fake cannot inject this read failure.

**Disposition — fix before dispatch.** Distinguish a confirmed absent key, an explicitly supported format reset and a storage error. On a storage error, preserve both blobs, record ERROR and continue with the next device. Test a populated ring whose meta read fails once and whose subsequent storage operations would succeed. Verify that the ring survives the failure and resumes from the original cursor.

##### HIGH findings

**H1 — Both WROOM pre-upload reads bypass the floor.** Checks line 610 conditions the floor on `[ "$want" = "v7.7.1.3" ]`. In `b_v2`, lines 674–681, `echo "v2 PASS (before)"` and `return` precede the floor check. Prompt §7 requires “at least 1,024 B in every reading of the step”. With .170 already running v7.7.1.3 and a simulated minimum of 900 B, both pre-upload blocks printed PASS. The next reboot resets that since-boot minimum. **Disposition — fix before dispatch.** Check the floor immediately after parsing every .170 status response, before saving a baseline or returning PASS. Test 1,023 and 1,024 B for both versions and both pre-upload commands.

**H2 — The delayed gate can reuse the first hour’s work.** Checks lines 719 and 728 use `(d.get("writes_ok") or 0) >= 1` and `if not last > last0:`. Both refer to lifetime or pre-upload values. In an offline run, an identical storage response passed at uptimes 4,800 and 86,500 seconds. The matching `wroom24` boot and heap also passed. A writer that stops after its first good hour can pass all three checks. On a same-version retest .173 and .170 also lack the old-boot comparison that `c3heap` makes. Line 725 exempts full rings from growth, while Prompt 6.4 promises growth “by at least 10 for each such device”. **Disposition — fix before dispatch.** Save boot, per-device counters and epochs, and the old-engine epoch on the first post-upload PASS. At 24 hours require progress since that sample on the intended boot. For a full ring use advancing epochs/counters as overwrite evidence. Compare pre/post boot on same-version uploads, preserve the actual baseline version in the C3 table, and update §6.4 and S6 to match.

**H3 — On a full ring the destination is already counted as valid.** Prompt 2.2, lines 252–253, writes “with `slot` the meta's `next_slot`” and updates metadata “after a written segment, and only then”. With 24 valid slots, replacing the oldest blob and losing power before the meta update leaves old metadata pointing at a newer generation. Handoff line 59 acknowledges “a newer segment at `next_slot`, which the meta counts as the oldest”. Sorting by header epoch can recognize chronology, but cannot recover the displaced blob or establish that the replacement was published. NVS guarantees durability on commit and permits writes earlier. **Disposition — fix before dispatch.** Specify a crash-consistent handoff for a full ring. One option keeps the 24-slot limit by first committing metadata that excludes the outgoing slot, then committing its replacement, then publishing the updated meta. Exercise interruption at each boundary of first fill and wrap, including failed commit. Check which epochs the surviving meta can actually claim. [ESP-IDF commit semantics](https://docs.espressif.com/projects/esp-idf/en/v5.5/esp32/api-reference/storage/nvs_flash.html)

**H4 — The writer and HTTP reader access ordinary C++ fields without synchronization.** Review S2, lines 97–98, says “without a lock: a reading can mix two cycles”. S2 declares `static DevicePersistStatus g_dev_persist_status[PERSIST_DEVICE_ARRAY_LEN] = {};`. Concurrent conflicting accesses on the persist and httpd tasks are a C++ data race, even for naturally aligned fields. The JSON used for gate evidence can also combine a new result with an old epoch or count. **Disposition — fix before dispatch.** Define a synchronized publication and snapshot of one device’s status. Hold the synchronization only while copying/updating RAM. Format JSON and perform NVS calls outside it. Update S2, Review S2 and the RAM accounting together.

**H5 — The final gate accepts required jobs that never passed.** Checks line 1009 accepts `("success", "skipped", "neutral")` for every job. Prompt §7 says the named checks and seven browser jobs “pass on the final head”. The unmodified validation predicate returned success in offline fixtures where the compile and one browser job were skipped, and again when they were neutral. Lines 974–988 also use posted-review state only to reject a pending cell. A filled row alone can stand in for an absent external review. **Disposition — fix before dispatch.** Require success for each mandatory job on the final head and an actual posted A, C and B review for completed rows. Add negative cases for a skipped mandatory job and a completed row with no corresponding review.

**H6 — Review B’s screens do not cover all credential-bearing input or the outgoing comment.** Checks lines 1120–1123 impose literal-length and character restrictions, including `{16,}`. An ordinary short password in an explicitly named password field need not be refused. In answer mode line 1043 goes directly to `comment = rn.clean(answer) + "\n"`. The pinned runner separately calls `screen(f"turn {turn}", visible, answer=True)` before cleaning. Cleaning mentions and slash commands cannot establish that the answer is safe to publish. All current bundle texts passed the screens. **Disposition — fix before dispatch.** Apply a common fail-closed check to recognized credential fields regardless of value length, with explicit treatment of allowed references. Apply the runner’s answer screen and the key screen before writing a postable comment. Test refusal with generated fixtures and report only file/field locations.

**H7 — Manual review B can certify a different revision from the diff it received.** Checks line 1081 captures `pull["head"]["sha"]`, but line 1086 gets the diff via `"repos/%s/pulls/%s" % (repo, pr)`. The files, reviews and comments also come from the moving PR. No final position check binds them to the recorded head/base. The answer step accepts the saved header without checking the current PR. The pinned runner calls `confirm_position(pr_url, token, pr)` before model use and before posting. **Disposition — fix before dispatch.** Verify head/base after collecting inputs and again before releasing the comment. If either moved, discard the package and regenerate. Apply the same key screen to the actual input snapshot on a runner fallback, which otherwise fetches data again.

##### MEDIUM and LOW findings

| ID | File and verbatim evidence | Consequence and disposition |
|---|---|---|
| M1 | Checks lines 714–715, `if all(d.get("result") == "pending" for d in devs):` | This WAIT has no uptime limit. The block returned WAIT with every device still pending after 24 hours. A persist lambda that never runs can leave the operator waiting indefinitely. **Fix in this render.** Allow the first no-point cycle, then fail after the next eligible :10 cycle and a defined grace period. |
| M2 | Checks lines 762 and 764, `sed -n 3p "$CS"` and `sed -n 4p "$CS"`. Prompt §2 says “every anchor of Task Groups 1 to 4 and of item 7.2”. | F enforces line positions absent from 7.1. §2 neither checks the current-state headings/rows nor verifies the supplied “You are here” heading as an anchor. **Fix in this render.** Check the named headings/rows in §2 and select them by unique text in F. Derive count and spelling assertions from stated requirements. |
| M3 | Prompt 5.1, “ending with `bash scripts/generate-header.sh`”. Pinned `AGENTS.md`, lines 95–96, places `bash scripts/preflight.sh` immediately afterward. | The referenced pipeline ends at two different commands. PRE-PR eventually runs preflight, after the device uploads. **Fix in this render.** Give TG5 the complete pinned sequence, including when preflight must pass before flashing. |
| L1 | Live current state, “**Produce the v7.7.1.3 bundle, then R6**”. Supplied consolidated template, “S3 (.191)”. | Completing 7.1 literally leaves the separate “What's Next” list directing the next session back to R6. The record template names the production aggregator in the S3 measurement column. **Correct the instructions** to advance that list and label the R6 S3 column .173. |
| L2 | Operator line 129, “run the first block yourself”, and Prompt review item 7, “If a commit comes after it, end that round again and run it again.” | An operator following only the page could merge with the agent’s final comment on an earlier head. **Clarify** that the agent posts a new final result on the changed head and the operator confirms it. |

##### Internal-round cross-check

I read audit-file §1 after the independent inspection. Round 1’s first-cycle WAIT, final head in the output, revised NVS RAM estimate, split JSON printing and tool-free AI Studio chat are visible in this revision. Round 2 added same-version baselines, five H1 cases, the refusal rule across both venues, the runner import from `origin/main`, header validation/cleaning and `wroom24 WAIT`. Those changes address the examples the two rounds identified. The unbounded all-pending case, stale 24-hour evidence, skipped pre-upload floor and full-ring publication failure survive them. The producer’s reported compiles and rehearsal results are prior-round evidence.

##### Confidence and execution limits

Subjective confidence in each finding’s reality is **K1 99%, H1 >99%, H2 >99%, H3 98%, H4 97%, H5 >99%, H6 99%, H7 99%**. I estimate a **20% chance of an additional missed HIGH**. The largest residual uncertainty is the behavior of interrupted writes on the physical boards.

I ran the bundle and fence digests, Bash syntax check, pinned lint, both credential screens, a byte comparison of manual answer cleaning against the runner, scope comparison, repeat-block fixtures, H1 declaration/type syntax check, synthetic board gates and final-CI predicate. Fixture paths and board responses were substituted locally. **Unable to verify independently:** a linked H1 run against the future agent implementation, the three final firmware builds, physical flash interruption, board readings on the candidate, live PR/CI and an actual AI Studio exchange. The supplied readings describe the operator’s baseline. The FAIL verdict follows from the pinned contracts and reproducible gate behavior and does not depend on any unrun check.

#### Report A3: GPT Astra 6.2, Codex (a revision of A2, by its own words)

#### R6 v7.7.1.3 audit — GPT Astra — 2026-11-24

**Verdict — FAIL. Hold dispatch.** The write contract can reset a populated device ring after a metadata read error. Two WROOM gates accepted a simulated 900-byte minimum against a 1,024-byte floor. The 24-hour gate accepted the same storage snapshot that passed after the first hour. All 13 bundle files match the setup manifest.

**Audit lane.** Independent MEDIUM producer audit, GPT Astra lane. The harness identifies the auditor as Codex, GPT-6. The independent §2 inspection preceded reading the two internal rounds. This is a verification of that audit in the same session.

**Report integrity and corrections.** The original workspace report and its audit copy were byte-identical: 20,797 bytes, SHA-256 `a42004869688874066b59266f577d2a50e3b801848a815d898acebf7acd6ee40`. The previous relative link failed to deliver the artifact. On recheck, H3 is a recovery question and L2 is withdrawn. A1 and A4 pass their listed structural checks. The findings below retain their original identifiers.

| C12 — Rule 40 | Pass on the change | The live handler already opens the old namespace, reads meta and obtains NVS stats. The added JSON is specified to use RAM. Check D compares old and new NVS-call counts. |
| C13 — readability | Partial | The 11-character meta key, 15-character segment key, layouts and current row order are readable. K1 can make existing history unreachable. The wrap interruption leaves a documented cursor/epoch mismatch. Physical recovery is **unable to verify** (H3). |
| C14 — review B | Fail | All 13 supplied texts and their candidate diffs, including deleted lines, passed both screens. A harmless answer matched the runner’s cleaning byte for byte. The runner comes from `origin/main`, the operator puts system text in system instructions, the review chat has no tools, and refusal stops both routes. Coverage and revision binding remain incomplete (H6, H7). [Google AI Studio terms](https://aistudio.google.com/docs/terms) |

| §2.C | Inspection and result |
|---|---|
| 1 — compliant but wrong | Traced both ring helpers, full-ID lookup, row builder, meta load/save, per-device writer and outer loop. The signatures fit the live types and declaration order. S2 follows the old engine, the eight definitions precede T3, and the handler follows the NVS fragment. The H1 translation unit passed syntax-only compilation against extracted live types and declarations. K1 and H4 are behavioral defects. |
| 2 — evidence theater | H1 covers row selection, wrap, deduplication, collision, space and selected write failures. Its commit always succeeds and its yield stub is empty. On a board, `v2 PASS` combines RAM counters with aggregate partition usage and old meta. The unchanged-snapshot fixture establishes its limit (H2). |
| 3 — scope | All 46 distinct `scope` paths match §3, including the two dynamic filenames and conditional lessons. Generated board-specific YAMLs are temporary build products. |
| 4 — timing and retest | The readings record .173 changing from no history partition to 524,288 bytes after USB. The schedule samples every 15 minutes and persists at :10. A first empty cycle can WAIT. Firmware fixes require fresh baselines and a new WROOM observation window. Review B follows the delayed readings. H1, H2 and M1 cover the gaps. |
| 5 — repeatability | Ran `scope`, `copy`, both `insert` modes and `dev … before` twice in disposable fixtures. Scope stayed at 46, copy accepted matching contents and neither fence duplicated. A repeated pre-upload reading replaces the saved baseline as specified. |
| 6 — doctrine | The MEDIUM lane, reviewer roles, post-merge limits and board order agree with the governing texts. CI success, anchor checks and pipeline timing need correction. PLAN-046 permits the operator to recheck a late commit. |

##### CRITICAL finding

**K1 — A failed metadata read can reset a populated ring.** Prompt 2.2, lines 243 and 249, says “otherwise it returns false and leaves `*meta` unchanged” and “a fresh meta when `load_device_meta_` gives false”. A storage read error and an absent key therefore lead to the same initialization. If the subsequent writes succeed, slot 0 is replaced and the new meta advertises one valid segment. On a full ring one previous slot is overwritten and 23 fall outside the committed history. The old temperature/humidity writer cannot restore every per-device metric. This is a conditional data-loss path established from the contract. No board loss was observed.

**Disposition — fix before dispatch.** Distinguish an absent key and an explicitly supported format reset from a storage error. On error, preserve the ring, record ERROR and continue to the next device. Exercise a populated ring with a failed meta read followed by healthy storage. Verify zero writes on failure and resumption at the original cursor.

##### HIGH findings

**H1 — Both WROOM pre-upload checks bypass the floor.** Checks line 610 tests `[ "$want" = "v7.7.1.3" ]`. In lines 674–681, `echo "v2 PASS (before)"` and `return` come before the floor check. Prompt §7 requires “at least 1,024 B in every reading of the step”. Both pre-upload commands returned PASS with simulated .170 already on v7.7.1.3 and its minimum at 900 B. An upload can then reset the since-boot minimum. **Disposition — fix before dispatch.** Apply the floor immediately after parsing every WROOM status response. Test both pre-upload commands at 1,023 and 1,024 B on both accepted versions.

**H2 — A first-hour snapshot passes again at 24 hours.** Checks lines 719 and 728 use `(d.get("writes_ok") or 0) >= 1` and `if not last > last0:`, where the comparison epoch comes from before the upload. Identical storage responses passed at uptimes **4,800 and 86,500 seconds**. The matching WROOM boot and heap passed too. This proves the delayed gate can miss a writer that stopped after its first good cycle. On same-version retests, .173 and .170 also lack the C3’s pre/post boot comparison. Line 725 exempts full rings from entry growth, while Prompt 6.4 promises growth “by at least 10 for each such device”. **Disposition — fix before dispatch.** Save first-cycle boot, epochs and counters. Require subsequent progress from devices with new samples and from the old engine at the delayed read. Use epochs/counters for full-ring overwrites and check the boot transition on same-version reflashes. Align §6.4 and S6 with those tests.

**H4 — The writer and HTTP reader share unsynchronized C++ fields.** Review S2, lines 97–98, says “without a lock: a reading can mix two cycles”. S2 declares `static DevicePersistStatus g_dev_persist_status[PERSIST_DEVICE_ARRAY_LEN] = {};`. Conflicting concurrent access to ordinary fields from the persist and httpd tasks is a C++ data race. It also undermines the status evidence used by the gates. **Disposition — fix before dispatch.** Synchronize publication and copying of one device’s status. Perform JSON formatting and NVS operations outside that short critical section. Update S2, the review text and RAM accounting together.

**H5 — Required CI jobs can be skipped or neutral and still pass the final gate.** Checks line 1009 accepts `("success", "skipped", "neutral")`. Prompt §7 requires the named checks and seven browser jobs to “pass on the final head”. The pinned predicate accepted fixtures where the compile and one browser job were skipped, then accepted neutral conclusions too. Lines 974–988 also allow a completed review row without requiring the corresponding external-review comment. **Disposition — fix before dispatch.** Require success for each mandatory job and posted evidence for each completed external round. Test skipped and neutral mandatory jobs and a filled row with no review.

**H6 — Review B’s input and answer screens have gaps.** Checks lines 1120–1123 impose length and character restrictions, including `{16,}`. A short literal in a named password field can escape the check. Line 1043 writes `comment = rn.clean(answer) + "\n"`. The pinned runner separately calls `screen(f"turn {turn}", visible, answer=True)` before cleaning. Cleaning alone cannot detect a credential in a proposed public comment. The supplied bundle and its candidate diffs passed the existing screens. **Disposition — fix before dispatch.** Screen recognized credential fields across supported lengths and formats, allowing documented references. Screen the answer before releasing the comment. Report only the affected location on refusal.

**H7 — The review header can name a different revision from its diff.** Checks line 1081 captures `pull["head"]["sha"]`, but line 1086 fetches the diff through `"repos/%s/pulls/%s" % (repo, pr)`. Collection continues against the moving PR without confirming the original head and base. The answer step checks the saved header. The pinned runner calls `confirm_position(pr_url, token, pr)` before model use and before posting. **Disposition — fix before dispatch.** Verify the head and base after collection and again before releasing the comment. Regenerate a package whose revision moved. Apply the same input screening to the actual snapshot used by a fallback run.

##### MEDIUM and LOW findings

| ID | File and verbatim evidence | Consequence and disposition |
|---|---|---|
| M1 | Checks lines 714–715, `if all(d.get("result") == "pending" for d in devs):` | All-pending still returned WAIT after 24 hours. Bound WAIT by the next eligible scheduled cycle and a defined grace period so a missing hourly call produces a failure. |
| M2 | Checks lines 762 and 764, `sed -n 3p "$CS"` and `sed -n 4p "$CS"`. Prompt §2 promises “every anchor of Task Groups 1 to 4 and of item 7.2”. | F requires line positions absent from 7.1. §2 checks the ladder row but omits the “You are here” heading and the current-state edit locations. Verify unique textual anchors before editing and select them by text in F. |
| M3 | Prompt 5.1, “ending with `bash scripts/generate-header.sh`”. Pinned `AGENTS.md` lines 95–96 place `bash scripts/preflight.sh` immediately afterward. | TG5 and the cited pipeline have different endpoints. PRE-PR runs preflight later, after uploads. Give TG5 the complete sequence and specify when preflight must pass. |
| L1 | Pinned current state, “**Produce the v7.7.1.3 bundle, then R6**”. Supplied record template, “S3 (.191)”. | Completing 7.1 literally can leave the separate “What's Next” list at R6. Advance that list and label this step’s S3 measurement column .173. |

##### Two dispositions corrected on recheck

**H3 — Full-ring interruption is a recovery question.** Prompt 2.2, lines 252–253, writes “with `slot` the meta's `next_slot`” before updating meta. Handoff line 59 explicitly anticipates “a newer segment at `next_slot`, which the meta counts as the oldest” and instructs restore to validate headers/counts and sort epochs. The cursor can be stale. That alone does not establish that a complete replacement is invalid or that restore will return corrupt data. **Disposition — remove the HIGH rating.** Test the first overwrite of a full ring with an interruption between the segment and meta writes. Specify whether recovery accepts a complete newer segment and verify the returned epochs. Hardware behavior remains **unable to verify**. [ESP-IDF commit semantics](https://docs.espressif.com/projects/esp-idf/en/v5.5/esp32/api-reference/storage/nvs_flash.html)

**L2 — Withdrawn.** Operator line 129 says “run the first block yourself”. PLAN-046 decision 1 expressly says the operator “runs the block again only if a commit came after”. Prompt review item 7 also calls for the agent to repeat the round after a later commit. The two instructions can both be followed. No change is required on this evidence.

##### Internal rounds, confidence and limits

The round-1 fixes for first-cycle WAIT, final-head output, NVS RAM estimates, JSON printing and a tool-free AI Studio chat are present. Round 2 added same-version baselines, five H1 cases, the cross-venue refusal rule, import from `origin/main`, header validation, answer cleaning and `wroom24 WAIT`. The independent fixtures still reproduce H1, H2, H5 and M1. The producer’s reported compiles and rehearsals are prior-round evidence.

Subjective confidence in each retained blocking finding is **K1 99%, H1 >99%, H2 >99%, H4 97%, H5 >99%, H6 99%, H7 99%**. These estimates concern the contract or gate defect. Its frequency on a board is unknown. Estimated chance of another missed HIGH is **20%**.

Independently checked file hashes, embedded fences, shell syntax, prompt lint, both screens, answer cleaning, scope, repeat execution, C++ declaration/type syntax, synthetic board gates and the CI predicate. **Unable to verify:** linked H1 against the future implementation, candidate firmware builds, physical power interruption, candidate board readings, live PR/CI or an AI Studio exchange. The FAIL verdict stands on the pinned contracts and reproduced gate results.

#### Report M: Muse Spark 1.3 (comparison)

#### R6 v7.7.1.3 audit — GPT Astra — 2026-11-24

##### Audit lane self-attribution

This audit was executed in the "GPT Astra" independent-audit lane defined by
`steps/R6/r6-astra-audit-prompt.md`, but the executing auditor is **Muse Spark**
(Meta Muse), not GPT. The operator sent the Astra prompt to Muse in this session;
nothing in this report should be read as claiming the work was done by GPT, and
the reconciliation file for v7.7.1.4 should record Muse Spark as the auditor of
this lane. The audit followed the Astra prompt exactly, in its order: §2 first,
then the internal rounds in `v7.7.1.3-audit.md` §1, and only then this report.
Nothing outside the §1 paths and live sources was read; nothing on GitHub was
changed. No login, key or token value, and no login-form placeholder, appears in
this report.

##### Verdict: CONDITIONAL PASS

**No CRITICAL and no HIGH finding.** The §3 bar for dispatch — zero remaining
HIGH or CRITICAL defects — is met. The bundle is **not** an unconditional PASS:
it carries 14 MEDIUM findings and 22 LOW findings below, and dispatch should
be conditional on the planning session fixing, or explicitly accepting with a
recorded rationale, every MEDIUM finding. Three MEDIUMs should be fixed before
dispatch rather than merely accepted: **M1** (board evidence does not prove
dual-write), **M2** (the review-B answer path does not screen for credentials),
and **M9** (re-running a `before` reading destroys the baseline it is supposed
to preserve). The remaining MEDIUMs are fixable in the same render; none of
them, on its own, would stop an honest agent or let a wrong implementation pass
silently, which is why this is a conditional pass and not a fail.

Two claims that arrived as HIGH from the adversarial pass were checked against
the bundle and downgraded, with the reasons recorded in the findings: the
`.173` USB-flash order is pinned by the prompt and by the `v2 … before`
block (see L12), and `v2 PASS` is not the whole evidence for dual-write — the
host test H1 verifies the payload semantics and the `used_entries` growth is
independent board-level evidence of real writes (see M1). One claim that
arrived as a MEDIUM from the doctrinal pass was dropped outright after a
recount: §3's "CHANGED by hand (16)" lists exactly 16 files, and the 46-path
union is correct (see the §2.A note). A claim that the NVS entry arithmetic
did not reconcile was resolved by reading the assumption audit §5, which
derives it (see C10). A claim that the AI Studio paid/unpaid status was
unstated was resolved by PLAN-046 item 2 in the decision log, which states it
(see C14).

##### Inputs read

  `78c83bbcf71ffb0a9a6fedc14f90a84724a1e1ed`, read only as §1 allows:

- The SHA-256 check of §1 item 2: **all 13 bundle files matched**

  supplied files). This verifies the unpacked `bundle/` files, not the tar

Method: the three §2 sections were run as independent passes (doctrinal,
cross-cutting, adversarial) against the live product repo, then every finding
that would have decided the verdict was re-checked by the auditor against the

and the prompt with their own implementations of items 2.2 and 4.1 to

##### MEDIUM findings

###### M1 — `v2 PASS` does not prove dual-write on a board

- File: `bundle/prompts/phase7/v7.7.1.3-checks.sh`, block `b_v2`; prompt §6.4 / §7.
- Quote (checks.sh): `good = [d for d in devs if d.get("result") in ("ok", "dedup") and (d.get("writes_ok") or 0) >= 1]` and `if used - used0 < 10 * len(growing):` … `print("ERROR used_entries grew by %d …")`.
- Why: four gaps, each real. (1) No segment is ever read back: `v2` reads only
  `/api/storage-stats` — the new code's own RAM status plus NVS usage counters —
  so a segment written with wrong payload data but a plausible header counts as
  ok. (2) No old-vs-new agreement is checked: `v2` proves the old engine's
  `last_persist_epoch` grew and the new engine reported ok/dedup, never that
  both engines persisted the *same* hour's data for the *same* device. (3) The
  evidence is self-reported: the ok/dedup verdicts come from
  `g_dev_persist_status`, written by the code under test. (4) Dedup counts
  toward the 3-device bar, so a board that wrote once and then dedups forever
  keeps passing. Meta↔segment consistency is likewise unchecked: `v2` verifies
  the meta key format (FNV-1a hash) but never that `last_written_slot` points
  at a slot holding a valid segment.
- Why MEDIUM and not HIGH: `v2` is not the whole evidence. H1 verifies the
  payload semantics on the host (segment header, counts, data epochs and
  values, meta, wrap, dedup, NVS_LOW, collision, failed writes), and the
  `used_entries` growth check is independent board-level evidence that blobs
  were really written — it is not self-reported. A wrong-payload implementation
  would have to pass H1's payload assertions first. The residual hole is
  narrow but real: a handler that misreports, or a bug that manifests only
  against real NVS or the live device order, passes `v2`.
- Fix: add a read-back check (read at least one segment per device and compare
  its payload and epochs against live history) and an old-vs-new same-hour
  agreement check, or downgrade, in the prompt and in §7, what `v2` is allowed
  to claim — from proof of dual-write to "the new engine reported writes and
  NVS usage grew".
- Disposition: **fix before dispatch**. This is the step's headline claim and
  its first runtime write; the read-back check is the one addition that would
  close it.

###### M2 — `reviewb answer` does not screen the model answer before posting

- File: `bundle/prompts/phase7/v7.7.1.3-checks.sh`, block `b_reviewb`, `answer` mode.
- Quote (checks.sh): `comment = rn.clean(answer) + "\n"` — preceded only by a
  check that the answer begins with the turn-3 header unchanged.
- Why: the runner (`scripts/external-review.py`) screens every model answer
  with `screen(…, answer=True)` and fails if it contains a login literal. The
  AI Studio answer path only checks the header and applies `clean()`, which
  marks lines starting with `/` and replaces `@` — it neither removes nor
  detects secrets. A model answer echoing a credential from the (screened)
  inputs, or hallucinated from them, would be posted as `comment.md`.
- Fix: run the runner's `screen()` and the `reviewb` key regex over
  `answer.md` before writing `comment.md`, and refuse on a hit exactly as the
  input path refuses.
- Disposition: **fix before dispatch**. The inputs are screened; the output
  should be screened by the same standard.

###### M3 — The `reviewb` block has no prompt sentence at all

- File: `bundle/prompts/phase7/v7.7.1.3-agent-prompt-gpt-codex.md`; checks.sh block `b_reviewb`.
- Quote: the prompt never names `reviewb`, its inputs or its outputs. The
  review round (prompt line 756) says only: "the operator starts reviews A
  and C, then review B (Gemini 4 Neon in AI Studio with part B of the review
  file, or the runner)".
- Why: producer-contract 8.1 requires every gate/checkpoint to be a check-file
  block *and* every block assertion to come from a prompt sentence. This
  direction is violated: the block is anchored only in the review file
  (Part B), the operator page and process guide §2.4. The prompt's own §8
  describes the body check, not `reviewb`.
- Fix: add one sentence to the prompt's review round naming the block, its
  inputs and its outputs, or record `reviewb` as an operator-side block
  outside contract 8.1 with the exception stated.
- Disposition: fix in the same render.

###### M4 — The check file asserts things the prompt never says (over-asserting)

- File: `bundle/prompts/phase7/v7.7.1.3-checks.sh`.
- Quotes: `sec2` checks the SHA-256 of the supplied texts S2/T3/H1 and the
  existence of `config/gateway-sat-s3-4m-173.json.bak` (prompt §2 names
  neither); block `E` asserts `VERSION = "7.7.1.3"` in the render script,
  `App.version = 'v7.7.1.3'` in app-shell.js, and a count of 2 for `v7.7.1.3`
  in the C3 YAML, where prompt CHECKPOINT E says only "VERSION is 7.7.1.3
  and the side effects of bump-version.sh are present"; block `F` requires
  CURRENT-STATE line 3 to be the "Last verified" line and line 4 the
  next-action line naming v7.7.1.4, plus the exact row format
  `^| v7.7.1.3 | #<pr> |` (prompt 7.1 specifies neither line numbers nor
  that format — a compliant file with one added line above would fail F);
  block `v2` defines "first cycle" as uptime < 4500 s, requires the meta key
  to equal the FNV-1a hash, and counts growth as `10 * len(growing)`
  excluding full rings, where prompt 6.4 says only "each with its meta_key"
  and "grew by at least 10 for each such device"; block `prepr` asserts 9
  files in `firmware/core/*.h` and a no-deletion/type-change test (not in
  prompt §7); block `body` treats Inline as "posted" when the PR is not a
  draft (prompt §8 states only the `## Review A/C/B` comment rule).
- Why: an honest agent with a compliant implementation can fail a checkpoint
  on a requirement it was never given, and the failure message gives it no
  prompt sentence to check against. (The `inline` 30-minute timeout printing
  `inline PASS` is *not* part of this finding: the prompt's "at most 30
  minutes" makes that the designed behaviour.)
- Fix: add the missing sentences to the prompt, or remove the assertions from
  the check file. The CURRENT-STATE line-number assertions are the sharpest
  and should go first.
- Disposition: fix in the same render.

###### M5 — The check file does not assert things the prompt does say (under-asserting)

- File: `bundle/prompts/phase7/v7.7.1.3-checks.sh`, blocks `F` and `B`.
- Quote (prompt 7.3): the changelog entry must name the eight function names,
  `DeviceSegment`, and "no read path yet". Block `F` checks only
  `persist_all_devices_v2`, `persist_v2`, `24`, `252`, the new MAC and
  PLAN-046. Quote (prompt 7.1): the record must show Q5 "**Decided**" as well
  as Q3/Q4 "Implemented in v7.7.1.3"; block `F` checks only the latter.
  Quote (prompt §5): the word `free` is banned in the new code; block `B`
  counts only `\bfree\(`. Quote (prompt 7.5): the session log holds the
  24-hour evidence; block `F`'s grep list has `v2 PASS` and `image: ` but no
  `wroom24` string, so the 24-hour gate is enforced by nothing in the check
  file.
- Why: a prompt assertion with no block coverage is enforced only by human
  review, which is exactly what the check file exists to replace.
- Fix: add the missing checks to `F` (changelog names, Q5 Decided, a
  `wroom24` line in the session log) and widen block `B`'s forbidden-word
  count to bare `free`.
- Disposition: fix in the same render.

###### M6 — No in-PR step performs the routing of new recommendations

- File: prompt §7.6; process guide §2.5 item 6; producer-contract 8.6–8.7.
- Quote (prompt 7.6): the record states "every recommendation and where it
  was routed".
- Why: the process guide and contract require every new recommendation to be
  recorded in CURRENT-STATE "Unimplemented Recommendations" **or** opened as
  a GitHub issue — "no third option". The prompt only asks the record to
  *state* a routing, no step performs it, and block `F` does not check it.
  A record can claim a routing that never happened and pass `F`.
- Fix: add a step in Task Group 7 that performs the routing (record or issue)
  for each new recommendation, and have `F` check that the routed place
  exists.
- Disposition: fix in the same render.

###### M7 — The BASH_ENV login mechanism appears nowhere the agent reads

- File: decision log PLAN-046 item 6; operator page; prompt §2.
- Quote (decision log): the login reaches the Codex agent through `BASH_ENV`,
  with a script loading the login variables from the git-ignored
  `firmware/secrets.yaml`. The prompt says only (prompt line 39, and the §2
  set-check) that the login variables must be set, and the setup file never
  mentions the mechanism at all.
- Why: if the agent's shell lacks `BASH_ENV`, §2 fails with no recovery
  written in the prompt. The only mitigation is the operator page's
  separate-session check, which the agent never reads.
- Fix: one line in the prompt (§1 or §2) naming the mechanism, the script and
  the source file, and what to do if the §2 set-check fails.
- Disposition: fix in the same render.

###### M8 — The 1,024 B floor is not checked at every reading of .170

- File: checks.sh block `b_dev` / `b_v2`; prompt §7.
- Quote (prompt §7): "`min_free_heap` of .170 is at least 1,024 B in every
  reading of the step". Quote (checks.sh): the floor check in `b_dev` runs
  only when the wanted version is v7.7.1.3; `dev .170 before` and
  `v2 .170 before` record `min_free_heap` but do not fail below 1,024 B.
- Why: the §7 criterion overstates what the checks enforce. A below-floor
  pre-upload reading is the earliest signal that the board cannot keep the
  floor, and the prompt's own words promise it is caught.
- Fix: enforce the floor on the `before` readings too, or correct §7 to say
  "every reading after the upload".
- Disposition: fix in the same render.

###### M9 — Re-running a `before` reading overwrites the baseline

- File: checks.sh blocks `b_dev` and `b_v2`.
- Quote (checks.sh): the `dev` block writes its saved reading with an
  overwriting redirect to the `dev-$ip-$want.txt` file, and `b_v2` likewise
  overwrites `v2-$ip.txt`.
- Why: `dev <IP> before` run twice *before* the upload is harmless, but run
  *after* the upload it destroys the pre-upload baseline: the file then holds
  post-upload heap and boot, and `c3heap` fails fail-closed ("the saved
  reading before the upload belongs to this boot") — but the true baseline is
  unrecoverable without reverting the board and re-running Task Group 6. The
  same applies to the `v2` baseline. The check fails closed; the evidence is
  destroyed, not just the check.
- Fix: write `before` readings to a new file per boot (or refuse to overwrite
  a `before` file whose boot predates the current boot) instead of an
  unconditional overwrite.
- Disposition: **fix before dispatch**. The review-round re-run rule (round-1
  fix M4, now in the prompt) makes a second `before` run likely, not rare.

###### M10 — The WAIT retry loop is unpinned, and WAIT exits 0

- File: prompt §6.4; checks.sh header and block `b_v2`.
- Quote (checks.sh header): "v2, wroom24 and inline may also print a WAIT
  line and exit 0." Quote (prompt §6.4): on WAIT, run the block again after
  the next minute-10 cycle — with no stated maximum wait and no statement
  that WAIT is not PASS.
- Why: two failure modes. An agent that treats the first WAIT as failure
  stops, re-flashes or reports failure on a healthy board — most likely when
  an upload lands just before minute 10, so the first cycle after boot
  genuinely has no new 15-minute point. An agent (or a scripted chain) that
  treats exit 0 as pass proceeds past a WAIT as if `v2` had passed.
- Fix: pin "on WAIT, wait until after the next minute-10 cycle and re-run;
  WAIT is not PASS" and a maximum wait (for example two cycles) after which
  it is a failure.
- Disposition: fix in the same render.

###### M11 — A review-round firmware fix re-runs Task Group 6, but the re-run details are unpinned

- File: prompt review round item 4; checks.sh blocks `b_dev`, `b_v2`, `b_wroom24`.
- Quote (prompt): "a commit of a review round that changes the firmware
  repeats the device tests" — with the calls of a re-run now given (round-2
  fix F1). What is *not* pinned: whether the old `before` readings must be
  discarded (`b_dev` overwrites them silently, so the agent is never told
  the old file is now invalid evidence and must not be quoted); what happens
  if the agent reuses the old `boot` epoch for `wroom24` (fail-closed — the
  boot must be within 120 s of the given epoch — but the failure message does
  not say "you used a stale epoch"); and the unexplained restarts in
  `readings.txt` — ".170 (2252 -> 7332) and .189 (28376 -> 29456) restarted
  between the two readings; the cause is not known yet" — the prompt pins
  nothing about investigating a mid-window restart before accepting a fresh
  `boot` epoch. A restart during the 24-hour window resets uptime below
  86,400 s, so the clock restarts silently; if the restart was caused by the
  new firmware, the 24-hour test can never pass and the operator may keep
  supplying fresh epochs instead of investigating.
- Fix: pin "discard pre-fix before-readings; any restart inside the 24-hour
  window is STOP-and-investigate, not a fresh epoch."
- Disposition: fix in the same render.

###### M12 — MEDIUM tier prescribes fixed code, and long fixed texts arrive as fences, not supplied files

- File: prompt §2.2 and S2/T3/H1; producer-contract rules 5.2 and 6.5.
- Quote (contract 5.2): "MEDIUM prescribes only interfaces and the tests
  that encode them. HIGH may prescribe finished code, under rule 3.4."
  Quote (contract 6.5): "A long text goes to the agent as a supplied file:
  a new file, a whole rewritten file, or an insertion of more than 10 lines.
  It is in the bundle with its SHA-256 in the gate record, and the check file
  copies it (copy) after a hash check; the agent never retypes it."
- Why: this prompt is MEDIUM tier, yet S2 is fixed implementation, not
  interfaces — constants with fixed values (24 slots, 252 guard), a result
  enum, a status struct and a global array — plus H1, a fixed host test with
  a fixed fake-NVS implementation. The prompt cites PLAN-046 as authority for
  the fixed text but never names the departure from the tier rule. S2, T3
  and H1 are each far over 10 lines, yet they reach the agent as fenced text
  inside the prompt, extracted by the check file's `fence` function — not as
  files under `bundle/files/` copied by `copy` after a hash check, and with
  no SHA-256 in a gate record. The *treatment* matches the rule (a supplied
  file is a fixed text: a finding against it is reported, not fixed by the
  agent) but the *delivery mechanism* departs, and the mechanism is the trust
  anchor the rule exists to provide. Mitigation: the check file's
  verbatim-once checks catch a corrupted or duplicated fence.
- Fix: either supply S2/T3/H1 as files, or record the fence extraction as the
  approved exception to rules 5.2 and 6.5, with PLAN-046 named as the
  authority for the fixed values.
- Disposition: fix in the same render (a recorded exception is enough).

###### M13 — The "screens for login literals and keys" claim does not hold for both review-B paths

- File: review file Part B, line 167; decision log PLAN-046 item 2;
  `scripts/external-review.py` `screen()`; checks.sh block `b_reviewb`.
- Quote (review file): the block "screens them for login literals and keys".
  Quote (external-review.py): `screen()` calls only `login_literal()`, which
  matches an authorization header value and a user-and-password login form.
  The `reviewb` block adds a key/password/token regex on top of the runner's
  `screen()`; the runner fallback does not.
- Why: a bare key or token literal in the diff (for example an API key
  assignment) would pass the runner's screen and be sent to the model. The
  claim is true for the AI Studio path and false for the runner fallback. The
  runner is pre-existing product code outside R6's scope, so the fix in this
  bundle is to correct the claim, not to rewrite the runner.
- Fix: correct the review file and decision log to name which path screens
  keys, and record extending the runner's screen as a follow-up outside R6.
  (Round-2 fix F3 already made the refusal rule correct: on a refusal, review
  B runs nowhere on that data.)
- Disposition: fix in the same render (text correction).

###### M14 — H1 tests a configuration that never ships, and never tests the handler, the lambda or the assembled compile

- File: prompt §2.2, H1; checks.sh block `b_host`.
- Quote (H1): 7 persistent devices vs 4 live; a `devices[]` order with an
  extra entry first; a `wan` device that does not exist live; a fake NVS
  commit that always succeeds; fake free-entry accounting for the 251/252
  boundary.
- Why: H1 cannot catch a bug that manifests only at the live count or order
  (for example an array sized from the device count that is correct at 7 and
  wrong at 4, or vice versa), and it proves nothing about commit failure or
  allocation failure (the prompt handles a failed `new (std::nothrow)`
  allocation, but H1 never exercises it). Separately, the
  `/api/storage-stats` `persist_v2` object — the thing `v2` actually reads on
  a board — is produced by code H1 never executes, and the Task Group 3
  lambda line is exercised only by `esphome compile` in Task Group 6.
- Fix: add a live-config host case (4 devices, live order) and a host check
  that compiles the handler tail with extreme values and parses the output,
  or record the gap as an accepted limitation with the board `v2` as the
  backstop. (Round-2 fix F5 declined the handler host check for the same
  reason and relied on the re-run rule; with M9 fixed that reliance is
  sounder.)
- Disposition: fix or explicitly accept with a recorded rationale.

###### M15 — Scope omissions: canonical-tool side-effect files not in the 46

- File: prompt §3; `scripts/render_sensor_config.py` (`--write` targets);
  `scripts/generate-fixtures.js`.
- Quote (producer-contract 7.4): §3 must list "every file that canonical
  tools change as a side effect … taken from a rehearsal run".
- Why: `render_sensor_config.py --write` also writes `tests/fixtures/sensors.json`,
  `tests/fixtures/api-status.json` and `src/aggregator_config.h`, none of
  which is in the 46; `generate-fixtures.js` writes variants' fixture files
  of which only `manifest.json` is in scope. In *this* step those files carry
  no version or MAC, so they should be content-stable — and both internal
  rehearsals ran the pipeline to `PRE-PR PASS` without a scope-gate trip, so
  no change occurred in practice — but the §3 list is incomplete against
  rule 7.4. The failure direction is fail-closed (an unexpected change trips
  PRE-PR), not silent escape; the risk is that a tripped gate on a file the
  list never promised invites the agent to "fix" the list rather than
  investigate the change.
- Fix: add the files to §3 as known-stable, or record them as
  rehearsal-verified no-ops.
- Disposition: fix in the same render.

  Contract 7.7 wants each task group ending in a checkpoint; the `dev` /
  `v2` / `image` gates exist in substance but no letter is given.
