###### L2 — Dropped standard-section sentences have no stated reason

- File: prompt §1–§9 vs the live v7.7.1.2 prompt. Contract 7.11 requires a
  stated reason for dropped sentences; the drops appear step-driven but none
  is stated. Fix: one line per drop, or a single note that the drops are
  step-driven.

###### L3 — Contract 7.13 wording vs the prompt's check file

- File: producer-contract 7.13. Quote: the `inline` and `body` blocks are
  "those of the last merged check file", while the prompt runs them from this
  step's own check file. Contract-wording ambiguity, not an execution defect.
  Fix: reword 7.13.

###### L4 — Next-step handoff written "N/A" without testing whether v7.7.1.4 assumptions changed

- File: prompt §7.6. Quote: "N/A — Batch 2 is rolling (PLAN-044)". Prompt
  §7.4's architecture notes do change the v7.7.1.4 assumptions (24-slot ring
  / 252 guard replaced by its budget). The handoff does not exist yet — the
  planning session produces it after merge — so this is a note for that
  session, not a defect in the agent's work. Fix: test the assumptions when
  writing the handoff.

###### L5 — Blocks A/C exempt version lines from "no other line changed"

- File: checks.sh blocks `A`/`C`. The version bump (Task Group 5) has not run
  at those checkpoints, so the exemption should be unreachable. Harmless;
  remove it or leave it as a guard.

###### L6 — The only verbatim finished code outside S2/T3/H1 is two one-line call sites

- File: prompt §3.1. The two `persist_all_devices_v2` call lines are call
  sites, not bodies, and A3 otherwise passes. Note only. Separately, §5's
  narrowed `std::` ban lists only named tokens; an unlisted token such as
  `std::move` would pass the prompt and block `B`. Fix: widen the ban to
  `std::` generally or record the narrowing as deliberate.

###### L7 — Task Group 6.2's conditional recompile has no preceding `esphome clean`

- File: prompt §6.2. Every other §6 compile and process guide §2.3 say
  "clean before AND after compile". Fix: add the clean.

###### L8 — PLAN-046 wording divergences (three small ones)

- (a) Leaves-fleet condition: decision log item 5 makes `.170` leave the
  fleet only "if it cannot keep that floor and later memory changes do not
  give back about 4 KB"; plan §11 risk 11 states it without the condition
  (though the same row mentions the later memory changes). Fix: align the

- (b) Review-B order: plan §6 item 4 lists "the runner, or Gemini 4 Neon in
  AI Studio" (runner first), against the AI-Studio default in decision log

- (c) Plan §5's leftover line "a test board first: v7.7.1.3 names .189, .170
  and .173" names the boards in an order that is not .173-first, against

- Also LOW: the review-B token scope and duration (Issues read and write;
  Pull requests, Contents and Metadata read-only; 30 days) appears only in

  half of `body final`; the 228-byte figure is absent from the prompt §6, the
  process guide, the contract, the operator page and plan §5 (omission, not

###### L9 — Prompt §5.1 ends the AGENTS.md pipeline one step short

- File: prompt §5.1. Quote: run "the pipeline of `AGENTS.md`, in its order,
  ending with `bash scripts/generate-header.sh`" — AGENTS.md's pipeline ends
  with `bash scripts/preflight.sh`. Preflight is still run by the PRE-PR
  gate, so nothing is skipped in practice. Fix: name `preflight.sh` as the end.

###### L10 — "No new static buffer besides the status array" is asserted, not checked

- File: review file S2; checks.sh block `B`. CHECKPOINT B checks first lines,
  guard order, forbidden words and includes — not for an added `static`
  buffer in the eight agent-written definitions. The guarantee rests on
  review, not on a gate. Fix: add a `static` check to block `B`.

###### L11 — Cross-path refusal is procedural, not technical

- File: review file Part B; operator page step 8.1; checks.sh `reviewb`.
  `reviewb` prints "run no review B on this data, by the runner or
  elsewhere", but there is no shared refusal state between the AI Studio path
  and the Actions-tab runner, so a second operator could still run the runner
  on refused data. The review file and operator page both state the rule, and
  the runner refuses its own inputs. Fix: record the refusal in the PR (for
  example a comment naming the place) so the other path can see it, or
  accept as operator discipline.

###### L12 — The .173 baseline is post-wipe, and the prompt never says so

- File: prompt §6 intro; operator page "Before you start"; `readings.txt`.
  The prompt and the `v2 … before` block do pin the order: the operator's
  USB flash of .173 completes before dispatch, and `v2 … before` stops if
  the board has no history partition, with a message naming the missing flash.
  What is *not* stated is that .173's `before` baseline is therefore a
  freshly erased NVS (zero old-ring segments, unlike .189/.170 with 1,080),
  so its old-engine growth starts from zero and its evidence measures a
  wipe-and-rebuild, not an upgrade preserving production history. Fix: one
  sentence in §6.1 recording that the .173 baseline is post-wipe.

###### L13 — The architecture doc still says a `DeviceSegment` is 226 bytes

- File: product `Docs/v7.7-v7.8-persistence-architecture.md` §5.2 (and
  §5/§10 tables). The live struct is 228 bytes, verified by the
  `static_assert(sizeof(DeviceSegment) == 228)` in `data-model.h` (24-byte
  header + 12 bytes of counts + 192 bytes of data). The prompt says 228 and
  governs, but an agent that sizes a buffer or a test expectation from the
  architecture doc first truncates 2 bytes. Fix: correct the doc to 228
  before dispatch, or it will mislead the next reader.

###### L14 — `first_epoch` is undefined when every row's first point has epoch 0

- File: prompt §2.2 line 242. Quote: `header.first_epoch` is "the smallest
  non-zero epoch of data[k][0] over the rows". H1 covers one row starting at
  epoch 0, but not all rows starting at 0 (plausible right after a reboot
  before time sync). Then `first_epoch` stays at its default of 0 while
  `last_epoch > 0`. Fix: one H1 case with all rows starting at epoch 0.

###### L15 — T3 tests only `dev_ring_next_`, never `dev_ring_valid_`

- File: prompt §2.2, T3. T3 asserts only the cursor helper;
  `dev_ring_valid_` ("returns `valid + 1`, and never more than `max_slots`")
  is exercised only inside H1's wrap case. A `valid+1`-without-cap
  implementation compiles, passes T3, and is caught only if H1's wrap case
  inspects `valid_segments` — it does, but the compile-time tripwire the
  prompt advertises for the ring helpers covers only half of them. Fix: add
  the cap assertion to T3.

###### L16 — Two scope entries are conditional dead entries

- File: prompt §3. `Docs/lessons/index.md` and `Docs/lessons/firmware.md`
  are touched by Task Group 7 only if the operator supplies a lesson number;
  if none is given they sit in the scope list unmodified. Harmless (the
  scope gate checks changed paths against the list, not the reverse), but the
  list then over-claims. Fix: obtain a lesson number or drop them from the list.

###### L17 — `copy` is not idempotent; `insert` has no order repair

- File: checks.sh blocks `b_copy`, `b_insert`. A second `copy` fails its own
  pre-condition (the 8 existing files are no longer `main`'s versions, the 3
  new files exist) — fail-closed, not corrupting, but Task Group 0 cannot
  simply be re-run without manually restoring `main`'s versions first.
  `insert S2` after `insert T3` has succeeded (in a state where S2 was then
  removed) lands S2 after T3, breaking the required order, and re-running
  `insert T3` is a no-op so there is no self-repair. Fix: make a `copy`
  re-run a documented no-op when the hashes already match the supplied
  files, and make `insert` refuse or repair when S2 would land after T3.
  (`scope` is idempotent for identical arguments; `dev` is covered by M9.)

###### L18 — The prompt's precedence line omits the producer-contract

- File: prompt line 7. Quote: "where this prompt and
  `Docs/development-process-guide.md` conflict, the process guide governs;
  `AGENTS.md` governs both." The contract's preamble makes the contract
  govern producer prompts, but the prompt's own precedence line never mentions
  the contract, so a prompt-vs-contract conflict has no resolver stated in
  the prompt itself. Fix: add "; the producer-contract governs this prompt."

###### L19 — Full-ring power loss leaves the orphan segment counted

- File: prompt §2.2 steps 7–8; session handoff carry-forward. In a
  not-yet-full ring an orphan segment is not counted (`valid_segments` not
  yet incremented). In a full ring, a power loss between a segment and its
  meta leaves a newer segment at `next_slot`, which the meta counts as the
  oldest; v7.7.1.4 orders the segments by their header epochs and checks
  magic, version, `metric_count`, `points_per_segment` and `counts[k]` of
  each before use. So the orphan is counted in `valid_segments` (24) and is
  only rescued from mis-ordering by v7.7.1.4's epoch ordering — which is
  specified in the handoff but is not part of this bundle and is not enforced
  by any check here. A torn segment blob would fail the header-magic check; a
  fully-written one is a genuine (newest) segment, mis-ordered, not a
  false-valid one. Fix: already recorded in the handoff (round-1 fix L4);
  carry it into v7.7.1.4's bundle as a stated requirement, not only a handoff
  note.

###### L20 — The operator page's two uses of "app" are not distinguished

- File: operator page steps 8.2–8.4. Step 8.2 requires a plain chat, not the
  app, for the reviewing model; step 8.4 says post `comment.md` "through the
  operator's AI Studio app" as an alternative to `gh pr comment`. The app is
  evidently meant only to post a fixed file, but the two uses of "app" are
  not distinguished in the text. Fix: one sentence in 8.4 saying the app
  posts the saved file only and the reviewing model never runs in it.

###### L21 — C7 note: §1.3 delegates "the live values of this step" to the assumption audit

- File: prompt §1 item 3; `phase7-batch2-assumption-audit.md` §4.
  The assumption audit is a supplied file in this bundle, which the agent is
  told to read in §1, not another prompt — so contract rule 4.1 is not
  violated. The operative values are inlined in the prompt. The residual is
  that the live `devices[]` order and the reference image sizes at `a33a5cc`
  live only in that file. Recorded as a LOW note, not a finding against the
  bundle.

###### L22 — C10 note: the NVS entry arithmetic is derived, in the assumption audit

- File: `phase7-batch2-assumption-audit.md` §5. A 232-byte blob takes 10
  entries (8 data, 2 overhead): confirmed by 1,080 × 10 + 4 = 10,804 used
  entries on .189 and .170, against 5,324 free of 16,128 total. The new ring
  costs 4 devices × 24 slots × 10 + 4 metas × 4 = 976 entries, far below the
  5,324 free, and one cycle writes at most ~4 segments + 4 metas plus the
  old engine's one segment, far below the 252-entry guard. The safety
  conclusion holds with a large margin. Recorded here so the derivation is
  visible in the audit record; the bundle should cite it in the prompt's §2.2
  or §6 rather than leaving it to be found in the assumption audit.

##### Checks that could not be run, and other limits

- No live boards, no GitHub run, no dispatch: the device blocks (`dev`,
  `v2`, `image`, `wroom24`, `reviewb`, `inline`, `body`) were judged by
  reading, against the live product code and the operator readings. The
  internal rounds did run the setup file and the prompt with their own
  implementations to `PRE-PR PASS`, including the three compiles, and ran
  the board blocks against stand-ins; those rehearsals corroborate the
  reading but are not this audit's own execution.
- The v7.7.1.4 restore and the runner's behaviour on `origin/main` after
  `a33a5cc` are prospective and were checked only against the texts and code
  named above.
- The bundle's own texts were screened with the runner's `screen()` and with
  the `reviewb` key regex by this audit: **all 15 texts pass both** (prompt,
  check file, review file, audit file, operator page, plan of record,
  decision log, process guide, producer contract, prompt-audit template,
  consolidated audit template, session handoff, assumption audit, setup file,
  readings). This closes the one gap the cross-cutting pass could not close
  on its own (the audit file itself).

##### Confidence statement

(a) **Each HIGH finding's reality:** there is no HIGH finding, so nothing to
assess here. The two candidates that arrived as HIGH were re-checked against
the bundle and downgraded with reasons in M1 and L12: `v2 PASS` is not the
whole evidence for dual-write (H1 verifies the payload, `used_entries`
growth is independent), and the `.173` flash order is pinned by the prompt
and by `v2 … before`.

(b) **Chance of a missed HIGH:** low, but not negligible — I would put it at
roughly 10–20%. The reasons it is low: three independent passes (doctrinal,
cross-cutting, adversarial) plus two internal rounds that executed the bundle
to `PRE-PR PASS` with their own implementations, and every verdict-deciding
claim was re-checked by the auditor against the prompt and check file. The
reasons it is not lower: the device blocks were judged by reading, not by
running against the boards; the `v2` growth threshold (10 entries per growing
device) was not calibrated against a rehearsal of an actual persist cycle;
and the `v2 … before` baseline storage was confirmed to overwrite like `dev`
(M9) but the exact growth arithmetic across a real before/after window was
not observed. A missed HIGH, if one exists, is most likely to be in the
device evidence — the area M1 already flags as the weakest.

(c) **Checks that could not be run:** the device blocks against real boards
(no boards reachable from here); `reviewb` / `inline` / `body` against a real
PR (no live GitHub run in this session); the 24-hour `wroom24` reading
(prospective by definition). The lint check (C9) *was* run, twice, in a copy
of the product repo: exit 0, 3 L6 warnings, all false positives of the
heuristic (each flagged line lists `--write` before `--check` on the same
line).

##### Check of the internal rounds' findings

Read only after §2 was complete, per §1 item 4.

**Round 1 — verdict FIX FIRST; 1 HIGH, 6 MEDIUM, 10 LOW.** The HIGH (H1:
`v2` fails instead of printing WAIT when a board boots between minute 0 and
minute 10) is real and its fix holds in this bundle: `b_v2` now prints WAIT
below 4,500 s uptime when the only failures are the old engine not writing
or fewer than three devices written, and the prompt asks for uploads between
minute 12 and minute 55. Round 2 re-tested it and it holds. All six MEDIUMs
(M1 body-final head, M2 RAM of new keys, M3 `persist_v2` entry length, M4
review-round firmware fix, M5 template wording, M6 model holding a GitHub
write tool) were fixed as the dispositions state, and the fixes are visible
in the bundle I read — with two qualifications this audit adds. First, M4's
fix (re-run Task Group 6 after a firmware-changing fix) is real in outline
but incomplete in detail (see M11: old before-readings, stale boot epoch,
mid-window restarts). Second, M6's fix (plain AI Studio chat, no tools) is
real, but the operator page's wording still leaves the "app" ambiguity
(L20) and the answer path is unscreened (M2). Round 1's LOWs are fixed as
stated, except L6 (setup file vs prompt ordering of `gh pr ready` and the
PATCH), which was deliberately not changed — acceptable, as the dispositions
say. Round 1's Q2 (the API encryption key literal in the C3 YAML) is known
and recorded as RV-08; R6's hunks do not reach it, and this audit's key
screen confirms no hit in the R6 diff texts.

**Round 2 — verdict FIX FIRST; 0 HIGH, 2 MEDIUM, 8 LOW.** Both MEDIUMs were
fixed as the dispositions state and the fixes are visible in the bundle:
F1 (Task Group 6 cannot run again once a board runs v7.7.1.3) — `dev <IP>
before` and `v2 <IP> before` now accept v7.7.1.2 or v7.7.1.3, and the prompt
gives the calls of a re-run; F2 (the `reviewb` key screen missed the
project's own secret names and GitHub tokens) — the regex now matches key
words after any word prefix and the GitHub token patterns, and this audit ran
it against all 15 bundle texts with no hit. Of the LOWs, F5 (entries
"printed in parts" checked only on the board) was deliberately not changed,
relying on the F1 fix; with M9 fixed that reliance is sounder, but the
handler check itself is still missing (see M14). F3, F4, F6, F7, F8, F9, F10
are fixed as stated. Round 2's Q1 (c3heap reads before the first v2 cycle, so
it cannot see the NVS RAM of the new keys) is answered in the dispositions as
intended, with the .170 floor as the guard — this audit agrees, with M8 as
the remaining gap in that guard. Q3 (wroom24 printing a failure before 24
hours) is fixed: it now prints `wroom24 WAIT` below 86,400 s.

**What the internal rounds missed that this audit found:** the `v2 PASS`
evidence gap (M1), the unscreened review-B answer (M2), the `reviewb` block
with no prompt sentence (M3), the check file's over- and under-assertions
(M4, M5), the unperformed recommendation routing (M6), the missing BASH_ENV
mechanism in the prompt (M7), the floor not enforced on `before` readings
(M8), the `before`-reading overwrite (M9), the unpinned WAIT loop (M10), the
re-run details (M11), the tier and supplied-file doctrinal drift (M12), the
runner-vs-reviewb screening asymmetry (M13), and the scope omissions (M15).
None of these is HIGH. The internal rounds' execution to `PRE-PR PASS` is
consistent with this audit's verdict: the bundle runs, and its defects are in
what its evidence proves and in what its checks assert, not in whether it
executes.

#### R6 v7.7.1.3 audit — Gemini-Neon-High — 2026-11-24

##### Verdict

**FAIL** — 0 CRITICAL, 1 HIGH, 5 MEDIUM, 4 LOW findings.

The firmware write-path design, compile-time test `T3`, host test `H1`, NVS entry math, Rule 40/61 compliance, 46-path scope guard, and PLAN-046 cross-document alignment are strong and compile cleanly against live `main` (`a33a5cc`). However, one **HIGH** trust-boundary defect remains in the `reviewb` block of `v7.7.1.3-checks.sh` (extracting the trusted AI Studio `system.txt` system instructions and turn preambles from the untrusted PR head without mechanically verifying the file's SHA-256 against the bundle hash), alongside five concrete **MEDIUM** findings (three more `reviewb` / AI Studio data-boundary gaps, a literal `sizeof(DeviceSegment)` spelling trap in CHECKPOINT B, and a `used_entries` growth false-failure edge case in `b_v2` on re-flash/full rings). All findings are compact and fixable in a single bundle re-render before dispatch.

##### Detailed §2.C Adversarial Inspection Notes

1. **Compliant-but-wrong:**
   - *Looked for:* Missing headers or forward declarations in `dashboard/sensor_history_multi.h` assembly order (`config.h` → `data-model.h` → `nvs-persistence.h` → `health-check.h` → `deferred-management.h` → `ping-adapter.h` → `aggregator-runtime.h` → `web-handler.h` → `registration.h`); signature or type mismatches against `SensorEntity`, `MetricState`, `HistoryBuffer`, `DeviceHistoryMeta`, `DeviceSegment`, `NvsKey`, `PERSIST_*`; `constexpr` legality of `dev_ring_next_`, `dev_ring_valid_`, and `dev_persist_result_name_`; zero-length array issues if `PERSIST_DEVICE_COUNT == 0`; `b_B`/`b_D` static-check false positives against compliant implementations.
   - *Found:* All headers (`<cstring>`, `<new>`, `<nvs.h>`, `<nvs_flash.h>`) are in `firmware/core/config.h`; all Phase 7 types/constants are in `data-model.h`; all key/NVS helpers precede S2 in `nvs-persistence.h`; `handle_storage_stats_()` in `web-handler.h` comes 5 fragments after `nvs-persistence.h`. A contract-conforming implementation written from §2.2 alone compiled cleanly against live `data-model.h` and `nvs-persistence.h` with `g++ -std=gnu++20 -O0 -Wall` (0 warnings) and passed all of T3 and H1 (`H1: all checks passed`). One false-positive trap was found in `b_B` line 406 (M4: `sizeof(DeviceSegment)` literal requirement rejecting `sizeof(*seg)`). Note also that `b_B`'s `close()` brace scanner skips `//` comments and string/char literals but not `/* ... */` block comments (low risk unless an agent puts unbalanced braces inside a block comment).

2. **Evidence-theater:**
   - *Looked for:* Whether `v2 PASS` can succeed without real flash writes or dual-write; whether `H1` leaves major contract paths untested; whether `b_D` can be bypassed by calling new NVS wrappers.
   - *Found:* `v2 PASS` checks both real NVS `used_entries` growth on flash and old-engine `history.last_persist_epoch` advance, so it genuinely proves dual-write on `.189`/`.170` (where the old ring is already full at 1,080/1,080 and adds 0 net entries); on a freshly erased `.173`, +14 entries come from the old engine's first segment + meta + namespace, while 3–4 v2 devices add +42 to +56 entries. Payload bytes are not read back until v7.7.1.4 by design, so payload correctness relies on `H1` + `T3`. In `H1`, `g_open_count` is incremented (line 361) but never asserted; `maybe_yield_nvs_scan_` is an empty stub `{}` (checked textually by `b_B` instead); and failure injection does not cover `nvs_get_stats != ESP_OK`, `nvs_commit != ESP_OK`, `new (std::nothrow)` returning `nullptr`, `metric_count`/`points_per_segment` meta mismatch, or `meta.next_slot >= meta.max_slots` clamping. In `b_D` line 521, the NVS-call regex (`\bnvs_\w+\(|open_history_nvs_\(|load_history_meta_\(`) does not list `load_device_meta_(` or `save_device_meta_(` (covered by review S2 instead). Finally, `b_v2` lines 725–727 have an inverted problem — a false-failure risk on re-flash/full rings when NVS page-split blobs coalesce (M5).

3. **Scope leakage:**
   - *Looked for:* Discrepancies between the 46 paths in `b_scope`, the text of prompt §3, and the files touched by Task Groups 0–7, `bump-version.sh`, `render_sensor_config.py`, `provision.sh`, and `generate-fixtures.js`.
   - *Found:* Exact 46-for-46 match between `b_scope` and §3. Confirmed `config/sensors.json` + the 4 `.bak` files + `firmware/core/data-model.h` are the only tracked source files holding the old `outside` MAC (besides historical `Docs/archive/completed-phases/phase4-implementation-plan.md`), and `render_sensor_config.py --write` updates `tests/fixtures/manifest.json`, `src/gateway_manifest.h`, `firmware/esp32-c3-multi-sensor.yaml`, and `dashboard/sensor_history_multi.h`. Confirmed `config/gateway.json` and `firmware/*-gw.yaml` are gitignored and `provision.sh satellite` cleans active configs.

4. **Deferred ambiguity:**
   - *Looked for:* Interaction of `.173`'s USB flash with `v2 192.168.120.173 before`; `:10` persist minute and `v2 WAIT` timing; review-round firmware fixes re-running Task Group 6 on boards already on `v7.7.1.3`; Review B timing after `wroom24`.
   - *Found:* `.173` USB flash (`readings.txt` line 26: `history_bytes 524288, nvs_stats available True`) satisfies `v2 before` cleanly. For `:10` persist timing, 15-minute buckets (`:00, :15, :30, :45`) always add either an average or a gap (`add_gap(epoch)`) with `epoch > 0` for all 4 persistent devices (`data-model.h` lines 180–239), so only a boot in `:00:01..:09:59` lacks a new point at the first `:10` cycle; the second `:10` cycle occurs at most 70 minutes (4,200 s) after boot, which `first = up < 4500` covers with a 5-minute margin. Review-round firmware re-runs properly accept `v7.7.1.3` in `dev before` and `v2 before`, enforce `preboot < boot - 30` in `c3heap`, and restart `wroom24` from the new `boot` epoch — except for M5 (`b_v2` `used_entries` growth on re-flash) and L4 (`CURRENT-STATE.md` snapshot column header). Review B is consistently ordered after the 24-hour readings across prompt, review file, and operator page.

5. **Non-idempotent steps:**
   - *Looked for:* Re-running `insert S2`, `insert T3`, `copy`, `scope`, and `dev`.
   - *Found:* `insert` returns cleanly when `contains == 1` and preserves the trailing blank line (`t + wc -l - 1 == last_text_line`). `copy` skips files matching their SHA-256 and skips `Docs/plan-of-record.md` once `por_ok` passes after item 7.2. `scope` overwrites `.git/scope-allowed.txt` deterministically for the same arguments (prompt §3 line 98 warns to keep the original date). `dev` overwrites `dev-$ip-$want.txt`; if `dev <IP> before` is accidentally run post-upload, `c3heap` line 618 explicitly catches it (`preboot < boot - 30`).

6. **Doctrinal drift:**
   - *Looked for:* Drift between PLAN-046 decisions, `development-process-guide.md`, `producer-contract.md`, `prompt-audit-template.md`, `plan-of-record.md`, `consolidated-audit-template-phase7.md`, `r6-setup.txt`, `v7.7.1.3-review.md`, and `Docs/operator/R6-persist-write-path.md`.
   - *Found:* Strong consistency across the doctrinal files (`body <PR#> final`, review B placement, Muse qualification, `r6-setup.txt` internal numbering). One doctrinal gap: `Docs/operator/R6-persist-write-path.md` omits the Unpaid Service / public-data-only boundary that `Docs/development-process-guide.md` §2.4 and `PLAN-046` establish (M3).

##### CRITICAL Findings

None.

---

##### HIGH Findings

###### H1 — `b_reviewb` extracts trusted `system.txt` and Turn 1–3 preambles from the untrusted PR head without mechanical SHA-256 verification

- **File:** `bundle/prompts/phase7/v7.7.1.3-checks.sh` (lines 1082–1109, 1147–1152)
- **Quoted text:**
  ```python
  def at_head(path):
      obj = json.loads(gh("repos/%s/contents/%s?ref=%s" % (repo, path, head)))
      return base64.b64decode(obj["content"].replace("\n", "")).decode("utf-8")
  prompt, review = at_head(ppath), at_head(rpath)
  ...
  def turns():
      lines = review.split("\n")
      got = {}
      for name in ("System", "Turn 1", "Turn 2", "Turn 3"):
          label = "Part B, %s — " % name
          hits = [i for i, l in enumerate(lines) if l.startswith(label)]
  ...
  t = turns()
  ...
  files_out = {
      "system.txt": t["System"] + "\n",
      "turn1.txt": t["Turn 1"] + "\n\n" + "\n\n".join(w1) + "\n",
      "turn2.txt": t["Turn 2"] + "\n\n" + "\n\n".join(w2) + "\n",
      "turn3.txt": t["Turn 3"] + "\n\nBegin your answer with this header, unchanged; then your answer:\n\n" + header + "\n",
  }
  ```
- **Why HIGH:** `system.txt` is placed into the AI Studio chat's **System Instructions** — the highest-trust privilege boundary enforcing `<<<BEGIN UNTRUSTED DATA>>>` isolation. In `scripts/external-review.py` (`template()`, lines 256–265), the trusted system prompt and turn preambles are loaded strictly from `main`, never from the PR head; similarly, internal Round 2 F6 moved `external-review.py` loading in `b_reviewb` to `origin/main` so the PR cannot alter what reviews it. Yet `b_reviewb` still pulls `system.txt` and `Turn 1–3` instruction preambles from `at_head(rpath)` (the untrusted PR branch head) and never compares `sha(review)` or `sha(prompt)` against the bundle's known SHA-256 hashes — even though `v7.7.1.3-checks.sh` line 64 already embeds `v7.7.1.3-review.md`'s exact SHA-256 (`22f70c343a3ad29002f2c4bca6862209d3fe6042115faa0556354ca6789f551e`) and `$SAVE/files/$RF` is already verified on the build host. Relying exclusively on the operator visually comparing two 64-hex-character SHA-256 strings in terminal output leaves a live prompt-injection escalation path from the PR head into `system.txt`.
- **Concrete fix:** Read `turns()` from the locally staged, hash-verified bundle copy `$SAVE/files/$RF` (after checking its SHA-256 against the hardcoded hash in `COPY`), rather than from `at_head(rpath)` — and also add bounds checking in `turns()` (`while i < len(lines)` / `while j < len(lines)`). Keep fetching `prompt` and `review` from `at_head(...)` for `w1` (untrusted data blocks) and printing their SHA-256 values, and optionally warn/fail if `sha(review)` at the head differs from the verified bundle hash.

---

##### MEDIUM / LOW Findings

###### M1 (MEDIUM) — `b_reviewb` omits raw PR title screening, regressing PR #240's inline security fix

- **File:** `bundle/prompts/phase7/v7.7.1.3-checks.sh` (lines 1113–1133)
- **Quoted text:**
  ```python
  metadata = json.dumps({"number": pull["number"], "title": pull["title"], "state": pull["state"],
                         "base_ref": base_ref, "base_sha": base, "head": head}, ensure_ascii=False)
  data1 = [("agent-prompt", prompt), ("review-file", review)]
  data2 = [("pr-metadata", metadata), ("pr-body", pull.get("body") or ""), ("changed-files", changed), ("diff", filtered),
  ...
  try:
      rn.screen("diff", diff, diff=True)
      for name, content in data1 + data2:
          rn.screen(name, content, diff=name == "diff")
  ```
- **Why MEDIUM:** Live `scripts/external-review.py` line 306 explicitly calls `screen("pr-metadata", pr["title"])` on the unescaped PR title (recorded in `CURRENT-STATE.md` line 128 as an inline security fix of PR #240). Executing `rn.screen()` on a synthetic title containing a quoted `-u` login form confirmed that `rn.screen("pr-metadata", raw_title)` refuses it (`True`), whereas `rn.screen("pr-metadata", json.dumps({"title": raw_title}))` misses it (`False`) because `json.dumps` escapes `"` as `\"`.
- **Concrete fix:** Call `rn.screen("pr-metadata", pull["title"])` before building `metadata` and include `("pr-title", pull["title"])` in the `key.search` loop.

###### M2 (MEDIUM) — `reviewb <PR#> answer` does not screen `answer.md` for credentials/keys or check GitHub's 65,000-character comment limit

- **File:** `bundle/prompts/phase7/v7.7.1.3-checks.sh` (lines 1040–1048)
- **Quoted text:**
  ```python
  answer = norm(open(os.path.join(outdir, "answer.md"), encoding="utf-8").read())
  if not answer.startswith(header + "\n") or not answer[len(header):].strip():
      print("ERROR answer.md does not begin with the header of turn3.txt, unchanged, followed by the review: send turn3.txt again, or run review B on the runner"); sys.exit()
  comment = rn.clean(answer) + "\n"
  changed = sum(1 for x, y in zip(answer.split("\n"), comment.rstrip("\n").split("\n")) if x != y)
  open(os.path.join(outdir, "comment.md"), "w", encoding="utf-8").write(comment)
  ```
- **Why MEDIUM:** Unlike `scripts/external-review.py` lines 360 and 393 — which call `screen(..., answer=True)` on the model's visible response and reject comments exceeding `65000` characters before posting — `reviewb <PR#> answer` only runs `rn.clean(answer)`. If Gemini 4 Neon writes a login-like example or key-shaped literal in a suggested fix, `comment.md` is written and posted unscreened (violating §7 / S8 / SEC-003). If the answer exceeds GitHub's 65,536-character comment cap, `gh pr comment` fails at posting time without a clear diagnostic.
- **Concrete fix:** In `reviewb <PR#> answer`, run `rn.screen("answer", answer, answer=True)` and the same `key` regex over `answer`, refusing to write `comment.md` on a hit; also fail cleanly if `len(comment) > 65000`.

###### M3 (MEDIUM) — Operator page Step 8 omits the explicit AI Studio Unpaid Service / private-data prohibition required by C14

- **File:** `bundle/files/Docs/operator/R6-persist-write-path.md` (lines 98–105)
- **Quoted text:**
  ```markdown
  8. Review B, in Google AI Studio with Gemini 4 Neon (PLAN-046):
     1. On the build host, run the first block below with the PR number. It writes four files and prints the head and two SHA-256 values; compare the two values with the gate record. If it prints `refused`, run no review B, not on the runner either: the data holds a login or a key. Paste the line into the planning session; it names the place, never the value.
     2. In AI Studio, open a new chat with Gemini 4 Neon. Use a plain chat, not your app: the model that reads the PR must have no way to write to GitHub. Put the text of `/root/v7713/reviewb-<PR#>/system.txt` into the chat's system instructions. Attach `turn1.txt` and send it. When it answers, attach and send `turn2.txt`; then `turn3.txt`.
  ```
- **Why MEDIUM:** Audit check C14 explicitly requires that "the operator page keeps the private planning repository, the fleet file and logins out of AI Studio." `Docs/development-process-guide.md` §2.4 (line 138) and `Docs/decisions/decision-log.md` (PLAN-046) state that free AI Studio is an Unpaid Service where Google and human reviewers may read submitted content and that only public repository text may be submitted, but the operator page — the document the operator actively executes during Step 8 — never states this boundary or warns against attaching/pasting files from `/root/Planning-Workflow`, `config/fleet.local.csv`, or logins.
- **Concrete fix:** Add one explicit sentence to Step 8.2: free AI Studio is an Unpaid Service (Google and human reviewers may read what is submitted), so attach only the four files written by `reviewb` — never anything from the private planning repository (`/root/Planning-Workflow`), `config/fleet.local.csv`, or a login.

###### M4 (MEDIUM) — CHECKPOINT B (`b_B`) hardcodes the literal spelling `sizeof(DeviceSegment)` inside `nvs_set_blob(...)`, violating contract rule 8.1

- **File:** `bundle/prompts/phase7/v7.7.1.3-checks.sh` (line 406) vs `bundle/prompts/phase7/v7.7.1.3-agent-prompt-gpt-codex.md` (lines 252, 282)
- **Quoted text (`v7.7.1.3-checks.sh` line 406):**
  ```python
  seg = re.search(r"nvs_set_blob\([^;]*sizeof\(DeviceSegment\)", p, re.S)
  ```
  **Quoted text (`v7.7.1.3-agent-prompt-gpt-codex.md` line 282):**
  ```markdown
  in `persist_device_segment_`, the call of `nvs_get_stats(HISTORY_PARTITION_LABEL` and `DEV_PERSIST_MIN_FREE_ENTRIES` before the segment write, and the segment write before `save_device_meta_(`;
  ```
- **Why MEDIUM:** Right above S2 in `firmware/core/nvs-persistence.h` line 618, the existing `persist_hourly_segment()` writes `nvs_set_blob(handle, key, snapshot, sizeof(*snapshot));`. A coding agent following the file's own idiom (`nvs_set_blob(handle, sk.s, seg, sizeof(*seg))`) or passing a `constexpr size_t` local variable satisfies item 2.2 step 7 and passes `H1` line 457 (`g_blobs[sk].size() == sizeof(DeviceSegment)`), yet fails CHECKPOINT B. This violates `prompts/producer-contract.md` rule 8.1 ("No supplied test and no check depends on an order, a spelling, a shared state or any other choice that the prompt leaves free").
- **Concrete fix:** Relax `b_B` line 406 to match `r"nvs_set_blob\([^;]*sizeof\s*\(\s*(?:DeviceSegment|\*\s*seg)\s*\)"` (or simply `r"nvs_set_blob\("` inside `persist_device_segment_`, since `H1` line 457 already verifies the exact runtime byte length `sizeof(DeviceSegment)`), OR explicitly state `sizeof(DeviceSegment)` in CHECKPOINT B's prose. Relaxing the regex is preferable under contract rule 8.1.

###### M5 (MEDIUM) — `b_v2` `used_entries` growth check can false-fail on full-ring or hours-2–23 re-flashes when page-split NVS blobs coalesce

- **File:** `bundle/prompts/phase7/v7.7.1.3-checks.sh` (lines 725–727)
- **Quoted text:**
  ```python
  growing = [d for d in good if (d.get("valid_segments") or 0) < p.get("slots", 24)]   # a full ring overwrites: no new entries
  if used - used0 < 10 * len(growing):
      print("ERROR used_entries grew by %d since 'v2 %s before', less than 10 for each of the %d devices whose ring is not full" % (used - used0, ip, len(growing))); sys.exit()
  ```
- **Why MEDIUM:** In ESP-IDF NVS, a multi-page blob that spans a 126-entry page boundary consumes 1 extra chunk-header entry (11 entries instead of 10 for a 228/232 B segment; 5 instead of 4 for a 60 B meta). When an existing key is overwritten and the new copy fits on a single page, `used_entries` decreases by 1 entry.
  - If Task Group 6 runs again after a review-round firmware fix when rings are already full (`valid_segments == 24`, so `len(growing) == 0`), the threshold is `10 * 0 = 0`; any single coalesced blob among the 5 overwritten segments or 5 overwritten metas produces `used - used0 == -1 < 0`, triggering a false `ERROR` and stopping the agent.
  - Similarly, on a re-flash during hours 2–23 (`dm_*` metas already exist in `used0`, removing the +4-entry-per-device first-write cushion), each growing device adds +10 entries, so a single coalesced overwrite elsewhere gives `used - used0 == 10 * len(growing) - 1`, also falsely failing.
- **Concrete fix:** Guard the growth check with `if growing:` (do not require `used - used0 >= 0` when `len(growing) == 0`), and when `growing` is non-empty allow a small coalescing margin on re-runs (e.g., `used - used0 < 10 * len(growing) - (6 if any((d.get("valid_segments") or 0) > 1 for d in growing) else 0)`, or `9 * len(growing)`).

- **(a) Reality of each HIGH finding:**
  - **H1: 99% confident.** Verified directly in `v7.7.1.3-checks.sh` lines 1082–1109 and 1147–1152: `review = at_head(rpath)` fetches from the PR head, `turns()` parses `Part B, System — ` directly from `review`, and `system.txt` is written without comparing `sha(review)` against line 64's embedded bundle hash.
- **(b) Probability of an additional missed HIGH defect:** **~10%.** All 13 bundle files, `r6-setup.txt`, `astra/readings.txt`, all relevant live firmware/script/doc files at `a33a5cc`, `lint-prompts.sh`, the `external-review.py` + `key` screens, and a live `g++` compile/run of `S2` + contract implementation + `T3` + `H1` were directly verified. Remaining uncertainty is concentrated in checks that could not be executed in this environment (below).
- **(c) Checks unable to run in this environment:**
  1. **Real board reads/uploads (`.173`, `.189`, `.170`) and full ESPHome 2026.4.1 cross-compiles (RISC-V C3, Xtensa WROOM, Xtensa S3):** No LAN access to the boards and no ESPHome toolchain installed in this sandbox (`unable to verify` live board behavior and Xtensa/RISC-V codegen directly; mitigated by the scratch `g++ -std=gnu++20` compile of `S2` + 8 functions + `T3` + `H1`, static inspection of `handle_storage_stats_()` and `render_sensor_config.py`, and Round 1/2 compile evidence).

  3. **Direct HTTPS fetch of `https://aistudio.google.com/docs/terms`:** Blocked by tool URL policy (`unable to browse directly`); verified via search index snippets of `aistudio.google.com/docs/terms` (publication date March 23, 2026, Unpaid Services vs Paid Services terms). Verdict does not swing on this check.

##### 5. Detailed §2.C Adversarial Inspection & C10/C13 Technical Verification

###### 5.1 Scenario 1 — Compliant-but-wrong (declaration order, mental & `g++` compile)
- **What was checked:** Assembled fragment order in `scripts/assemble-sensor-history.sh` (`config.h` → `data-model.h` → `nvs-persistence.h` → `health-check.h` → `deferred-management.h` → `ping-adapter.h` → `aggregator-runtime.h` → `web-handler.h` → `registration.h`); exact placement of `S2` after `persist_hourly_segment()`, the 8 definitions of item 2.2 after `S2`, and `T3` at EOF of `firmware/core/nvs-persistence.h`; availability of every type/constant in `web-handler.h` (`handle_storage_stats_()`) and in `b_host` (`h1_types.h` + `h1_funcs.h`); integer-promotion and boundary cases in `dev_ring_next_` / `dev_ring_valid_`; buffer-chunk sizing against `char num[160]` in `handle_storage_stats_()`.
- **What was found:**
  - Every symbol referenced by `S2`, the 8 functions, and `T3` (`HistEntry`, `HistoryBuffer`, `MetricState`, `SensorEntity`, `devices`, `NUM_DEVICES`, `PERSIST_POINTS_PER_SEGMENT`, `PERSIST_DEVICE_COUNT`, `PERSIST_DEVICE_ARRAY_LEN`, `PERSIST_DEVICE_IDS`, `PERSIST_METRIC_COUNTS`, `DEV_HIST_MAGIC`, `DEV_HIST_VERSION`, `MAX_PERSIST_METRICS`, `DEVICE_ID_MAX_LEN`, `MAX_DEVICE_SLOTS`, `DeviceHistoryMeta`, `DeviceSegment`, `HISTORY_PARTITION_LABEL`, `NvsKey`, `device_meta_key_`, `device_segment_key_`, `open_history_nvs_`, `maybe_yield_nvs_scan_`, `export_latest_entries_`) is declared earlier in `data-model.h` or `nvs-persistence.h`.
  - Every symbol referenced by `handle_storage_stats_()` in `web-handler.h` (#8 in assembly) is declared in `data-model.h` (#2) or `nvs-persistence.h` (#3). Both `firmware/esp32-c3-multi-sensor.yaml` and `generate_board_yaml()` include `../dashboard/sensor_history_multi.h` before the `on_time` lambda calling `persist_all_devices_v2(now.timestamp)`.
  - A clean implementation written solely from item 2.2's contract was compiled and executed with `b_host`'s exact extraction script under both `g++ -std=gnu++20` and `g++ -std=gnu++23 -Wall -Wextra`: `0` warnings, `H1: all checks passed`.
  - Splitting each `persist_v2` device JSON object across 2–3 `snprintf(num, sizeof(num), ...)` calls keeps each chunk well under `160` bytes even at `UINT32_MAX` / `UINT16_MAX` and 32-character device IDs. (Note: when `PERSIST_DEVICE_COUNT == 0`, looping `0 .. PERSIST_DEVICE_COUNT - 1` emits `"devices":[]` cleanly without reading the dummy 1-element `PERSIST_DEVICE_ARRAY_LEN` slot.)

###### 5.2 Scenario 2 — Evidence-theater (`v2 PASS` on boards and `H1` on host)
- **What was checked:** Whether an agent or buggy firmware could achieve `v2 PASS` without dual-writing to flash, and whether `H1` + `T3` actually enforce every behavioral requirement of item 2.2.
- **What was found:**
  - **On boards (`v2 PASS`):** Not theater. `b_v2` checks three independent signals simultaneously: (1) `history.last_persist_epoch > last0` (read from NVS `hist_meta` on flash, proving the old engine wrote in the cycle); (2) `nvs_stats.used_entries - used0 >= 10 * len(growing)` (read from `nvs_get_stats("history", ...)` on flash — on `.189` and `.170` the old 1,080-slot ring is already full so old-engine overwrites add `0` net entries, meaning `+30` to `+56` net entries physically proves new blobs were written to the `history` NVS partition); and (3) `persist_v2` RAM state (`writes_ok >= 1`, `0` failures, matching FNV-1a `meta_key`s). Because R6 intentionally adds no v2 read path, on-device byte-level payload readback is deferred to `v7.7.1.4` and covered on the host by `H1`. (On a hypothetical post-24h re-flash where all 4 rings already hold 24 segments, `len(growing) == 0`, so `v2` relies on `last > last0` and fresh-boot `writes_ok >= 1`.)
  - **On host (`H1` + `T3`):** High behavioral fidelity across 20+ cases (decoupled `devices[]` ordering, `"wan"` prefix vs `"wan_ping"` `strcmp`, skipping `history == nullptr` metrics, fewer history metrics than `metric_count`, epoch-0-only buffer, epoch-0 first point in `first_epoch`, segment-before-meta write order, immediate `nvs_commit` after meta write, dedup, 26-cycle wraparound at 24 slots, 251 vs 252 `free_entries` guard, segment-write failure recovery, meta-write failure status preservation & slot retry, collision preservation, ring-size / length / version / magic reset, and `open_history_nvs_` failure). Three minor branches in 2.2 are not fault-injected in `H1`: `nvs_commit` returning an error (`H1`'s fake `nvs_commit` always returns `ESP_OK`), `nvs_get_stats` returning an error, and the `"V2 persist:"` `ESP_LOGI` prefix. Reviewers A/B/C inspect those lines directly.

###### 5.3 Scenario 3 — Scope leakage (the 46 paths of §3 vs Task Groups 0–7)
- **What was checked:** Set comparison of §3 vs `b_scope`, `.gitignore` coverage of generated/temporary artifacts, and intra-file diff checks in `v7.7.1.3-checks.sh`.
- **What was found:**
  - Exact 46-for-46 match between §3 and `b_scope` (`2` setup + `11` copied + `14` unique hand-edited not already in copied + `17` unique bump/pipeline not already in hand-edited + `2` new = `46` unique paths).
  - `.gitignore` at `a33a5cc` ignores `dashboard/dashboard.min.html`, `firmware/.esphome/`, `config/gateway.json`, `config/aggregator.json`, `config/sensors-*.json`, and `firmware/*-gw.yaml`, so `scope-gate.py`'s `--others --exclude-standard` scan will not trip on build or provisioning artifacts, and `.git/scope-allowed.txt` resides inside `.git/`.
  - `Docs/lessons/index.md` and `Docs/lessons/firmware.md` are included in `scope-allowed.txt` unconditionally (as required by `producer-contract.md` rule 8.6), while §5 forbids editing them unless the operator supplies a lesson number; reviewers check that constraint if no lesson number is issued.

###### 5.4 Scenario 4 — Deferred ambiguity (USB flash, persist minute, review-round re-flash, Review B timing)
- **What was checked:**
  1. Operator USB flash of `.173` vs agent `v2 192.168.120.173 before`.
  2. Minute-10 persist schedule vs `v2` waiting (including boots between `:00` and `:10`).
  3. Review-round firmware fix after boards already run `v7.7.1.3` (re-running Task Group 6: `dev <IP> before`, `v2 <IP> before`, `c3heap`, `wroom24` boot epoch, `used_entries` delta).
  4. Review B running after the 24-hour readings.
- **What was found:**
  1. `astra/readings.txt` confirms `.173` was erased and USB-flashed on 2026-11-24 and already reports `v7.7.1.2`, `history_bytes 524288`, `nvs_stats.available True`. If that state were ever absent, `b_v2` (`before`) stops cleanly with an explicit message naming the missing USB flash.
  2. Averages run at `:00/:15/:30/:45` and persist runs at `:10`. Uploads started between `:12` and `:55` always cross at least one 15-minute boundary before `:10`. Even if an upload of `.189` or `.170` slipped into `:00–:10`, `b_v2` (`first = up < 4500`) detects `((len(good) < 3 and rest_empty) or not last > last0)`, prints `v2 WAIT`, and exits `0` until the next hour's `:10` cycle (`up` ~3,600–4,200 s `< 4,500` s).
  3. On a review-round firmware fix (or single self-repair after a device check failure), prompt line 749 instructs re-running `5.2`, `CHECKPOINT E`, and `6.1`–`6.5`. `b_dev` (`before`) and `b_v2` (`before`) both accept `^v7\.7\.1\.[23]$` and overwrite `$SAVE/dev-$ip-before.txt` and `$SAVE/v2-$ip.txt`; `c3heap` compares `.189` against the fresh pre-upload reading (`preboot < boot - 30` holds because `.189` ran throughout `6.1`); `dev 192.168.120.170 v7.7.1.3 floor` emits the new `boot <epoch>` for `wroom24`; and `b_v2` counts `10 * len(growing)` only over devices with `valid_segments < 24` (each newly written 228 B `DeviceSegment` adds 10 NVS entries while overwriting existing `dm_<hash>` adds 0 net entries, so `+10` per growing device holds on re-runs too).
  4. Operator page steps 6–8 and `v7.7.1.3-review.md` line 10 consistently place Reviews A & C after the inline round, `wroom24` at 24 hours, and Review B last (see LOW note `L-4` regarding `S6` phrasing during Reviews A & C).

###### 5.5 Scenario 5 — Non-idempotent steps (`insert`, `copy`, `scope`, `dev` run twice)
- **What was checked:** Exact state transitions when `insert S2`, `insert T3`, `copy`, `scope`, and `dev` are invoked twice.
- **What was found:** All four blocks are idempotent:
  - `b_insert` checks `contains "$NV" "$TMP/$what.txt"`; when already `1`, it prints `inserted $what` and returns `0` without modifying `$NV`.
  - `b_copy` skips any file whose SHA-256 already equals `$want`, and for `Docs/plan-of-record.md` also skips when `por_ok` passes (preserving item 7.2's edits across `copy` and `copy verify`).
  - `b_scope` truncates `.git/scope-allowed.txt` via `cat > "$a"` before appending the 2 dynamic paths and verifying `46` lines.
  - `b_dev` writes `$SAVE/dev-$ip-before.txt` when `want=before` and `$SAVE/dev-$ip-v7.7.1.3.txt` when `want=v7.7.1.3`, so re-running `dev .189 v7.7.1.3 c3heap` never clobbers `dev-192.168.120.189-before.txt`.

###### 5.6 Scenario 6 — Doctrinal drift & C10 NVS arithmetic check
- **Doctrinal drift:** None found. All deliverables of `Docs/development-process-guide.md` §2.5 stay in-PR (§6/§7); §9 is strictly tag + issue-close verification; all three R6 boards are tested with test board `.173` first; `phase7-batch2-assumption-audit.md` §4 records live values from `a33a5cc` and answers all six §4.1 questions.
- **C10 NVS entry arithmetic (verified against ESP-IDF v5.5 `nvs_flash.html` and `readings.txt`):**
  - Partition `0x80000` = `524,288 B` = `128` pages × `4,096 B`; each page holds `126` entries of `32 B` = `16,128` total entries (`10,804 used + 5,324 free = 16,128` on `.189` and `.170`).
  - Old engine: `1` namespace entry + `3` entries for 20 B `HistoryMeta` + `1,080 × 10` entries for 232 B `SegmentSnapshot` (`1` `BLOB_DATA` header + `ceil(232/32)=8` data + `1` `BLOB_IDX`) = `10,804` entries (exact match to `.189` and `.170`).
  - New v2 engine (4 devices, 24 slots): per device, `DeviceHistoryMeta` (`60 B` → `1` header + `ceil(60/32)=2` data + `1` `BLOB_IDX` = `4` entries) + `24 × DeviceSegment` (`228 B` → `1` header + `ceil(228/32)=8` data + `1` `BLOB_IDX` = `10` entries) = `244` entries/device × `4` = `976` entries (`+56` on first cycle; `4,348` `free_entries` remaining at 24h steady state).
  - Because `nvs_stats.free_entries` includes the `126` entries of NVS's 1 reserved empty GC page (`available_entries = free_entries - 126`), `DEV_PERSIST_MIN_FREE_ENTRIES = 252` guarantees at least `126` writable `available_entries` (one full page), whereas an old-engine overwrite needs only `10` transient entries before erasing the superseded segment (`3` for `hist_meta`).

##### 6. Findings

###### LOW Findings

###### L-1 (LOW) — `b_D` checks `\bnew\b` on unstripped source in `handle_storage_stats_()`, so an ordinary `//` comment containing the word `new` fails `CHECKPOINT D`

- **File and line:** `prompts/phase7/v7.7.1.3-checks.sh`, lines 521 and 537–538 (in `b_D`).
- **Quoted text:**
  ```python
  if len(re.findall(r"\bnvs_\w+\(|open_history_nvs_\(|load_history_meta_\(", fn)) != len(re.findall(r"\bnvs_\w+\(|open_history_nvs_\(|load_history_meta_\(", ofn)):
      sys.exit("handle_storage_stats_() calls NVS more often than on main")
  ...
  if re.search(r"\bnew\b|malloc|std::string", tail):
      sys.exit("no new allocation in persist_v2")
  ```
- **Why it matters:** Unlike `b_B` (line 417, `sed 's://.*$::'`), `b_D` does not strip `//` comments from `tail` before testing `\bnew\b`. A natural comment in `handle_storage_stats_()` such as `// Phase 7: print persist_v2 for the new engine (no new allocation)` triggers a false `CHECKPOINT D` failure (`no new allocation in persist_v2`), costing the agent its single self-repair comment under setup item 10 (or stopping the run if the agent edits code without noticing the comment triggered `\bnew\b`). Conversely, line 521's NVS-call count regex checks `\bnvs_\w+\(|open_history_nvs_\(|load_history_meta_\(` without including the new `load_device_meta_\(|save_device_meta_\(` helpers.
- **Concrete fix:** Strip `//` line comments from `tail` (e.g. `tail_code = re.sub(r"//.*", "", tail)`) before running `re.search(r"\bnew\b|malloc|std::string", tail_code)`, and add `|load_device_meta_\(|save_device_meta_\(` to line 521's regex.
- **Disposition recommendation:** `accept-as-is` (or apply if re-rendering `v7.7.1.3-checks.sh`).

---

###### L-2 (LOW) — `b_B` requires literal `sizeof(DeviceSegment)` inside `nvs_set_blob(...)` and `"delete "` with a trailing space, narrower than item 2.2's prose

- **File and line:** `prompts/phase7/v7.7.1.3-checks.sh`, lines 406–415 (in `b_B`), vs `prompts/phase7/v7.7.1.3-agent-prompt-gpt-codex.md`, lines 252 and 282.
- **Quoted text:**
  ```python
  seg = re.search(r"nvs_set_blob\([^;]*sizeof\(DeviceSegment\)", p, re.S)
  ...
  for w in ("open_history_nvs_(", "maybe_yield_nvs_scan_(", "nvs_close(", "new (std::nothrow) DeviceSegment", "delete ", "persist_device_segment_("):
  ```
- **Why it matters:** Producer contract rule 8.1 states that no check should depend on a spelling choice that the prompt leaves free. Prompt 2.2 step 7 specifies `writes the whole DeviceSegment, sizeof(DeviceSegment) bytes`, while `persist_hourly_segment()` immediately above in live `firmware/core/nvs-persistence.h` (line 618) writes `nvs_set_blob(handle, key, snapshot, sizeof(*snapshot))`. If the agent mirrors line 618 with `sizeof(*seg)` (or binds `const size_t sz = sizeof(DeviceSegment);` before calling `nvs_set_blob`), `b_B` fails `CHECKPOINT B` once. The failure message (`persist_device_segment_ needs ... nvs_set_blob(... sizeof(DeviceSegment)) ...`) is explicit, so the one-repair rule recovers it cleanly, making impact LOW.
- **Concrete fix:** Accept `sizeof\s*\(\s*(?:DeviceSegment|\*seg)\s*\)` in `b_B` line 406 (or state `sizeof(DeviceSegment)` explicitly in `CHECKPOINT B` line 282) and match `r"\bdelete\b"` instead of `"delete "` in line 413.
- **Disposition recommendation:** `accept-as-is` (self-diagnosing error message).

---

###### L-3 (LOW) — Prompt §1 and Review File check `S7` name two `CURRENT-STATE.md` recommendation rows, while item 7.1 and `b_F` require three

- **File and line:** `prompts/phase7/v7.7.1.3-agent-prompt-gpt-codex.md`, line 49 (vs line 701), and `prompts/phase7/v7.7.1.3-review.md`, lines 111–113.
- **Quoted text:**
  - `v7.7.1.3-agent-prompt-gpt-codex.md` line 49:
    > ``1. `CURRENT-STATE.md` — "Version and Phase", "Open Issues" (the row "WROOM heap"), "Phase 7 Device Validation Snapshot", "Board Fleet and Measurements" and "Unimplemented Recommendations" (the two rows of PR #246); Task Group 7 edits them.``
  - `v7.7.1.3-review.md` lines 112–113:
    > `fleet rows, the row "WROOM heap", the C3 snapshot with the new values, the two rows of PR #246 in Unimplemented Recommendations),`
  - `v7.7.1.3-agent-prompt-gpt-codex.md` line 701:
    > ``- In "Unimplemented Recommendations", the row "Restart check 2026-11 | Firmware `.bin` size checkpoint …" becomes **Implemented in v7.7.1.3** (CHECKPOINT E and the block `image`); the row of PR #246 review A Q3–Q5 becomes **Implemented in v7.7.1.3** for Q3 and Q4 and **Decided** for Q5 (the full `DeviceSegment`; the blob size returns in v7.7.1.4), and the row of the `c3heap` stop becomes **Implemented in v7.7.1.3** (PLAN-046).``
- **Why it matters:** Item 7.1 and `b_F` (lines 770–772) require updating three rows in "Unimplemented Recommendations" (adding the `.bin` size checkpoint row alongside the two PR #246 rows), whereas §1 item 1 and review checklist `S7` still say `"the two rows of PR #246"`. The agent will follow item 7.1 and pass `b_F`, so nothing breaks, though an external reviewer reading `S7` literally could question the third edited row.
- **Concrete fix:** Update prompt §1 line 49 and `v7.7.1.3-review.md` line 112 to say `"the .bin size checkpoint row and the two rows of PR #246"`.
- **Disposition recommendation:** `accept-as-is` (or polish if re-rendering).

###### L-4 (LOW) — Review File check `S6` lists the 24-hour readings without noting they are still pending when Reviews A and C run

- **File and line:** `prompts/phase7/v7.7.1.3-review.md`, lines 106–110 (vs line 10 and `Docs/operator/R6-persist-write-path.md` lines 96–98).
- **Quoted text:**
  ```text
  S6. Device evidence. .173 first (dev before and after, v2 before and after a cycle, the S3 image),
      then .189 (dev before, c3heap after, v2) and .170 (dev before, dev with the floor after, v2,
      the WROOM image), and the 24-hour readings (wroom24, the second reading of .189, v2 of .189 and
      .170). Each output is verbatim in the PR body and the session log; a block that stopped was
      reported, not rerun with another value.
  ```
- **Why it matters:** Per `Docs/operator/R6-persist-write-path.md` steps 6 and 7 and the table at the top of `v7.7.1.3-review.md` (line 10, outside the pasted Common part), Reviews A and C run *before* the 24-hour wait of `wroom24`, while Review B runs *after* it. Under Common part rule 4 (`Missing evidence is UNCLEAR, not FAIL`), Reviews A and C evaluating `S6` strictly before step 7 may mark `S6` `UNCLEAR` and output `FIX FIRST` solely because the 24-hour readings have not happened yet.
- **Concrete fix:** Append `(recorded after the 24-hour wait, before review B; pending during reviews A and C)` after `.170)` in `v7.7.1.3-review.md` line 109.
- **Disposition recommendation:** `accept-as-is` (Reviews A and C also read prompt item 6.5, which states the timing).

---

###### L-5 (LOW) — Minor check-vs-prose alignment gaps in `6.4` (`growing` ring filter) and `b_body` (`Fixes #215` / `Coding agent:` line)

- **File and line:** `prompts/phase7/v7.7.1.3-agent-prompt-gpt-codex.md`, line 688 (vs `v7.7.1.3-checks.sh` lines 725–726), and `prompts/phase7/v7.7.1.3-checks.sh`, lines 955–968 (`b_body`, vs prompt lines 782–783, 787, 801).
- **Quoted text:**
  - `v7.7.1.3-agent-prompt-gpt-codex.md` line 688:
    > ``at least three devices have the result `ok` or `dedup` with `writes_ok` of 1 or more; the history partition's `used_entries` grew by at least 10 for each such device since `v2 <IP> before`;``
  - `v7.7.1.3-checks.sh` lines 725–726:
    ```python
    growing = [d for d in good if (d.get("valid_segments") or 0) < p.get("slots", 24)]   # a full ring overwrites: no new entries
    if used - used0 < 10 * len(growing):
    ```
- **Why it matters:** (a) `b_v2` rightly restricts the `10 * N` entry-growth check to devices whose 24-slot ring is not yet full (`valid_segments < 24`), while prompt 6.4's prose omits `whose ring is not full`. (b) `b_body` checks the five `## ` headings, `PRE-PR PASS at <head>`, checkboxes, Round Summary rows, and (in `final` mode) GitHub check runs, without asserting that `Fixes #215` or the `Coding agent:` first line under `## Pre-Implementation Verification` is present in the PR body.
- **Concrete fix:** Add `whose ring is not full` after `for each such device` in prompt line 688, and add regex checks for `(?m)^Fixes #215\s*$` and `Coding agent:` in `b_body`.
- **Disposition recommendation:** `accept-as-is` (external reviews and operator merge checklist cover `Fixes #215`).

---

###### L-6 (LOW) — `reviewb <PR#> answer` cleans `answer.md` with `rn.clean()` without running `rn.screen(..., answer=True)` or removing stale files on a later refusal

- **File and line:** `prompts/phase7/v7.7.1.3-checks.sh`, lines 1040–1045 and 1057, 1128–1133 (in `b_reviewb`).
- **Quoted text:**
  ```python
  answer = norm(open(os.path.join(outdir, "answer.md"), encoding="utf-8").read())
  if not answer.startswith(header + "\n") or not answer[len(header):].strip():
      print("ERROR answer.md does not begin with the header of turn3.txt, unchanged, followed by the review: send turn3.txt again, or run review B on the runner"); sys.exit()
  comment = rn.clean(answer) + "\n"
  ```
- **Why it matters:** In live `scripts/external-review.py` (line 360), the runner calls `screen(f"turn {turn}", visible, answer=True)` before `clean()` so that if the model invents an example command containing a login-shaped placeholder (which `system.txt` rule 8 forbids), the output is rejected before posting. Because `reviewb <PR#>` already screened all PR inputs before anything reached AI Studio, no real credential can be present in `answer.md`, so there is no secret-leak risk; however, screening `answer` prevents posting a hallucinated login-like placeholder into the PR comments. Also, if `reviewb <PR#>` succeeded on an earlier commit and a later run on a new commit hits a `Refused` / `key` error at lines 1128–1133, the older `system.txt` and `turn1.txt`–`turn3.txt` remain in `$SAVE/reviewb-$pr/`.
- **Concrete fix:** Call `rn.screen("answer.md", answer, answer=True)` (and `key.search`) before writing `comment.md` in `reviewb <PR#> answer`, and unlink existing `system.txt`/`turn*.txt` in `$SAVE/reviewb-$pr` at the start of `reviewb <PR#>`.
- **Disposition recommendation:** `accept-as-is` (`Before you merge` line 142 also checks `No review and no comment holds a login`).

Each turn: one Markdown report titled `R6 v7.7.1.3 re-audit, turn <1 | 2> — GPT Astra — <date>`. Under the title, a table: the model as your interface names it; the harness or environment; the context window; the reasoning effort; the tokens read, written and thinking; whether your context was compacted, and how often; whether the session stopped on a usage limit and was resumed; the commits that you read. Then: Verdict (PASS / CONDITIONAL PASS / FAIL); a table of the checks of the turn; CRITICAL, HIGH, MEDIUM / LOW findings (file, quoted text, why, concrete fix); a disposition recommendation per finding; a confidence statement ((a) each HIGH finding's reality, (b) the chance of a missed HIGH, (c) any check you could not run). Return each report as one Markdown file named `r6-astra-reaudit-turn<1 | 2>-<YYYY-MM-DD>.md`. If you cannot create a file, answer with the report alone, as Markdown, with nothing before or after it.

## §5 Constraints (binding)

1. No invented file contents, signatures or line numbers; a check you cannot run is `unable to verify`, not PASS.
2. No new functionality.
3. Quote verbatim when you claim a defect.
4. If a verdict depends on a check you could not run, say so.
5. Read section 2 of the audit file, and `astra/reports/`, only in turn 2.
6. Never write a login, key or token value, nor a placeholder in a login's form (such as a user and a password joined by a colon after `curl -u`), nor a quoted value after a password-like name: name the file and the line. Your reports go into R6's diff, and review B refuses such lines.
7. Read and write nothing outside the paths of §1 and the live sources; change nothing on GitHub.

##### Verdict

**FAIL. Hold dispatch.** The 24-hour gate accepted a board whose old and new writers had both stopped after hour two. Review B can read PR discussion that changed after screening, and its designated fallback cannot fit even the fixed part of the bundle within the pinned runner’s input limit. These are findings against the checks and handover instructions. They do not depend on a prediction about the firmware Codex will write.

The meta-read correction held. The unmodified H1 passed against a local control implementation using the pinned product types. Changing only the handling of the read error to start a fresh ring caused four assertions to fail. All 14 setup hashes matched. Both credential screens cleared all 14 bundle texts, including the audit file, and their projected product diffs. I processed the audit file as opaque input for hashing, screening and size measurement. I did not inspect its section 2, the earlier reports or the dispositions.

The isolated error-as-fresh mutation exited 1 on result, writes/meta, status and recovery. IDF v5.5 distinguishes a missing key and an insufficient buffer from other read errors. |

Against the earlier planning commit, 12 existing bundle files changed, the planner page was added and the Batch 2 assumption audit was byte-identical. The setup switched tar date and manifest, added the planner page and updated its repeated prompt/check hashes. The tar itself was optional and was not used.

For 7.1–7.6, F checks the new What’s Next heading, the snapshot preamble and header, and the C6 row prefix. For 7.2, F checks the numbered Done row and copy verification constrains the other plan edits to “You are here”. For 7.3–7.4, F checks the changelog names and the placement of both fixed architecture notes. For 7.5–7.6, F looks for selected log outputs, the seven record headings and five populated metric rows. F alone does not attest to every later board reading or each browser result. The final round must supply those records.

###### Board timelines and adversarial inspection

| Case | Result of the supplied logic |
|---|---|
| First upload and WAIT | The operator’s readings show .173 had no usable history partition before its USB flash, then reported v7.7.1.2 and 524,288 history bytes. That satisfies the partition prerequisite for taking the agent’s own pre-OTA baseline. On fixtures, unchanged boot failed; a newer boot with all devices pending returned WAIT, as did a first empty cycle before the deadline. All-pending at uptime 4,500 s failed. A later sample with qualifying writes and sufficient growth passed. The product YAML schedules points at quarter-hours and persistence at minute 10. |
| WROOM floor and 24 hours | 1,023 B failed in `dev` and `v2`, before and after upload, and in `wroom24`. At 86,410 s with matching boot and 1,024 B, `wroom24` passed. A same-boot reading with no progress since the first PASS failed. One early advance followed by a long stall passed (H01). |
| Firmware fix, rings partly filled | Both baseline modes accept v7.7.1.3. `dev before` replaces the heap/boot baseline for that upload, and `v2 before` erased the previous first-PASS file. Four rings still partial after writing required at least 28 more aggregate entries. A delta of 27 failed and 28 passed. Four rings going from 23 to 24, with zero growth, also passed (M01). |
| Firmware fix, rings already full | A new boot with successful overwrites, a newer old-engine epoch, and unchanged partition occupancy passed. This is the healthy full-ring case. A new .170 upload starts its 24-hour observation again. A later check must still show that both writers remained active. |
| Review sequence | A/C precede B. The operator starts B after their assessments and the 24-hour evidence. A firmware fix requires fresh device evidence under the existing review-round rule. The operational limit is keeping the body, comments and discussion read by B identical to those screened (H02). |

**§2.C(1), compliant-but-wrong.** I checked all eight signatures and their order against `SensorEntity::metric_states`, the history pointer, the generated persist IDs/counts, the key helpers, `DeviceHistoryMeta` and `DeviceSegment`. The host build used the product’s extracted structs, key functions and exporter, followed by S2, eight fixture definitions and T3. The HTTP handler can consume the previously declared status-copy helper. The live struct assertions give 60 bytes for meta and 228 for a segment. The modified contract supports a single writer and a reader taking one device copy at a time. It promises no snapshot across all devices.

**§2.C(2), strength of evidence.** H1 discriminates the requested error-as-fresh mutation. It is a fake NVS test. On a board, `used_entries` counts the entire partition and the per-device statuses come from RAM. A rising aggregate supports a new-key write, but cannot identify which device supplied the growth. IDF describes two overhead entries plus one per 32 bytes of blob data. A new 228-byte blob ordinarily accounts for ten entries on that basis. The seven-entry test is a tolerance on net growth. A full-ring overwrite need not increase the count. The new 24-hour comparison proves at most one advance after the first sample (H01).

**§2.C(4), deferred ambiguity.** I traced the USB preparation, the before/after OTA readings, WAIT, a late first PASS, a same-version reflash, and the wait before B. A different boot estimate establishes a restart. On a same-version reflash, the successful upload command and build record supply the additional evidence about which image booted. The day-of-dispatch fleet, future implementation and actual review rounds were outside this turn.

##### CRITICAL findings

None identified in the changed parts.

##### HIGH findings

###### H01 · The 24-hour PASS accepts writers that stopped early

**Evidence.** Checks lines 758–770 compare with the first PASS using `if up - pup >= 3600:`, `if a > b` and `if not last > plast:`. Prompt 6.4 requires “progress since that first PASS”.

**Consequence.** In the replay, the first PASS was at uptime 4,000 s. By hour two, each device’s count had advanced from one to two and the old epoch had advanced once. At uptime 86,410 s, both engines still reported those hour-two values. The actual block printed `v2 PASS`. It met the literal first-PASS comparison while accepting more than 20 hours of missing writes. If the first PASS is taken late, the one-hour condition can skip the comparison completely at the nominal 24-hour reading.

**Disposition: fix before dispatch.** Keep the first-PASS comparison. For the 24-hour reading, also check the old engine and at least three eligible device epochs against the most recent scheduled cycle that had fresh points. Use the epochs already returned by the endpoint and allow for the quarter-hour sampling interval. Where the data cannot distinguish a quiet sensor from a stopped writer, record insufficient evidence and take a fresh qualifying sample. Add the hour-two-stall and late-first-PASS cases to the rehearsal.

###### H02 · The review-B screen and the reviewer can receive different PR data

**Evidence.** The checks block screens the PR body and discussion, then at line 1224 tests only `if again["head"]["sha"] != head or again["base"]["sha"] != base:`. Part B tells the next session to read “the PR body, the list of changed files, the diff, the reviews, the review comments and the conversation comments.”

**Consequence.** A head SHA identifies repository files. A PR body or comment can change while that SHA stays the same. The reviewer independently fetches that mutable material after preparation. An otherwise compliant operator can therefore expose previously unscreened text to the external session. In a harmless replay, I changed only the discussion after preparation. The saved line still applied, and answer mode printed PASS. No private data was used or disclosed in the test. Google’s terms allow human access to interactions and describe continued service processing with telemetry disabled. [Google Antigravity Additional Terms, clauses 3–5](https://antigravity.google/terms)

**Disposition: fix before dispatch.** Keep the single operator line and the commit-pinned review file. Bind the body and discussion to the exact screened bytes, for example through a read-only snapshot identified in that line, and direct B to read that copy. A later discussion update needs a newly screened snapshot. Preserve the refusal rule for both venues. This fixes enforcement of the operator’s public-data decision.

###### H03 · The designated review-B fallback cannot fit the fixed R6 input

**Evidence.** Operator page step 8.5 says “If Antigravity cannot run it, run review B on the review runner instead”. Product `scripts/external-review.py` line 18 sets `LIMIT_CHARS = 600000`. Lines 321–325 count both wrapped input groups and refuse an oversized input.

**Consequence.** I reconstructed the canonical Git diff for just the 13 fixed bundle paths against the product pin. I excluded the editable plan of record, all implementation changes, the PR body, reviews and comments. The runner’s own diff filter and wrapper gave **648,127 characters** for the agent prompt, review file and that diff alone. Even counting only added/removed line payloads plus the two specification texts gave 619,501. Both credential screens cleared the material. The fallback therefore has a separate, deterministic size refusal when given the full evidence. The primary Antigravity path has no such demonstrated size failure.

**Disposition: fix the fallback before dispatch.** Rehearse a complete representative R6 payload through the runner’s actual size calculation. Remove duplicate transmission of already supplied text while retaining all review-relevant changes, or obtain a separately approved runner/input-budget correction. Keep full-input screening before any filtering. Until a complete input fits, describe the fallback as unavailable for R6.

| ID | File and verbatim evidence | Consequence and recommended disposition |
|---|---|---|
| M01 · Growth on the last free slot | Checks lines 692 and 752. The baseline saves `h.get("valid_segments")` for the old engine. The post-upload test selects devices with `(d.get("valid_segments") or 0) < p.get("slots", 24)`. | The write from 23 to 24 normally creates the final slot. All four such transitions passed with zero aggregate growth because all four devices were already full at observation. **Fix** by saving each observable pre-upload v2 ring count and counting devices known to have added slots since that baseline. Handle a first install, a previously full ring and an unavailable per-device baseline explicitly. Retain the seven-entry tolerance as aggregate corroboration. |

Job matching at line 1050 includes `.startswith(name + " (")`. | Omitting one criterion and duplicating another produced final PASS. Replacing the aggregator job with an unrelated seventh browser suffix also passed. The pinned predecessor’s actual names were `preflight-and-compile`, `prompt-lint`, `pr-body-check`, and `browser-tests (…)` for 1sensor, 2sensor, 3sensor, 4sensor, mixed, system and aggregator. **Fix** by comparing the 17 canonical criterion texts from §7 once each and the exact required job-name set, retaining the latest-run and success checks. [Product PR #246 browser jobs](https://github.com/GCV-Sleeper-Service/ESP32-GW-multi-sensor/actions/runs/45309927810) |

An older review can also satisfy a row naming a different commit. **Fix** by binding each row to the posted review comment and its reviewed commit. D line 550 searches `re.search(r"g_dev_persist_status\b", tail)`. | B requires the setter and rejects the raw array between S2 and T3, but never requires the getter in either writer path. D forbids direct reads only after the format-string anchor. It can miss a raw read added earlier in the same handler, or repeated getter calls while formatting a device. **Fix** by checking the getter in both status-update paths, scanning the whole changed handler for direct array access, and checking one local copy per emitted device. The fixed S2 lock itself is sound on inspection. |

The operator uses these diagnostics for troubleshooting. **Fix** by screening the normalized answer before echoing any input-derived data. On a bad PR/head, report the field, line and expected reference only. |
| M07 · F accepts an incomplete C3 snapshot and C6 decision | Checks line 812 tests the snapshot header. Line 814 searches only for “C6 test board .184” at the start of the decision text. Prompt 7.1 requires “its table these two rows, with your readings” and “First measure a build without the native `api:` for this board alone”. | I ran the changed F predicates with the correct header, no measurement rows and a C6 row ending immediately after the board identifier. They passed. The changelog check, in contrast, searches the R6 entry for all eight function names and both decision numbers. **Fix** by confining snapshot validation to its section, requiring numeric before/after rows for both metrics and comparing the fixed C6 row with the supplied text. |

##### Confidence and verification limits

| Question | Assessment |
|---|---|
| Reality of H01 | Reproduced using the supplied `v2` block. Confidence is high that stale writers can pass. The replay makes no claim that a particular board has stalled. |
| Reality of H02 | The read sequence and the same-head discussion-change replay establish the gap. Exposure depends on what changes and when the reviewer fetches it. No external review was started. |
| Reality of H03 | The fixed-path reconstruction alone exceeds the pinned runner limit, even with the editable plan omitted. GitHub can format a diff differently. A fallback that receives a truncated diff would still lack the complete evidence required for PASS. |
| Chance of a missed HIGH | Residual risk is concentrated in real device timing, NVS occupancy during overwrites and the eventual PR’s review data. Host mutation tests, actual check blocks and exact source hashes give stronger coverage for the meta-read and deterministic gate paths. |
| Unable to verify | Independent C3, S3 and WROOM builds under ESP-IDF 5.5, live board readings and OTA, execution of the future implementation, the operator’s dispatch shell, and an actual final R6 PR or external review. The latter items are pending by design. The FAIL verdict does not rely on any of them. |

The IDF references used for the read-error classes, blob storage accounting and critical-section semantics were the [v5.5 NVS guide](https://docs.espressif.com/projects/esp-idf/en/v5.5/esp32/api-reference/storage/nvs_flash.html) and the [v5.5 FreeRTOS guide](https://docs.espressif.com/projects/esp-idf/en/v5.5/esp32/api-reference/system/freertos_idf.html#critical-sections).
