##### Verdict

**FAIL. Hold dispatch. The turn-1 verdict stands.** The supplied 24-hour check passed when both writers had stopped after hour two. The final PR check passed with a missing acceptance criterion, a missing required browser job, or a scheduling note in place of Review B. Review B can read discussion that changed after screening. Section 2 also identifies an additional call into a helper that can erase the shared history partition. The planning session’s reason for dismissing that path does not hold on initialization failures.

The metadata read-error repair **does** hold. The unmodified H1 passed against the host control. Treating a read error as an absent key made four assertions fail, including the check that the next cycle resumed at the preserved slot. I would close D1 and retain its severity disagreement in the record. I would lower turn-1 H03, the fallback size refusal, to MEDIUM under PLAN-037 because it stops the fallback before transmission. These changes affect individual findings. The reproduced false passes independently support FAIL.

##### Checks of the 37 dispositions

I read §2 of the audit file, including the original prompt, session descriptions, dispositions, comparison and five reproduced reports. I treated A3 as the revision of A2 that it identifies itself to be. “Plan” below gives the planning session’s assessment of the **original** defect. Each judgment identifies what holds in the corrected bundle. A proposed increase for a closed defect is historical. The open findings and their recommended dispositions follow the table. PLAN-037 leaves disputed higher ratings to the operator. [Audit §2.3][dispositions] · [Original reports][reports] · [PLAN-037][decision]

**Source shorthand.** P is the [agent prompt][prompt], C the [executable checks][checks], R the [review file][review], O the [operator page][operator], and PC the [producer contract][contract]. Quoted fragments come from the named files at the pinned commits. Board and PR replays used the supplied checks with local responses.

| ID | Plan | Judgment, evidence and recommendation |
|---|---|---|
| D1 | Fixed · HIGH | **Agree with closure. Original CRITICAL is justified for v2-only history.** P 2.2 requires “it writes nothing” when the meta read returns −1. H1 checks retention, no writes for that device, the ERROR result and the correct retry slot, while allowing another device to continue. The old snapshot has `HistEntry temp` and `HistEntry hum`, while the product also persists `wan_ping`. Overwriting its slot can irreversibly lose stored samples. The other 23 slots lose their index in this scenario, which is a different consequence. No loss was observed in R6. Close the repair and correct the “no loss in R6” rationale. [Legacy snapshot][legacy] · [Device map][model] |
| D2 | Fixed · MEDIUM | **Accessor design fixed. Enforcement partial. Original HIGH.** P S2 declares `static portMUX_TYPE g_dev_persist_status_mux = portMUX_INITIALIZER_UNLOCKED;` and both accessors copy a single device record under this lock. A concurrent C++ read and write is a data race even when individual words are aligned. B requires `dev_persist_status_put_(` in each writer path. D searches only `tail` for raw access. Retain S2 and close the enforcement gap in M3 below. Independent target compiles: unable to verify. |
| D3 | Fixed · MEDIUM | **Agree with closure. Original HIGH.** In both `dev` and `v2`, C checks `[ "$mh" -ge 1024 ]` for .170 before saving the pre-upload reading. The replay rejected 1,023 B on both before and after paths and on `wroom24`. The latter accepted 1,024 B at 24 hours. The omitted pre-upload check could have authorized an upload below the operator’s floor. Keep the tests. |
| D4 | Fixed · MEDIUM | **Agree with closure. Original HIGH.** C uses `[ "$preboot" -lt $((boot - 30)) ]` and `if not boot0 < boot - 30:`. Both post-upload checks rejected the pre-upload boot in the replay. Previously, a rerun could accept old evidence for a build that never reached the board. Keep the new-boot checks together with the actual upload record. |

| D6 | Fixed · MEDIUM | **Agree.** C uses `first = up < 4500`. All devices pending produced WAIT at 400 s and a failed checkpoint at 4,500 s. A first cycle with no new points also produced WAIT early. P 6.4 distinguishes WAIT from PASS. Close the indefinite-wait finding. |
| D7 | Fixed · MEDIUM | **Partial. Keep MEDIUM open.** C tests `if growing and used - used0 < 7 * len(growing):` after classifying rings by their ending occupancy. Four partial rings needed 28 entries in the replay. Four rings growing from 23 to 24 needed zero. A ring full at the baseline can legitimately reuse a slot. Correct the classification in M2. The seven-entry margin is an engineering estimate pending board evidence. |

Three healthy devices cannot hide an unknown fourth result at PASS. The old block could certify a nonconforming device response. Keep the four-device check. |

The agent prompt names the block but omits the answer-mode requirements that cause it to stop, including C’s `if len(comment) > 65000:`. Make the operator-only specification explicit as proposed in M6. |
| D18 | Fixed · LOW | **Partial. LOW is fair.** C §2 now checks the 7.1 locations and “7.2 anchor: the row 9 of the ladder”. P §2 lists 6.1, 7.1, 7.2 and 7.4. A2 M2 also named the supplied `## 2. You are here` heading. C uses it later in `por_ok`, without checking it as a distinct §2 anchor. The supplied plan is hash-checked. Add the missing anchor or narrow the claim about checking every anchor. |
| D19 | Fixed · LOW | **Agree for the three named F checks.** C F searches `for x in $FUNCS DeviceSegment persist_v2 24 252` and both plan numbers, and requires `Decided` on the Q3–Q5 row. The `free` restriction applies to the call. M M5 also requested evidence of the subsequent WROOM run. The initial F legitimately precedes it. P 7.5 already requires the output. Verify the later evidence at final closeout under H2. |
| D20 | Fixed · LOW | **Agree on routing. Partial on verification. LOW is fair.** P 7.6 directs the agent to “route each new one to a row” and “name the row”. PC 8.7 supplies the destination. F verifies headings and metric rows, without matching a recorded recommendation to that destination. Close the missing instruction. Verify the named rows in final review as part of M6. |
| D21 | Fixed · LOW | **Agree.** P §2 names `BASH_ENV` and says “never ask for the login and never type it”. O requires `agent-check.sh` in the dispatch shell. I reconciled the 14 diagnostic lines with the operator instructions. The historical missing prerequisites are recorded. Dispatch-day results: unable to verify, pending by design. Close the documentation change. |
| D22 | Fixed · LOW | **Agree.** P 5.1 says the pipeline runs through header generation and “its last command, `bash scripts/preflight.sh`, runs in the PRE-PR gate”. That gives the omitted command and its actual place in the step. Close. |
| D23 | Fixed · LOW | **Agree for all three named labels.** P 7.1 supplies `Produce the v7.7.1.4 bundle, then R7`, uses the version observed before the last upload, and P 7.6 says “the S3 column is .173” with unmeasured metrics recorded as `N/A`. The step-specific directions resolve the generic template’s .191 example. A1 M04 also asked what happens to Review B if its own finding changes firmware. Preserve the new readings and identify the head B actually assessed (L3). |
| D24 | Fixed · LOW | **Agree.** P §1 item 11 now reads `/root/v7713/files/prompts/phase7/consolidated-audit-template-phase7.md` before TG0. Item 1 includes “the two rows of PR #246 and the row of the `.bin` size checkpoint”. R S7 refers to the recommendations “that item 7.1 names”. Close both corrections. |
| D25 | Fixed · LOW | **Agree.** The supplied architecture note in P 7.4 says “a `DeviceSegment` is 228 bytes” and cites the product `static_assert`. F checks placement of that fixed note. Close for the bundle. The product-document edit is a future R6 deliverable. |
| D26 | In part · LOW for R6 | **Agree on the wrap recovery question. Partial for the writer. MEDIUM remains.** The handoff now specifies testing “an interruption between the segment and the meta at the first fill and at the wrap”. A3 appropriately revised A2’s claim that a corrupt restore was established. P H1 nevertheless implements `g_ops.push_back("commit"); return ESP_OK;`. It cannot establish the writer’s behavior on failed commit. Keep the R7 recovery handoff and address the R6 evidence in M5. |
| D27 | No · LOW | **Disagree. Conditional HIGH.** P 2.2 mandates `open_history_nvs_(&handle, NVS_READWRITE)`. The product’s boot restore can return false and the old writer can return on `if (snapshot == nullptr) return;` or insufficient history before it opens NVS. The assumption that initialization has already succeeded does not hold on those paths. Reopen under H4. [Initialization and erase][init] · [Old hourly writer][hourly] |
| D28 | Fixed · LOW | **Agree.** PC 9.8 names the table `"Process metrics"` and `§4 of the template`. PC 7.13 now allows “the rounds of the step and the fixes of a later audit (PLAN-047)”. Both cross-reference and reuse rule agree with the corrected bundle. Close. |
| D29 | Known, no change · LOW | **Disagree with leaving the conflict. LOW is fair.** Setup step 7 starts `When done: gh pr ready` and step 8 starts `Write the PR body`. P orders the body update before `gh pr ready <PR#>`, and PC 7.13 orders body then ready. The setup accurately copies an inconsistent guide. Correct the guide and its copy together (L1). |
| D30 | No · LOW | **Disagree on literal contract compliance.** PC 6.5 says “an insertion of more than 10 lines” goes as a supplied file through `copy`. P embeds S2, T3 and H1 in fences. C uses `insert` for S2 and T3 and `host` extracts and hashes H1. Fixed bytes and positive checks are real. Prior use in R4 explains the practice. LOW for the delivery inconsistency. Record the narrow exception or align the packaging (L2). |
| D31 | Accepted · LOW | **Agree with acceptance as a test limitation.** H1 has `PERSIST_DEVICE_COUNT = 7` and `NUM_DEVICES = 6`, deliberately covering missing and colliding cases in a different order. The product has four persistent IDs. The actual .173 `/api/storage-stats` check is the intended integration evidence for the handler and scheduled call. Keep the host cases and require the planned board result. It is pending. |
| D32 | No · LOW | **Agree for the pinned inputs.** The renderer’s `if old == content:` returns before the write. The status fixture derives from role and ID, the aggregator header from aggregator and board configuration, and the sensor fixture from `fixture_manifest(sensors)`. The producer reports unchanged outputs in rehearsal. Keep the actual changed-path check. Independent reproduction of the whole generation pipeline: unable to verify. [Renderer][renderer] |
| D33 | No · unrated | **Agree.** T3 contains both `dev_ring_valid_(24, 24) == 24` and `dev_ring_valid_(30, 24) == 24`. These cover the cap at and above capacity. Close as a false finding. |
| D34 | No · unrated | **Agree.** C `copy` takes `n=$((n + 1)); continue` for a matching destination and has a separate `por_ok` branch for the permitted plan edit. The repeated copy and verify replay passed for all 12 paths. Close as a false finding for the claimed rerun. |
| D35 | In part, no change · LOW | **Agree with qualification.** C overwrites the saved `dev` reading. A second pre-upload reading on the running post-upload boot invalidates that baseline, and the strict `preboot` comparison then refuses. P 6.5 requires “the verbatim output of every `dev`, `v2` and `image` block” in both records. A fresh baseline is necessary on an authorized reflash. Accept LOW and use the recorded pre-upload reading to diagnose an accidental repeat. |
| D36 | In part, no change · LOW | **“In part” is fair. The stated reboot guarantee is too strong. MEDIUM.** C uses `if abs(boot - pboot) > 120:` and `wroom24` accepts a caller-supplied epoch within 120 s. In a replay the board was observed at +90 s, then restarted at +110 s. The first and late `v2` and `wroom24` all passed after 24 hours on the second boot. A 121 s mismatch failed. Correct the guarantee in M7. |
| D37 | Yes for fallback, deferred · LOW | **Agree about the difference. Disagree with LOW and unrestricted deferral.** The runner’s `screen` uses `if login_literal(line):`; the fallback later uses `comment = clean(header + "\n\n" + answers[2])` and posts it. The bundle’s additional key screen does not inspect that returned answer. O 8.5 instructs the operator to clear `dry_run`. Treat that boundary as HIGH if the route becomes usable. Keep the deterministic size refusal separately under M1. |

##### CRITICAL findings

No open CRITICAL defect established in the changed bundle. The conditional loss described under D1 concerns the superseded contract. D27 identifies a reachable request to erase shared storage. The evidence does not establish the physical partition state, recoverability of its contents, or an actual data loss.

##### HIGH findings

| Finding | File and verbatim evidence | Consequence, evidence and recommended disposition |
|---|---|---|
| H1 · D5 · A stalled writer passes at 24 hours | C `v2` compares with “the first v2 PASS of this boot” and tests `if up - pup >= 3600:` followed by `if not last > plast:`. The device test is `if a > b`. | First PASS was at uptime 4,000 s, after the first hourly write. The old-engine epoch and all device counters advanced once more at hour two. At uptime 86,410 s, with no further progress, `v2 PASS` was returned. An unchanged first-hour record failed as intended. A late first PASS also shortens or skips the comparison. **Fix before dispatch.** At the 24-hour reading, use existing epochs and counters to establish old-engine and per-device progress in a recent eligible cycle. Allow for fresh-sample availability and the scheduled write time. Where the readings cannot distinguish a quiet device from a stalled writer, obtain another qualifying observation before PASS. Rehearse both the hour-two stall and late-first-PASS cases. |
| H2 · D9 · Final PASS accepts substituted evidence | C `body` uses `len(boxes) < criteria`, `.startswith("## Review B")` and `startswith(name + " (")`. P §8 says “a filled row needs that comment”. | Three separate replays returned `body final PASS`: one duplicated criterion replaced an omitted one, an unrelated seventh browser job replaced `aggregator`, and a scheduling note stood in for B. The healthy control passed. The gate rejected an explicitly skipped job and a missing B comment. **Fix before dispatch.** Match the 17 distinct §7 criteria exactly once, require the seven named browser variants plus the three fixed jobs, and match each filled review row to its actual posted review and reviewed commit. Accept operator-posted reviews and earlier reviewed heads when recorded accurately. At final closeout verify that the 24-hour outputs required by 6.5 are in the records. This raises the combined turn-1 M02/M03 issue to HIGH because the operator explicitly relies on final PASS for merge. |
| H3 · D10–D12, D14–D15, D37 · Review B can use different data than the operator screened | C checks `if again["head"]["sha"] != head or again["base"]["sha"] != base:`. R Part B asks for “the reviews, the review comments and the conversation” as well as the code. The fallback uses `if login_literal(line):` and `request(comment_url, token, payload={"body": comment})`. | The code diff and supplied files are pinned. A body edit or discussion update can occur after preparation with neither SHA changing. I changed a harmless public comment in the fixture at the same head. The handoff still applied and answer mode passed. The fallback separately fetches its inputs and posts a cleaned answer without the block’s additional key check. D11’s detector also has narrower coverage than its comment claims. **Fix before relying on either route.** Keep the one-line handoff, identify an immutable copy of the screened PR body and discussion in it, and direct B to those bytes. Re-screen when material changes. Give both venues the same checks for the actual outgoing material and final publication text. If runner work is outside R6, mark that route unavailable until those controls hold. No exposure was observed. |
| H4 · D27 · An additional caller can reach the shared-partition erase path | P 2.2 requires `open_history_nvs_(&handle, NVS_READWRITE)`. In the product, restore can `return false` on open failure, the hourly writer has `if (snapshot == nullptr) return;`, and the common initialization helper calls `nvs_flash_erase_partition(HISTORY_PARTITION_LABEL)`. | Ordinarily previous initialization succeeds. After unsuccessful boot initialization the old hourly writer can also skip its open, including when the snapshot has insufficient data. The new writer then invokes initialization on its own. I compiled the pinned initialization helper with in-memory return codes and an erase-call counter. After an unsuccessful first initialization and a skipped old open, each of the two specified recovery errors independently led to one erase request. The flash driver was stubbed. **Reopen before dispatch.** Make the v2-only call fail and record ERROR without requesting erase when prior safe initialization cannot be established. Leave the legacy recovery behavior intact. Add this sequence and the already-initialized control to the producer’s rehearsal. [Product helper][init] · [Old hourly writer][hourly] |

##### MEDIUM findings

| Finding | File and verbatim evidence | Recommended disposition |
|---|---|---|
| M1 · The fallback exceeds its input limit and lacks two reporting fields | O 8.5 says “run review B on the review runner instead”. The runner sets `LIMIT_CHARS = 600000` and refuses at `if size > LIMIT_CHARS:`. The guide requires “the context window” and “whether the context was compacted”. The runner header has `Model version` and `Tokens`, with neither of those two fields. | The runner’s own filter and wrapper gave **648,127 characters** for 13 fixed paths, the prompt and review file alone. A lower bound using only added/removed lines plus both specification texts was **619,501**. The editable plan, implementation and discussion were excluded. **Reclassify turn-1 H03 to MEDIUM, retain turn-1 M04, fix before advertising the fallback.** Rehearse its real budget with the complete required evidence, remove duplication while keeping full screening, and specify how its header records unavailable fields. Until it fits, give the operator an accurate stop instruction. [Runner][runner] · [Process guide][guide] |
| M2 · D7 · A ring filling its last slot escapes the growth check | C classifies a ring using `(d.get("valid_segments") or 0) < p.get("slots", 24)` at the end of the interval. | The replay passed four transitions from 23 to 24 slots with zero growth. Four rings ending at 23 needed 28 entries. Base the exception on the starting occupancy and on whether the interval included allocation. Preserve the full-to-full overwrite case. Rehearse partial, last-slot and already-full baselines. |
| M3 · D2 · Gates do not enforce one synchronized copy per device | P 4.1 requires “one copy of each device's status”. C B tests `"dev_persist_status_put_(" not in p`, while D searches `re.search(r"g_dev_persist_status\b", tail)`. | B permits an update built without loading the current record. D can miss a raw read earlier in the changed handler or multiple getter calls producing one response. Check both writer paths for read/modify/publish through the supplied accessors, scan the complete changed handler for raw access, and verify one snapshot per emitted device. Keep allocation, I/O and formatting outside the lock. |
| M4 · D10 · Error output precedes screening | C interpolates `table["Head reviewed"][:40]` and `table["Pull request"][:20]` before `rn.screen("answer", answer, answer=True)`. | A malformed field can be copied into a diagnostic the operator is instructed to relay. The usual correctly formed answer is screened. Move screening ahead of diagnostics that use supplied text. On a mismatch, identify the field, line and expected public reference. Screen the cleaned comment just before publication as part of H3. |
| M5 · D26 · H1 cannot distinguish a committed update from a failed commit | P 2.2 specifies “after a written segment, and only then, the meta”. Its H1 commit stub ends `g_ops.push_back("commit"); return ESP_OK;`. The handoff says the next step “tests an interruption between the segment and the meta”. | Two ordered set calls in an immediately updated fake store cannot establish what a reader could recover after interrupted persistence. **Accept** the R7 restore-policy handoff. For R6, state the writer’s promised result on commit failure. Exercise it with staged writes, a failed commit and reopening of the simulated store. Report any power-cut behavior outside the evidence. A corrupt restore on IDF 5.5 was not established. [Handoff][handoff] · [ESP-IDF NVS guide][nvs] |
| M6 · D17, D19, D20, D23 · The document checks cover only part of the evidence they claim | P CHECKPOINT F says the documents “hold what they must”. P 7.1 requires “its table these two rows, with your readings”. C searches for the header and the prefix `C6 test board .184`. PC 8.1 requires a prompt sentence for each assertion. | The changed F predicates passed with the header alone and a truncated C6 row. Validate both named numeric measurement rows in their section and the complete fixed C6 decision. Check later evidence and named recommendation destinations when the final records exist. Give review-round item 6 an explicit cross-reference to the operator-only `reviewb` modes, mandatory metadata, refusal and length rules. This carries forward turn-1 M07. |
| M7 · D36 · The reboot claim exceeds the timestamp evidence | P 6.5 specifies “started within 120 s of that epoch”. C rejects `abs(boot - pboot) > 120`. The disposition says the checks “stop on a reboot”. | The +110 s restart replay passed after a full day on the replacement boot. The elapsed-day condition held. Continuous operation since the observed post-upload boot remained unproved. Read the expected epoch from the saved post-upload observation, distinguish clock tolerance from boot identity, and use available independent restart evidence or record the interval as indeterminate. Keep the 86,400 s uptime requirement. |

##### LOW findings

| Finding | File and verbatim evidence | Recommended disposition |
|---|---|---|
| L1 · D29 · Setup orders readiness before the body | Setup has `7) When done: gh pr ready` and `8) Write the PR body`. PC 7.13 says “the agent edits the PR body” before it “runs `gh pr ready`”. | Align the guide’s dispatch block and its setup copy with PC and P. The PR should already contain the intended body when marking it ready starts review. [Setup][setup] |
| L2 · D30 · Fixed-text delivery differs from the contract | PC 6.5 says “the check file copies it (`copy`) after a hash check”. P says `... insert S2` and `... insert T3`. C `host` extracts `"Supplied text H1 — "`. PC 5.2 says “MEDIUM prescribes only interfaces and the tests that encode them”. | Preserve all three successful hash checks. Reconcile the long-insertion rule with the actual fence mechanism, and record PLAN-047’s reason for the fixed accessor bodies in S2. H1 is compiled through `host`. The pending gate record should describe that accurately. |
| L3 · D18, D23 · Two small documentation gaps | C §2 identifies “the row 9 of the ladder” and later relies on `## 2. You are here`. R places B after “the 24-hour readings are in”. | Add the missing pre-edit heading check for D18. For D23, identify the replacement measurements and reviewed head if B’s own fix triggers a new TG6. Where B’s conclusion depended on superseded readings, return those items to B or have the operator record how final sign-off was obtained. |
| L4 · Turn-1 L01/L02 · Two revision labels still conflict | The audit template header says `v1.7` and its changelog says `v1.8`. The plan says “Review B, three turns, one comment”. R says “Part B — one session, three passes”. | Correct the template version. Distinguish the primary venue’s single request and three passes from the runner’s three API turns in the plan diagram. [Audit template][template] · [Plan][plan] |

##### Confidence and limits

| Question | Assessment |
|---|---|
| Reality of H1 | High. The supplied gate returned PASS for stale hour-two data at 24 hours. Its unchanged-counters control returned failure. Actual behavior of future firmware is a separate question. |
| Reality of H2 | High. Three independent substitutions passed the real final block using local PR responses. Strict-success and closing-line checks also rejected their negative controls. |
| Reality of H3 | High for the difference between the screened and subsequently fetched inputs, and for the fallback’s different output checks. The same-head replay used harmless data. Actual exposure depends on the later content, timing and venue. |
| Reality of H4 | High for the conditional source path and for the erase request under the injected return codes. Its frequency and the state of useful flash contents on a real board are unknown. This is the least certain HIGH in terms of field impact. |
| Chance of a missed HIGH | A missed HIGH remains plausible in NVS failure sequencing, boot-time behavior, or the interaction between the eventual PR and its live review. The most thoroughly exercised cases are the meta-read mutation, deterministic gate outcomes, and fixed input sizes. The five reports do not represent five independent executable validations. |
| Checks unable to verify | Independent C3, S3 and WROOM target builds, physical OTA, power interruption and NVS page allocation on a board, the complete renderer rehearsal, the future R6 PR and external review, and dispatch-day operator and agent readings. The producer reports target builds. Future records, the gate record and dispatch paragraph are pending by design. FAIL does not depend on those checks. |

### 3.3 Dispositions

#### Turn 1: dispositions

The planning session checked each finding of turn 1 against the bundle at `e8c9afd` and the product at `a33a5cc`, then fixed the bundle. Ratings by PLAN-037; where the auditor rated higher, the operator decides, and every finding below is fixed.

| ID | Finding | Holds | Rating | Disposition |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| R1-H01 | The 24-hour `v2` passed writers that stopped after hour two: it compared only with the first PASS | Yes: one advance after the first PASS met the test; a first PASS taken late skipped it | HIGH | Fixed: every `v2` after the upload also needs the newest persisted point of the old engine and of at least three devices to be at most 5,400 s old at the reading (a write in the last cycle at minute 10, of a point at most 25 minutes older); a writer that stopped, or a quiet sensor, stops the block; the first-PASS comparison stays; part-2 cases for the stall after hour two, a stall of the device writers only and a late first PASS |
| R1-H02 | Review B could read PR text and discussion that changed after the screen, at the same head | Yes; the data is public GitHub text, so the exposure is to Google's reviewers of a text that anyone can read | MEDIUM | Fixed: `reviewb <PR#> answer` reads the PR text and discussion again, screens them, refuses on a hit (nothing posted) and says when they changed; the block fails when the discussion changes while it reads; the operator starts review B right after the line, and nobody posts on the PR until the answer is checked |
| R1-H03 | The runner fallback cannot take R6's PR: about 648,000 characters of fixed input against its 600,000 | Yes; the planning session's own figure of 557,500 had measured an older, smaller audit file | HIGH | Fixed by the operator's decision of 2026-11-25 (PLAN-047): R6's fallback is Gemini 4 Neon in another surface of the AI Pro subscription, with the same line, checks and public-data rule; the runner is named unavailable for R6 in the review file, the operator page, the prompt, the process guide (its input limit) and the plan of record |
| R1-M01 | A ring that filled its last slot in the cycle counted as full, so its new key needed no growth | Yes | MEDIUM | Fixed: `v2 before` keeps each device's ring when the board reports it (a rerun on v7.7.1.3), `-1` when not known; a ring that is full now and was not full before counts as growing; part-2 cases with 0 and 28 new entries |
| R1-M02 | `body final` counted criteria and browser jobs, so a duplicate or another job name could stand in | Yes | MEDIUM | Fixed: each criterion of §7, as the prompt writes it, once and ticked (evidence may follow it); the ten required jobs by their exact names (the seven browser jobs of `.github/workflows/browser-tests.yml`: 3sensor, 1sensor, 2sensor, 4sensor, mixed, aggregator, system; read on PR #246's head); §8 says both |
| R1-M03 | A comment that only began with `## Review B` stood for a review | Yes | MEDIUM | Fixed: a review comment begins `## Review X -` (or a dash or colon) and names the commit of its row; part-2 cases for a scheduling note, a heading without a commit and another commit |
| R1-M04 | The runner's header has no rows for the context window and compaction, while the process guide asks every review for them | Yes | LOW | Fixed: the process guide states the runner's own header and that those two fields count as `not shown`; R6 does not use the runner |
| R1-M05 | B did not require the getter in the writers; D checked only the tail of the handler | Yes | MEDIUM | Fixed: B requires `dev_persist_status_get_(` in both writers; D scans the whole handler for the array and needs one getter call; three negative tests |
| R1-M06 | A bad header field was echoed before the answer was screened | Yes | MEDIUM | Fixed: the answer is screened first; a bad field is named, never echoed; part-2 case with a login in the header |
| R1-M07 | F accepted the snapshot without its rows and a C6 row cut short | Yes | LOW | Fixed: 7.1 gives the form of the two rows (`<MEASURED>` values) and F checks both; F compares the C6 row with the line of the prompt; two negative tests |
| R1-L01 | The audit template's header says v1.7 | Yes | LOW | Fixed: v1.8 |
| R1-L02 | The plan's diagram says "Review B, three turns, one comment" | Yes | LOW | Fixed: one line and three passes in Antigravity, three turns on the runner |

R2, the status copy: the auditor could not repeat the three target builds; the planning session built C3, S3 and WROOM with S2 on 2026-11-25 (rehearsal part 1). ##### Verdict

**FAIL. Hold dispatch.** The new NVS open avoids the erase-capable recovery path, all 14 files match the manifest, and prompt lint passes. Seven HIGH findings remain in the acceptance and review gates. Among them, the board gate accepted a missed hourly write, the final PR gate accepted missing 24-hour evidence, and the answer check approved a PR discussion that changed during its read. These results came from executing the supplied checks on controlled inputs. They do not depend on access to a live board or a future PR.

##### Turn 1 checks

| Check | Result | Evidence |
|---|---|---|
| S1. Open without erase | Contract passes. Enforcement partial. | Live boot restore calls `open_history_nvs_`, which calls `ensure_history_nvs_ready_`. The latter can erase on two initialization errors. Prompt 2.2 instead specifies the direct read/write open with the live partition and namespace constants. B requires that call and bars both helpers and partition initialization from the new persistence code. H1 checks all three open arguments. Its fake returned success when enabled and an error when disabled. Its assertions sample only two of seven devices on failure. M01. [ESP-IDF NVS][nvs] |
| S2. Commit | Contract passes. Recovery test partial. | Items 2.2.7–8 and the following status paragraph require an error and the pre-write metadata in RAM if saving the metadata fails. H1 checks all three RAM metadata fields after the failed commit. The fake makes the new metadata visible before commit, and the retry assertion accepts several incorrect outcomes. M02. [ESP-IDF NVS][nvs] |
| S3. Board blocks | **FAIL** | First-cycle wait, normal growth, stalled counters, late first PASS, mixed ring sizes and the 30-second boundary were exercised. The current time, boot and ring checks admit both false passes and a false stop. H01, H02, M03, L02. |
| S4. PR body | **FAIL** | A correctly formed final body with all 17 criteria and evidence on the criterion lines passed. Removing or duplicating a criterion, omitting a named job, skipping a required job, omitting a posted review, or removing the closing line failed. Seven browser job names agree with the live workflow matrix. Two incorrect bodies still passed. H03, H04. |
| S5. Review B | **FAIL** | The exact key screen and the product runner’s login screen cleared all 14 bundle texts, including the 356,517-byte audit file. Both screens also cleared these texts as added diff lines. A rejected answer header produced a location without echoing its contents. With simulated read-only GitHub responses, preparation detected a mid-read edit. Answer mode detected a pre-existing clean edit and refused a screened discussion, but missed a mid-read edit. H05. The runner’s limit is 600,000 **characters** across its wrapped inputs. The R6-specific fallback is recorded in PLAN-047. M05. |
| S6. D, F and §2 | **FAIL** | D counted an added per-device NVS helper and allowed a line comment containing `new`. Its other handler checks missed defects or rejected valid code. F accepted the specified snapshot rows, and the prompt supplies exactly one C6 row for its fixed-line comparison. F and §2 also accepted malformed inputs. H06, H07, M04, L01. |
| S7. PLAN-047 texts | **FAIL** | Process guide §2.2 block A, contract 7.13 and setup steps 7–8 put the PR body before `gh pr ready`. The plan, decision, guide §2.4, operator step 8 and review file give the same R6 fallback. Operator step 7 puts the 24-hour readings before Review B. The failure table still directs the operator to the unavailable runner. M05. |

Ten of the fourteen bundle files differ from the comparison commit. The setup file also differs. The planner page, consolidated record template, batch assumption audit and R6 handoff are byte-identical. For the audit file, byte comparison, hashing and automated screening produced no excerpts from the withheld section. I used the original §2.C instructions embedded in the permitted part of the audit file. Section 3, the deferred disposition rows and the reports directory were excluded from the substantive review.

The PLAN-047 text check also covered contract 5.3 (audit lanes), 6.5 (fixed supplied texts), 7.13 (review rounds), 8.3 (review-file instructions) and 9.8 (hand-over). The planner page and audit template v1.8 retain the independent audit lanes. The handoff still prescribes the short status-copy critical section and preserves the distinction between a failed metadata read and a fresh ring. These passages introduce no competing R6-specific instruction. The process guide qualifies the general runner fallback by capacity. The record template and generic venue lists should defer to the step’s review file, especially when an operator must record use of the alternate subscription surface. [Contract][contract] · [Process guide][guide] · [Decision][decision]

##### CRITICAL findings

None identified.

##### HIGH findings

###### H01. A recent point does not establish a write in the required cycle or after the upload

**Evidence.** The check uses `old_fresh = now - (last or 0) <= FRESH` and tests the old epoch against the **pre-upload reading** with `if not last > last0:`. Prompt 6.4 concludes “so both engines wrote in the last cycle”. [Check lines 745–789][v2] · [Prompt item 6.4][boardprompt]

**Observed.** At 00:11, a 23:00 point is 4,260 seconds old. The 00:10 write had been missed. Both engines had progressed since the first PASS earlier in the run, so the gate returned PASS. In a separate first-upload fixture the legacy writer advanced after the baseline was captured, during the compile, then did nothing after reboot. Its last point was 1,800 seconds **before** the new boot. The first board gate still passed. A future timestamp also passed the one-sided age test.

**Disposition.** Fix before dispatch. Keep the age limit as a sanity check, reject future epochs outside a defined clock tolerance, and use a pair of observations spanning the scheduled persist to demonstrate the required write. For the first upload, establish legacy progress from an observation on the new boot. A healthy writer with insufficient evidence can WAIT.

###### H02. A late first PASS lets the 24-hour check pass without any subsequent progress

**Evidence.** The comparison with the first PASS runs only under `if up - pup >= 3600:`. The block otherwise falls through to `print("PASS")`. Prompt 6.5 says the two checks “check the progress since the first `v2 PASS` of 6.4”. [Check lines 776–792][v2] · [Prompt item 6.5][boardprompt]

**Observed.** A first PASS at uptime 85,200 seconds and a second reading at 86,460 seconds passed with identical per-device success counts and an identical legacy epoch. The WROOM’s 24-hour uptime can be genuine while this second piece of evidence is still outstanding. The same fall-through can establish a *first* baseline during the final check if its saved record is missing.

**Disposition.** Fix before dispatch. Give the 6.5 invocation an explicit progress requirement. Require the first-PASS record from this upload and at least one subsequent eligible cycle. Return WAIT until that cycle can be observed, then require legacy advancement and increased counts for at least three devices. Keep the ordinary first-PASS path for 6.1 and 6.4.

###### H03. A sentence reporting missing 24-hour evidence satisfies the final gate

**Evidence.** The required text is `EVIDENCE="wroom24 PASS"`, and the test is `if x not in body:`. The prompt requires “the line `wroom24 PASS` of 6.5”. [Body check lines 1056–1061][body] · [Prompt §8][bodyprompt]

**Observed.** Removing the output line failed. Replacing it with the sentence “The requested wroom24 PASS is still outstanding.” made the same final body pass. An agent could consequently post a green merge-gate comment while its PR explicitly says the observation is missing.

**Disposition.** Fix before dispatch. Require the exact output line in the 6.5 device-evidence block. Match it alongside the WROOM reading for that run. Retain the existing final-line check for `Fixes #215`, which rejected the missing closer.

###### H04. A review can be credited to a commit other than the one its header says it reviewed

**Evidence.** `named = [h for t in reviews_of(r) for h in re.findall(r"\b[0-9a-f]{7,40}\b", t)]` collects hashes from the entire comment. The review file requires the full commit in its “Head reviewed” field. [Body check lines 1068–1089][body] · [Review header rule][header]

**Observed.** A valid Review A heading and a header naming another commit passed when the review mentioned the row’s commit in its findings as a prior change. Removing that incidental mention made it fail. The problem also applies to reviews that cite fixes or earlier reviews in normal use.

**Disposition.** Fix before dispatch. Read the unique `Head reviewed` field from the review’s opening table and validate the full SHA there. If the PR table allows abbreviations, compare its unambiguous abbreviation to that field only. Apply the same heading grammar to the posted review and to Review B’s pre-post answer check.

###### H05. Review B answer mode can publish a candidate based on a changing discussion

**Evidence.** Answer mode reads `data = ks.discussion(gh, pages, rn, repo, pr, pull)`, screens that single collection, then writes `comment.md`. Preparation mode performs a second collection and compares digests. [Answer mode lines 1221–1233][answer] · [Preparation lines 1290–1304][prep]

**Observed.** Both modes saw the same simulated ordinary PR-body edit while they fetched reviews. Preparation refused. Answer mode fetched the PR once, printed PASS and wrote a comment, without reporting the edit. A clean change completed *before* answer mode was correctly reported. A screened value already present at read time was correctly refused. A refused repeat of answer mode also left the previous successful `comment.md` available at the posting path.

**Disposition.** Fix before dispatch. Invalidate the old candidate when answer checking begins. Collect the PR text and discussion twice, require a stable head/base and digest across the two collections, and only then publish the screened candidate. Preserve the existing distinction between a clean change since preparation and a change during validation. This reduces the observable race. Live GitHub discussion cannot be frozen by a commit SHA.

###### H06. D can certify four devices’ status while the handler reads only device zero

**Evidence.** D uses `if fn.count("dev_persist_status_get_(") != 1:`. Item 4.1 requires “one copy of each device's status, `dev_persist_status_get_(i)`”. [D lines 551–560][handlercheck] · [Prompt item 4.1][handlerprompt]

**Observed.** The unmodified D validator accepted a handler fixture that emitted the right four IDs and keys, but read index zero in every iteration. Distinct device failures or stale rings could then be reported as the first device’s success. H1 exercises the writer, while the board gate consumes the JSON produced by this handler.

**Disposition.** Fix before dispatch. Validate the copy against the current persist index in the device loop. Exercise the handler with deliberately different results, counters and cursors for each device and check the output row by row.

###### H07. D still accepts an added NVS recovery call in the HTTP handler

**Evidence.** D compares `len(re.findall(NVSCALL, fn))` with the baseline count. Its list includes the new write helpers but omits the live recovery helper `ensure_history_nvs_ready_`. That helper contains `nvs_flash_erase_partition(HISTORY_PARTITION_LABEL)` on two initialization-error paths. [D lines 534–539][handlercheck] · [Product recovery helper][recovery]

**Observed.** Against the pinned handler, D’s Python validator rejected an added `persist_all_devices_v2` call and accepted an added direct call to the recovery helper before the JSON formatting. The original handler already has NVS calls. Item 4.1 requires the added reporting work to use RAM. An extra recovery call can perform flash work on the HTTP task. Erasure depends on the driver error. No hardware erase was attempted or observed.

**Disposition.** Fix before dispatch. Preserve the original handler outside the one format-string change and the designated insertion after its print. Inspect the inserted code for direct NVS and NVS-backed helper calls, including recovery, using the live helper definitions. Add a negative fixture for this case.

##### MEDIUM and LOW findings

| ID | File and verbatim evidence | Consequence and disposition |
|---|---|---|
| M01 | H1 checks `g_dev_persist_status[0].writes_failed == failed_before + 1` and `g_dev_persist_status[2].last_result == DEV_PERSIST_ERROR`. [H1 lines 681–688][h1open] | The no-open case promises an error and one increment for **every** device. Only two results and one counter are tested. Snapshot all seven statuses and check each result and increment, along with the existing zero-write check. Exercise both documented classes of open failure through the same contract. Fix in H1. |
| M02 | H1 writes directly into `g_blobs[key]`; the retry assertion is `g_dev_persist_status[1].last_result != DEV_PERSIST_ERROR`. [Fake NVS][fake] · [H1 lines 597–610][h1commit] | The compiled fake exposed the new metadata after a failed commit and reopen. A retry returning MISSING, EMPTY or NVS_LOW could satisfy the assertion while the map still contains an internally consistent cursor. Test the expected DEDUP for already-visible metadata, and OK at the original slot when old metadata remains visible. Assert the epoch, cursor, counters and published RAM metadata in each case. State both legal recovery outcomes in 2.2. ESP-IDF allows writes before commit and gives no rollback guarantee. [NVS contract][nvs] |
| M03 | `pv[i].get("result") not in (None, "pending")` and `used - used0 < 7 * len(growing)`. [Before and growth checks][v2before] | EMPTY may occur before any metadata was loaded on this boot. Its zero RAM count is then unknown. With three full rings and the fourth recorded this way, a healthy overwrite stopped for insufficient growth. A second fixture had 23 valid segments but the last slot’s blob already allocated after a failed metadata update. The successful retry brought the count to 24 with zero new entries and also stopped. Mark unobserved counts unknown. Use cursor, epoch and count progress for retries and overwrites. Require entry growth only where a new physical key is established. |
| M04 | D uses `re.sub(r"//[^\n]*", "", tail)` before its allocation check. [D lines 543–561][handlercheck] | A harmless block comment containing “new” failed. A real allocation added earlier in the handler passed. Strip both C++ comment forms while preserving string literals, and check all additions to the handler. Pin the permitted edit region as in H07. |
| M05 | Operator failure table says “Or run review B on the runner (step 8.5).” Step 8.5 itself says “The review runner cannot take this PR”. The record template still lists “or the review runner, or Muse on a step without review C”. [Operator procedure][operator] · [Failure table][failure] · [Record template][record] | The person following the failure path is directed to a venue PLAN-047 ruled out for R6. Correct that row, retain the same-session remedy for missing fields and the new-session remedy for a wrong head, and name the same-model subscription fallback. Make the generic record/template wording refer to the feasible fallback named by the step. Keep the refusal path a stop on every surface. |
| L01 | F uses `[0-9][0-9,]*` and `[+-]?`; §2 uses `grep -cx '## 2. You are here'`. [F lines 829–838][fcheck] · [§2 check][sec2] | F accepted a doubled comma and an unsigned positive delta that item 7.1 requires to be signed. The heading check accepted `## 2X You are here`; the supplied plan itself has the correct heading. Require the specified numeric form and use a fixed-string whole-line match for the heading. |
| L02 | Item 6.4 says “A writer that stopped, or a sensor that is quiet, stops the block”. The live buffer defines `void add_gap(uint32_t epoch) { add(epoch, NAN); }`. [Prompt 6.4][boardprompt] · [Product buffer][gap] · [Product exporter][export] | A quiet sensor can still generate newly timestamped gap entries. Also, the written threshold allows three of four devices to qualify. The three-writer/one-quiet fixture passed. Describe PASS as evidence of persistence, including gaps. Attribute a failure to inadequate qualifying progress without diagnosing radio silence from these fields. |

##### Adversarial inspection of the changed parts

| Original item | What was inspected and what happened |
|---|---|
| §2.C(1). Compliant interfaces | Traced the eight declarations against the live device and metadata types, the NVS/key helpers, S2’s accessors, the hourly lambda in the C3 YAML and its renderer, and the web-handler consumer. H1 passed a GNU C++20 syntax check with the pinned types and eight declared interfaces. Production constants agree with the fake open. S2 protects one status copy per critical section, leaving flash operations, formatting and yielding outside it. No declaration-order conflict found in the changed pieces. Distinct-device reporting still needs H06. [FreeRTOS critical sections][rtos] |
| §2.C(2). Does the evidence establish the claim? | First upload with no cycle or no new point returned WAIT, followed by PASS with fresh writes. Stalling after hour two failed at 24 hours with a point 79,200 seconds old. Mixed partial, last-slot and full rings passed at the ordinary growth threshold, and a known last-slot fill with too little growth failed. Full rings passed with zero growth. The recent missed cycle, the late first PASS, an already-allocated slot and the wrong handler index expose the limits of those positive tests. H1 distinguishes failed commit from successful return but underchecks the retry. |
| §2.C(4). Timing, reruns and handoff | The operator readings record 524,288 history bytes after the USB flash of .173. Task 6.1 checks that partition and waits for .173 to pass before .189 or .170. A firmware fix restarts Task Group 6, refreshes the before-readings and resets the WROOM epoch. In the supplied WROOM block, 86,399 seconds returned WAIT, 86,400 with a 30-second boot difference passed, and 31 seconds failed. The later `v2` comparison had the same inclusive boundary. The compile between the baseline and upload, and a first PASS close to 24 hours, still leave H01–H02. The operator requests the 24-hour readings before Review B, with the return path corrected as in M05. |

##### Confidence and limits

| HIGH finding | Basis for confidence in its reality |
|---|---|
| H01 | The supplied board evaluator returned PASS for a missed scheduled write and separately for legacy progress exclusively before the new boot. |
| H02 | Identical epochs and counters passed the 24-hour fixture. The time guard explains the result. |
| H03 | Replacing only the standalone evidence line with a sentence saying it was outstanding preserved final PASS. |
| H04 | Changing only where the row’s SHA appeared in a posted review changed rejection into acceptance. |
| H05 | A read-only API fixture changed the body during discussion retrieval. Preparation refused, while answer mode used its single PR read and wrote a candidate. |
| H06 | Changing the getter argument from the loop index to zero preserved D validation on the otherwise identical handler fixture. |
| H07 | Adding the existing recovery helper preserved D validation. The erase branch is present in the pinned product source. Its execution on a particular board is conditional. |

The chance of a missed HIGH remains material in live timing, flash behavior after a failed commit, and GitHub pagination or concurrent changes. Confidence is higher for the deterministic acceptance failures above. The vendor NVS page distinguishes an uninitialized storage driver (`ESP_ERR_NVS_NOT_INITIALIZED`) from a missing named partition (`ESP_ERR_NVS_PART_NOT_FOUND`) and requires registration before a named open. Both belong on the error path. Google’s terms permit human access to interactions and caution against submitting confidential data. The R6 public-data rule therefore applies on the named fallback as well under the operator’s decision. [ESP-IDF v5.5 NVS][nvs] · [Google Antigravity terms][google]

**Unable to verify live:** physical uploads, durable flash contents, the deployed HTTP response, the final PR’s size and CI results, or a real reviewer’s session. The future eight definitions do not exist at the product pin, so an end-to-end H1 run against the implementation was unavailable. Native checks exercised the supplied fake open/commit, not the future firmware. Board and GitHub tests used the supplied decision code with controlled inputs. No board, reviewer, dispatch or GitHub write was invoked for this audit.

##### Verdict

**FAIL. Hold dispatch. The turn‑1 verdict is unchanged.** All seven HIGH findings from this second re-audit remain open. The direct NVS open fixes the earlier writer-side erase hazard. The PR gate now rejects duplicate criteria and substituted browser jobs. Neither repair resolves the false passes involving a missed board write, absent 24-hour evidence, or a review credited to the wrong commit.

Section 3 also gives a specific reason to keep the Review B finding open. In a local replay, preparation screened one PR body, a separate read at the same head saw an edited body, and the original body was restored before answer checking. Both checks passed without reporting a change. The test used ordinary text and contacted no reviewer.

##### Disposition checks

R1 and R2 below are the two **earlier** reports reproduced in §3.2. H01–H07 in the findings section retain the IDs from **this** second re-audit’s turn 1. “Holds” closes the stated repair. “Partial” identifies the part to keep and the part to reopen. Severity judgments distinguish the original defect from any residue at the current pin. [Pinned instructions][instructions] · [Disposition register][register]

|---|---|
| R2-M2 → R1-M01 | **Partial, MEDIUM.** `(0 <= r0 < slots)` repairs the ordinary last-slot case. M03 remains for an unknown baseline or an already allocated slot. [Rings][rings] |
| R2-M3 → R1-M05 | **Partial.** `fn.count("dev_persist_status_get_(") != 1` checks frequency in source. The current-device requirement remains open at **HIGH**, H06. [D][dcheck] |
| R2-M4 → R1-M06 | **Holds for the diagnostic, MEDIUM.** The answer screen precedes any supplied header field in a diagnostic. The earlier report’s separate recommendation to screen the exact cleaned publication text remains unimplemented: `comment = rn.clean(answer) + "\n"` follows both screens. Apply those screens to the final candidate too. No disclosure was observed. [Answer validation][ansheader] |
| R2-L4 → R1-L01/L02 | **Holds, LOW.** “**Template version:** v1.8” and “one line and three passes” resolve the two revision labels. [Template][template] · [Plan][plan] |

###### Reopened section 2.3 rows and section 3 addendum

| Row | Judgment and rating |
|---|---|
| D1 · existing metadata read error | **Fix holds. The corrected data-loss rationale holds.** Item 2.2 says “it writes nothing” on a failed read. H1 checks `office_sets == 0 && g_blobs[omk] == kept`, another device’s success, and recovery at the preserved slot. The behavioral test region is byte-identical across the planning pins. The legacy writer iterates `i < NUM_SENSORS`, with three environmental sensors. The separate v2 list includes `wan_ping`, whose history is absent from the legacy snapshot. An overwritten slot can lose unique stored samples. Losing the index to the other slots is a separate consequence. The earlier **CRITICAL** impact rating is justified for actual irreversible loss under that fault sequence. Planning recorded HIGH. Close the repaired defect. No such loss was observed. [D1][d1] · [H1 read-error case][h1read] · [Live device map][model] · [Legacy writer][legacy] |
| D38 · comments and NVS helpers | **Partial.** D strips `//` comments on the allocation scan and explicitly includes `load_device_meta_`, `save_device_meta_`, `persist_device_segment_` and `persist_all_devices_v2` in its NVS pattern. The original line-comment and four named helper cases are fixed. A block comment containing “new” still rejects valid code, and the whole-handler count misses the pre-existing recovery helper. **MEDIUM** for a false checkpoint failure, **HIGH** for the missed recovery call. Reopen M04/H07. [D38 and D39][d38] · [D][dcheck] |
| D39 · when reviewers see 24-hour evidence | **Holds, LOW.** S6 says “they come after reviews A and C and before review B, so reviews A and C see them pending”. Operator steps 6–8 give the same order. Close the timing clarification. Final evidence enforcement remains H03. [Review S6][s6] · [Operator][operator] |
| D11 · §3.3 wording addendum | **Holds as a documentation correction, LOW.** The screen comment now says “a quoted value of 4 or more characters without a space”. The claim is limited to the detector’s stated forms. It gives no guarantee that all possible sensitive text is recognizable. [Screen comment][screen] |

##### CRITICAL findings

None open in the reviewed changes. D1’s historical impact is recorded above.

##### HIGH findings

Each row recommends a fix before dispatch. The tests exercised supplied validation logic on local inputs. Their outcomes establish defects in the gates independently of the firmware the coding agent will write.

| ID | File and verbatim evidence | Reproduced consequence | Recommended disposition |
|---|---|---|---|
| H01 · required hourly write | Checks: `old_fresh = now - (last or 0) <= FRESH`. [Source][v2] | At 00:11 a point from 23:00 was 4,260 seconds old. The missed 00:10 persist passed. Separately, legacy advancement during compilation, entirely before reboot, satisfied the first-upload check. | Require observed legacy and per-device advancement across the relevant scheduled persist on the new boot. Retain the age ceiling, reject implausibly future epochs, and WAIT when the observations cannot settle the result. |
| H02 · late first PASS | Checks: `if up - pup >= 3600:`. [Source][v2] | First PASS at uptime 85,200 seconds and final reading at 86,460 seconds passed with identical epochs and counts. | For 6.5, require the saved first PASS from this upload and a later eligible cycle. WAIT until the comparison is meaningful, then require legacy progress and three advancing device counts. |
| H03 · missing 24-hour evidence | Checks: `if x not in body:`. Prompt: “Put the outputs into the session log, the record and the PR body”. [Body][body] · [6.5][boardprompt] | A sentence saying the WROOM result was outstanding passed. A standalone WROOM PASS with the C3 reading and both later v2 results absent also passed. The record predicate accepted empty device evidence. | At final closeout, verify all four 6.5 outputs for the same upload in all three named records. Match actual output lines, board identities, boot and progress. Keep pre-24-hour F usable for earlier rounds. |
| H04 · wrong reviewed commit | Checks: `for h in re.findall(r"\b[0-9a-f]{7,40}\b", t)`. [Source][reviewrow] | A correctly headed review with a different `Head reviewed` passed because its findings mentioned the SHA in the PR row. | Bind each row to the unique opening header field of its posted review. Validate the full SHA there and resolve any allowed abbreviation against that field. Preserve legitimate reviews of earlier heads. |
| H05 · different Review B inputs | Checks: `data = ks.discussion(gh, pages, rn, repo, pr, pull)`. Disposition: “No snapshot attachment: a Gemini session reads the PR itself.” [Answer][answer] · [Register][register] | Answer mode missed a mid-read edit and wrote a posting candidate. In the separate edit-and-restore replay, preparation and answer saw identical text while the simulated reviewer saw a different body at the same head. A failed repeat also left the previous candidate on disk. | Check stability during answer validation, invalidate stale output and screen the final candidate. Bind the reviewer’s mutable inputs to the screened bytes, or explicitly leave equivalence unverified and obtain the operator’s disposition on that residual risk. A second read at answer time alone cannot establish what was read earlier. |
| H06 · wrong device reported | Checks: `fn.count("dev_persist_status_get_(") != 1`. Prompt: “one copy of each device's status”. [D][dcheck] · [4.1][handlerprompt] | D accepted a handler returning the first device’s status under every device ID. This can conceal a failed writer in the board evidence. | Check that the getter uses the current persist index inside the loop. Verify distinct per-device results, counters and cursors in the emitted rows. |
| H07 · HTTP handler reaches NVS recovery | Checks compare `len(re.findall(NVSCALL, fn))` to the baseline. The product helper contains `nvs_flash_erase_partition(HISTORY_PARTITION_LABEL)`. [D][dcheck] · [Recovery][recovery] | D rejected an added per-device writer call and accepted an added direct call to the legacy recovery helper. Actual erasure is conditional on the initialization error. | Constrain edits to the specified format string and insertion. Check the added reporting code for direct and indirect NVS work, including the recovery helper. Preserve the legacy handler calls already on the product pin. |

##### MEDIUM and LOW findings

| ID | File and verbatim evidence | Recommended disposition |
|---|---|---|
| M01 · open failure coverage | H1 samples `g_dev_persist_status[0].writes_failed == failed_before + 1` and `g_dev_persist_status[2].last_result == DEV_PERSIST_ERROR` for seven devices. [H1][h1open] | Check the error result and counter increment for every device, plus zero writes. Keep the R2-H4 direct open. |
| M02 · commit recovery | H1 accepts `last_result != DEV_PERSIST_ERROR`. [H1][h1commit] | Reopen the retry checks. With newly visible metadata, assert DEDUP and synchronized status. With the old metadata still visible and the same pending point, assert a successful retry at the original slot. Check epoch, cursor and counters. ESP-IDF permits writes before commit and specifies no general rollback on an error. [NVS][nvs] |
| M03 · ring occupancy | Checks use `pv[i].get("result") not in (None, "pending")` and `used - used0 < 7 * len(growing)`. [Source][rings] | EMPTY can precede a metadata read. Treat its unobserved zero as unknown. Require growth when a new physical key is established, and use cursor/epoch progress for already allocated slots. |
| M04 · D rejects a comment and misses an allocation | D uses `re.sub(r"//[^\n]*", "", tail)`. A valid block comment failed and an allocation earlier in the handler passed. [Source][dcheck] | Inspect all code added to the handler, ignoring both C++ comment forms while respecting literals. Keep the H07 region restriction. |
| M05 · unavailable fallback | The operator failure table says “Or run review B on the runner (step 8.5).” [Source][failure] | Remove that instruction. Use step 8’s same-model subscription fallback and the same checks. Keep a refusal binding on both venues. Qualify the generic record template’s venue list by the step’s selected fallback. |
| L01 · row and heading forms | F accepts `[+-]?` for the delta. §2 uses `grep -cx '## 2. You are here'`. [F][fcheck] · [§2][sec2] | Require the specified sign and comma grouping. Use a fixed-string full-line heading match. |
| L02 · quiet sensor interpretation | Prompt 6.4 says “a sensor that is quiet, stops the block”. The product uses `void add_gap(uint32_t epoch) { add(epoch, NAN); }`. [6.4][boardprompt] · [Buffer][gap] | Describe the three-device persistence threshold and timestamped gaps. A quiet fourth sensor can coexist with PASS. |
| L03 · recommendation destination, from R2-M6 | Item 7.6 says “and name the row”. F tests `if heads != ["§1", "§2", "§3", "§4", "§5", "§6", "§7"]:` and selected metric rows. [7.6][recordrule] · [F][frecord] | Verify at final review that a named recommendation destination exists. The record predicate passed a nonexistent destination. This is separate from the missing 24-hour records in H03. |

##### Confidence and verification limits

| Question | Evidence and remaining uncertainty |
|---|---|
| Reality of H01 and H02 | High for each. The supplied evaluator accepted a missed latest cycle and a first-upload legacy epoch predating boot (H01). It independently accepted unchanged counters at the final reading after a late first PASS (H02). The long-stall negative control failed as intended. |
| Reality of H03 and H04 | High for each. The final body validator accepted missing device outputs (H03) and the wrong header SHA when the expected SHA appeared elsewhere (H04). Correct-body, missing-job, scheduling-note and wrong-SHA-only controls distinguished the branches. |
| Reality of H05 | High for the input mismatch and stale publication candidate. Preparation, separate reads and answer validation were exercised with a fixed head and benign mutable text. Actual disclosure was neither attempted nor observed. The operator’s instruction to keep the PR quiet reduces likelihood. Google’s terms allow human access to interactions. [Antigravity terms][terms] |
| Reality of H06 and H07 | High for both D acceptance results. The correct-index control and the single changed argument isolated H06. A direct recovery call passed while a listed writer helper failed, isolating H07. Field impact depends on device values and driver state. |
| Chance of a missed HIGH | Material in physical flash failure behavior, real board timing and concurrent GitHub activity. Strongest coverage is for the deterministic local acceptance results. The two embedded reports describe the same older bundle. Their agreement alone does not verify a current fix. |
| Unable to verify | Independent C3, S3 and WROOM target builds, live OTA, flash durability after interruption, the final R6 PR and its exact runner input size, a real reviewer session and dispatch-day diagnostics. The producer reports three target builds. The current checks here included host syntax and compiled fake NVS behavior, not an end-to-end implementation or target build. The verdict does not depend on these unavailable observations. |

#### Dispositions of the second re-audit

The planning session checked each finding of reports R3 and R4 against the bundle at `e8f7219` and the product at `a33a5cc`, reproduced each HIGH with the rehearsal before fixing it, and rated it by PLAN-037. "Holds" means the finding is right and the bundle is fixed; the rehearsal cases named in each row pass after the fix (rehearsal part 2: 147 cases as expected; negative tests: 77 as expected; part 1 without the compiles: PRE-PR PASS; the firmware reference code did not change). The IDs H01 to L03 are those of turn 1 (R3), which turn 2 (R4) kept.

| ID | Finding (short) | Auditor | Planning session | Disposition and fix |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| H01 | A recent point (at most 5,400 s old) does not prove a write in the latest cycle; old-engine progress before the boot passed | HIGH | HIGH, holds | `v2` computes the latest persist cycle (minute 10, ended 60 s later; the boards' time zone has whole-hour offsets, so it is minute 10 of the UTC hour) and requires the newest persisted point of the old engine and of three devices to be a point of this boot from at most 25 minutes before that cycle, and not in the future. Rehearsal: "the old engine missed the latest cycle", "the device writers missed the latest cycle", "a persisted point in the future", "the old engine wrote only before the boot" (first cycle WAIT, later cycle FAIL) |
| H02 | A late first PASS lets the 24-hour reading pass without progress | HIGH | HIGH, holds | 6.5 runs `v2 <IP> later`: it needs the first PASS of 6.4 on the same boot, an uptime of 86,400 s or more, and a cycle that ended after the first PASS (else WAIT); then the progress of 6.4. Rehearsal: "later, no first PASS", "no cycle since the first PASS" (WAIT), "a late first PASS and nothing new since" (FAIL), "before 24 hours" (WAIT), "progress" (PASS) |
| H03 | A sentence naming `wroom24 PASS` passed `body final`; the other 24-hour outputs and the records were not checked | HIGH | HIGH, holds | `wroom24` and `v2 <IP> later` keep their PASS lines (board, boot, uptime, heap, progress) on the build host; `body <PR#> final` requires each kept line, unchanged, on a line of its own in the PR body and in the session log and the record at the head, and binds each to the boot of Task Group 6's reading after the upload (within 30 s) and an uptime of 86,400 s. The separate `dev .189` reading of 6.5 is dropped: `v2 <IP> later` prints the heap of both boards. Rehearsal: "a sentence that names wroom24 PASS", "the wroom24 line alone", "a kept PASS line missing", "a PASS line of an earlier upload", "the record at the head lacks a PASS line" |
| H04 | A review credited to the row's commit because its findings mention the commit | HIGH | HIGH, holds | The row of a review names one commit, which must be the field Head reviewed of the table that opens a posted review of that round, or its first 7 or more characters; a review of an earlier head beside it stays allowed. Rehearsal: "review B's header names another head, its text the row's", "review B without the field Head reviewed", "a review B of an earlier head beside the row's" (PASS) |
| H05 | Review B's answer check reads the discussion once; a failed run leaves an older `comment.md`; what the reviewer read is not bound to the screened bytes | HIGH | HIGH for the mechanical part, holds; the residual risk accepted by the operator | `reviewb <PR#> answer` deletes `comment.md` first, reads the head and the discussion twice and stops on a change between the reads, and screens the cleaned comment as well (R2-M4). The rest, that no read can show what a Gemini session read between the block's reads, is the residual risk that the operator accepted on 2026-11-25 (PLAN-048, decision 3): the operator keeps the PR quiet while review B runs; the step after R6 that moves review B to `agy -p` can give the reviewer a screened snapshot. Rehearsal: "a failed reviewb answer leaves no comment.md of an earlier run", "the discussion changes while the block reads", "the head moves while the block reads" |
| H06 | D accepted a handler that reads the status of device 0 for every device | HIGH | HIGH, holds | D requires one loop `for (<int type> i = 0; i < PERSIST_DEVICE_COUNT; i++)` in the added code, `dev_persist_status_get_(i)` and `PERSIST_DEVICE_IDS[i]` inside it with that index, and no other change of the index; item 4.1 states the same. A check on the board cannot replace it: healthy devices are written in the same cycles and show the same counts. Negative tests: "every device reads the status of index 0", "the id of another index", "the loop index changed in the loop", "a second loop" |
| H07 | D accepted an added call of the recovery helper `ensure_history_nvs_ready_` in the HTTP handler | HIGH | HIGH, holds | D now requires the handler to be `main`'s with only the end of the format string changed and code added between its last `resp->print(num);` and `request->send(resp);`, and the added code to call only `snprintf`, `resp->print`, `sizeof`, `dev_persist_status_get_`, `device_meta_key_` and `dev_persist_result_name_`; item 4.1 states the list. The handler's existing `open_history_nvs_` call on `main` stays, as the finding asks; the handoff records it for the read path of v7.7.1.4. Negative tests: "the recovery helper called in the added code", "a helper of another name called in the added code" |
| M01 | H1's open failure checks two devices | MEDIUM | MEDIUM, holds | H1 checks every device: `DEV_PERSIST_ERROR`, `writes_failed` + 1, `writes_ok` and `writes_skipped` unchanged, one open tried, nothing written. Negative test: "the open failure counts one device" |
| M02 | H1's retry after a failed commit only checks "no error" | MEDIUM | MEDIUM, holds | H1 now checks both cases that ESP-IDF allows after a failed `nvs_commit`: the new meta visible (the next cycle is DEDUP, `writes_skipped` + 1, the status shows that meta) and the old meta visible with the same point pending (the next cycle writes the same slot; cursor, count, epoch and `writes_ok` checked; the status shows the meta written) |
| M03 | `v2 before` takes a ring as known after EMPTY | MEDIUM | MEDIUM, holds | The ring of a device is known only after a cycle that loaded its meta (`ok` or `dedup`); otherwise -1. The growth check of a known ring stays as R1-M01 fixed it; an unknown ring grows only when it is not full now. A slot that a failed meta write left before the upload costs no new entry when it is written again; that needs a failed write in an earlier run, which stopped that run, and the block then stops (it fails safe) |
| M04 | D failed a block comment with "new" and missed an allocation earlier in the handler | MEDIUM | MEDIUM, holds | D strips both comment forms outside string literals and checks the whole added code; an allocation before the insertion point changes `main`'s part of the handler and fails. Negative tests: "an allocation earlier in the handler" (stops), "a block comment that says new" (passes) |
| M05 | The operator page's failure table sends review B to the runner, which cannot take R6's PR | MEDIUM | MEDIUM, holds | The row now points to step 8.5 (another Gemini surface); the refusal row binds every venue; the record template names the step's fallback instead of the runner alone |
| L01 | F accepts a malformed number and an unsigned delta; §2's heading match uses a regular expression | LOW | LOW, holds | F requires thousands commas (`[0-9]{1,3}(,[0-9]{3})*`) and a signed delta (or `0`); §2 uses `grep -cxF`. Negative tests: "a delta without its sign", "a number without its thousands comma" |
| L02 | 6.4 says a quiet sensor stops the block, but the buffer records a gap with its epoch | LOW | LOW, holds | 6.4 now says that a gap is persisted every 15 minutes when a sensor sent nothing, so only fewer than three devices that wrote stop the block |
| L03 | 7.6 asks to name the row of a routed recommendation; F does not check that it exists | LOW | LOW, holds | 7.6 gives the form `CURRENT-STATE.md, row "<its first cell>"`; F checks that each such row is a row of "Unimplemented Recommendations". Negative test: "a recommendation routed to a row that does not exist" |

### 3.5 Internal round 3, in place of a third re-audit (PLAN-048)

The operator chose on 2026-11-25 one internal sub-agent round of the changed parts in place of a third GPT Astra re-audit (PLAN-048, decision 2). Its prompt, verbatim (`<SP>` is the planning session's scratch folder):

````text
You are an independent auditor of a prompt bundle before it goes to a coding agent: an internal sub-agent round of
the planning workflow (PLAN-040, PLAN-048). The operator chose this round in place of a third external re-audit by
GPT Astra. You did not write the bundle, its fixes or the earlier reports.

Project: github.com/GCV-Sleeper-Service/ESP32-GW-multi-sensor. A clone of its main (a33a5cc) is at
<SP>/ESP32-GW-multi-sensor (read-only; make your own clones under your folder). The bundle is the step R6,
v7.7.1.3, "per-device persist write path, dual-write", tier MEDIUM. Your folder is <SP>/audit-r6c/. Inputs there:
r6-2026-11-25.tar (the agent prompt, its check file and twelve supplied files under files/), r6-setup.txt (the
setup file), readings.txt (the operator's board readings), stubs/gh (answers only "gh auth status"),
before/ (the tar and the setup file that GPT Astra's second re-audit read, at the planning commit e8f7219: diff
them against the new ones to find the changed parts). GPT Astra's second re-audit reports and the planning
session's dispositions are in earlier/: do NOT read them before step 4 below.
Do not read any other folder of <SP> (not rh/, not Planning-Workflow/, which is the private planning repository,
not work/ except work/c3/node_modules for a hard link, not audit-r6/ or audit-r6b/), and nothing on GitHub but the
product repository.
Environment: source <SP>/tc/env.sh; g++ is installed; copy <SP>/tc/secrets.yaml to firmware/secrets.yaml of your
clone; export ESP_USER=audit-user ESP_PASS=audit-value (placeholders); stage the /root paths of the setup file
under your own folder (edit REPO and SAVE only in a COPY of the check file; say what you changed). No board is
reachable. Use only the local ports 19100 to 19199 for any stand-in server, and stop every server and background
process that you started before you answer. Never push, never touch a board, never write a login, key or token value.

Scope: the parts changed since e8f7219. Audit them cold first:
- the check file: v2 (the latest persist cycle at minute 10 and the points of this boot; the mode "later" of 6.5
  with its kept PASS line; the ring baseline of "v2 before"), wroom24 (its kept PASS line), body (body final: the
  three kept PASS lines in the PR body, the session log and the record at the head, bound to Task Group 6's
  boots; the binding of a review row to the field Head reviewed of the review's header), reviewb answer (comment.md
  removed first, two reads, the cleaned comment screened), D (only the format string and the insertion; the
  allowed calls; one loop with the index; comments stripped), F (number form; a routed row exists), the §2 heading
  match, and the supplied host test H1 in the prompt (the open failure for every device; two commit-recovery cases);
- the prompt: 4.1, CHECKPOINT D, 6.4, 6.5, 7.3, 7.6, the criterion of the 24-hour readings in section 7, and
  the body rules in section 8;
- the supplied files: the decision log row PLAN-048 and every text that states the number of internal rounds
  (contract 5.4, Docs/planner-start-here.md step 6, the audit template v1.9 with its tier table, §7 note under
  step 4 and §9, the process guide's tier table, the plan of record), the operator page (step 7, the checklist
  before the merge, the failure table), the review file S6, the record template's reviewer row, the handoff's
  carry-forward list.
Decisions of the operator are not findings (PLAN-046, PLAN-047, PLAN-048 in the decision log of the bundle),
including the residual risk of review B that PLAN-048 (3) accepts. A finding that a decision is unsafe is welcome;
label it so. The planning session's design is open to findings. Pending by design: the PR number, the agent's
records, the review rounds, the gate record, the dispatch paragraph, the sections 3.4 and 3.5 of the audit file
(they take the second re-audit and this round), the operator's readings of the day.

Steps:
1. Run the setup file (staged) and the prompt with YOUR OWN implementation of 2.2 and 4.1, written from the prompt
   alone, through CHECKPOINT D, the host test and PRE-PR PASS (the compiles of CHECKPOINT E may be skipped: the
   firmware texts of the prompt that the compiles cover did not change since round 2; say so).
2. Exercise the blocks v2 (all three modes; place the persisted epochs before and after minute 10 of the hour, and

   final and reviewb answer as far as you can locally: a small local HTTP stand-in for /api/status/full and
   /api/storage-stats in a COPY of the check file with the board addresses mapped to your ports, and a stand-in gh

3. Report each finding: file, quoted text, why, a concrete fix, and a severity by PLAN-037 (CRITICAL, HIGH,

4. Only then read earlier/: for each finding H01 to L03 of the second re-audit, say whether its fix holds and

Output: "## Verdict: READY / FIX FIRST / BLOCKED", "## What I ran", "## Findings", "## Second re-audit fixes",
"## Questions". Begin with a table: the model as you know it, the context window, the reasoning effort, whether your

write files outside your folder).
````
