##### Verdict: READY

I found no CRITICAL or HIGH finding. There is one MEDIUM (M1): when .189 reboots after its first PASS, `v2 <IP> later` prints WAIT instead of stopping. The fix moves one existing test, so I recommend making it before dispatch. There are six LOW findings. All 14 fixes of the second re-audit (H01 to L03) hold when I reproduce them. Two of those fixes caused side effects: H02 caused M1, and H01/H02 caused L1.

##### What I ran

Everything ran in `.../scratchpad/audit-r6c/`, where `/root` is staged as `root/`. `origin` is a local bare clone of a33a5cc: I pushed only there, never to GitHub. `gh` is my own stand-in, `bin/gh`.

**Changes to check-file copies.** `checks-staged.sh` changes only REPO and SAVE. `checks-stand.sh`, used for steps 2 and 4, also changes these:
- `SAVE` can be overridden with `AUDIT_SAVE`.
- `sleep` does nothing.
- The board URLs point to 127.0.0.1 on ports 19173, 19189 and 19170.
- The time in `status_full` comes from `AUDIT_NOW`.

**Setup file (staged).**
- Step 0: 14 of 14 OK.
- Steps 1, 2 and 4: the branch, the draft PR (#900 in my stand-in) and the verbatim commit; step 4's check gave 2 of 2 OK.

**Prompt run, with my own 2.2 and 4.1 written from the prompt alone.**
- §2 PASS; scope (47 paths); copy (12 files) and CHECKPOINT 0; A; insert S2; my 2.2; insert T3; B; host (`H1: all checks passed`); C; my 4.1; D.
- Version bump and pipeline.
- E: I skipped the compiles. The firmware texts the compiles cover did not change since round 2: the S2 and T3 hashes in `supplied()` are unchanged, and only H1's hash changed. Instead, I compiled my inserted 4.1 code with g++ against a stub `resp` and parsed its JSON: valid, 972 characters with maximum values.
- Task Group 7, my own text; F failed once on my own wording ("No read path yet" capitalised), then passed after the fix.
- `PRE-PR PASS at 4128bd4…` (scope-gate 45 of 47 paths; lint; preflight including `playwright_manifest_spec`).

**Board blocks against a simulator** (`sim/boardsim.py`). It models points at :00, :15, :30 and :45, cycles at minute 10, the old engine restored at boot, and v2 metas kept across boots.
- Healthy sweep: uploads at minutes 12, 20, 33, 47, 55, 5 and 9. `v2` read at 9 positions per upload, from :10:20 to :59 and the next hours, then `later` read at 24 h. 105 checks, none unexpected: WAIT only before a cycle with a new point, never a false FAIL.
- `later` at minutes 2, 9, 10, 11, 12, 30 and 59, plus `wroom24`. Below 24 h both WAIT; at 24 h both PASS and keep their lines.
- Eight faults: v2 stops, old engine stops, OK without NVS growth, stale points, error, nvs_low, two devices empty, cycle-time epoch. Every broken writer gave FAIL; the cycle-time epoch, which is only a reporting variant, passed.
- Also run: reboot; missing samples (`:00` only passes; two missing gives WAIT then stop); clock skew of -100, +100 and +170 s; Task Group 6 run again with full rings; the H01 and H02 reproductions.

**Review round with the `gh` stand-in.**
- `inline` PASS; `body` PASS.
- Reviews A and C posted. The H04 negative (the row names a commit that the review mentions only in its findings) gave FAIL.
- `reviewb`, then `reviewb answer` with: a good answer; a missing field; another head; a login literal; a discussion change injected at gh calls 2 to 8; a login posted after the line.
- `body final` PASS, then 10 negative variants (N1 to N7 and N3).

**Mutation tests.** D: 21 variants. H1: 11 variants.

Every stand-in server ran inside its driver and stopped with it. No listener is left on 19100 to 19199.

##### Findings

**M1 — MEDIUM.** File: `v7.7.1.3-checks.sh`, `b_v2`, later mode. Quoted text:
```
if mode == "later" and up < 86400:
    print("WAIT the uptime is %d s, below 86,400 s: 24 hours have not passed since the upload; ...")
if os.path.isfile(passfile):   ...
    if abs(boot - pboot) > 30:   # "it rebooted after the upload. STOP and report"
```
- **Why:** .189 rebooted after its first PASS. `v2 192.168.120.189 later`, run 24 h after the upload, printed `v2 WAIT the uptime is 47300 s ... 24 hours have not passed since the upload`. It stopped only once the new boot itself reached 86,400 s, up to a day later. Within 4,500 s of a reboot, the `first` WAIT comes first instead. 6.5 says a reboot stops. .170 is covered because `wroom24` runs first and stops on a reboot. Since H03 dropped the `dev .189` reading of 6.5, `v2 .189 later` is the only 24-hour check of .189. There is no false PASS: `body final` and the eventual stop both catch the reboot.
- **Fix:** in later mode, compare the boot with the kept first PASS (or with `dev-<IP>-v7.7.1.3.txt`) before any WAIT. Add a rehearsal case: a reboot after the first PASS, then `later` at 24 h after the upload.

**L1 — LOW.** File: `b_v2`. Quoted text: `base = now - 60; CYCLE = base - base % 3600 + 600`.
- **Why:** the cycle is computed on the build host's clock. I set a board clock 100 s behind the host; after the first hour, a reading at host minute 11:10 failed with `the old engine's newest persisted point ... is not a point of this boot` on a healthy board. NTP makes this unlikely.
- **Fix:** a grace of 180 s, or WAIT while `now - CYCLE` is under 300 s and the newest point is exactly one cycle old.

**L2 — LOW.** File: `b_D`. D still has these gaps:
- (a) It does not check that the JSON is valid. A handler printing `]}` instead of `]}}` passes D and is found only at `v2 .173`, which costs about an hour and a reflash.
- (b) `DevicePersistStatus status;` declared before the loop, with `if (i == 0) status = dev_persist_status_get_(i);` inside it, passes D. This is the H06 defect class (device 0's status for every device) in another form.
- (c) A call written `(nvs_get_stats)(...)` gets past the allowed-call list. This only matters for deliberate evasion.
- (d) "Any integer type" rejects `int32_t` and `long`, and the functional cast `unsigned(x)` counts as a call.
- **Fix:** have D compile the inserted region with g++ against a stub `resp` and S2, with a different status per device, then parse and compare each row. That takes about 30 lines; I did it in `hjson/`. Failing that, require `<Type> <name> = dev_persist_status_get_(i);` as the first statement of the loop body.

**L3 — LOW.** File: H1 in the prompt. The case `"the new meta visible after a failed nvs_commit: dedup"` rejects a write path that writes back the old meta after a failed commit. My mutation of this kind failed H1. 2.2 does not forbid such a rollback, yet the prompt says each H1 case is "a sentence of 2.2". **Fix:** add to 2.2, step 8: "after a failed `save_device_meta_` it writes nothing more for the device in that cycle".

**L4 — LOW.** Round-count texts against PLAN-048.
- (a) PLAN-048 (1) says "each tier still runs one internal round ..., and a further round follows a new HIGH or MEDIUM", which reads as mandatory for every tier. Contract 5.4, `Docs/planner-start-here.md` step 6 and template v1.9 say "LOW runs one round; MEDIUM may run, and HIGH runs".
- (b) No text says what a HIGH step does when the cap of three is reached and a further round would be required.
- (c) Template §9 says "every tier = one internal sub-agent round, at most three per planning session" and drops the further-round clause.
- (d) The audit file is unchanged: line 3 says "one internal round, a second after a new HIGH or MEDIUM", and line 420 says "No third internal round runs".
- **Fix:** align the wording, add who decides at the cap, and update the audit file's line 3 together with §3.5.

**L5 — LOW.** File: the operator page's failure table.
- It has no row for the two WAITs of `v2 <IP> later` (below 86,400 s; no cycle since the first PASS). The `v2 WAIT` row describes only the first cycle.
- No row matches v2's reboot message (`it rebooted after the upload`).
- Audit template line 33 still says "or the runner"; M05 changed only the record template.
- **Fix:** add these rows, and qualify line 33 the way the record template was qualified.

**L6 — LOW.** File: `b_body`, `reviewed()`. It needs exactly the field name `Head reviewed` with a full 40-character commit.
- **Why:** a review A or C that writes the short commit GitHub shows, or a bold field name, cannot be credited. Nothing checks A or C before they are posted (B has its answer check), and the agent cannot repair a reviewer's comment, so the round stalls.
- **Fix:** accept 7 or more hex characters when they are a prefix of the row's commit. Alternatively, have the operator page say: check the A and C header before posting and ask for the full commit.

##### Questions

1. Is the boards' SNTP time reliably within 60 s of the build host's clock? If it is not, L1 becomes MEDIUM.
2. Does the cap of three internal rounds per planning session also cover the bundles of several steps that one session produces, and who decides when the cap is reached (L4)?

I did not use memory, and I have nothing new for it. My audit tools are in `.../scratchpad/audit-r6c/`: `sim/boardsim.py`, `sim/drive.py`, `sim/tg6.py`, `sim/h01.py`, `checks-stand.sh`, `bin/gh`, `hjson/main.cpp`, plus the logs `prepr.log` and `tg6.out`.

#### Dispositions

The planning session checked each finding against the bundle and the product at `a33a5cc` before acting. Every finding holds. All are fixed apart from L2 (a) and (c), which are left with the reasons below. The new cases pass in the rehearsal, and the negative tests stop as they should (see the gate record for the counts).

| ID | Auditor | Planning session | Disposition |
|---|---|---|---|
| M1 | MEDIUM | MEDIUM, holds | The source confirms it: in `v2`, the WAIT of the mode `later` below 86,400 s (and, within 4,500 s of a reboot, the WAITs of a first cycle) came before the check against the boot of the kept first PASS. Fixed: when a first PASS is kept, `v2` compares the boot with it right after the check that an upload happened, before any WAIT; `later` without a kept first PASS stops, and only then comes the WAIT below 86,400 s. Item 6.4 says that a later `v2 <IP>` stops at once after a reboot. Rehearsal case: "v2 173 later after a reboot since the first PASS" (FAIL; before the fix, WAIT) |
| L1 | LOW | LOW, holds | `status_full` takes the time of the reading from the build host (`int(time.time())`); the boards keep SNTP time (`platform: sntp`, line 175 of the C3 YAML). Fixed: a cycle counts as ended 180 s after minute 10, and a point up to 300 s after the host's time is not in the future; item 6.4 says so. Question 1: the boards' offset from the host was not measured; the grace covers 180 s |
| L2 | LOW | LOW, holds | (b) Fixed: the copy `DevicePersistStatus <name> = dev_persist_status_get_(i);` (or `const`, or `auto`) must be the first statement of the loop; item 4.1 says so. (d) Fixed: the loop index may have any of `int`, `long`, `unsigned`, `size_t`, `int8_t` to `uint32_t`, and a functional cast to these types is no call. (a) Not fixed: `v2` at .173, the test board that is uploaded first, parses the JSON and stops; the agent repairs its own edit once (setup file step 10) and repeats the reading, about an hour; a compile harness in D would add a second build environment to the check file. (c) Not fixed: it takes deliberate evasion, and reviews A and C read the handler. Negative tests: "the status of device 0 kept from the first pass of the loop" (stops), "a loop index of int32_t and a cast unsigned(x)" (passes) |
| L3 | LOW | LOW, holds | Fixed: step 8 of 2.2 says that after a failed `save_device_meta_` the writer writes nothing more for the device in that cycle, which is what H1's case "the new meta visible after a failed nvs_commit: dedup" tests |
| L4 | LOW | LOW, holds | Fixed: PLAN-048 (1) uses the tier wording of contract 5.4 (LOW one round; MEDIUM may, HIGH does, run a further round after a new HIGH or MEDIUM); contract 5.4, the planner page, the audit template §7 note and PLAN-048 say that a planning session that has run three rounds and owes a further one stops and asks the operator; template §9 keeps the further-round clause; the audit file's introduction names the third round, and its §1 sentence "No third internal round runs" keeps its text with a note that points to section 3.5. Question 2: yes, the cap counts every step that one planning session produces ("over every step that it produces") |
| L5 | LOW | LOW, holds | Fixed: the operator page's failure table has a row for the WAITs of `v2 ... later` and one for `v2`'s reboot stop; audit template line 33 names the step's fallback like the record template |
| L6 | LOW | LOW, holds | Fixed: the field may be bold or in backticks and may hold 7 to 40 hex characters; the row's commit and the field must agree, the one the first characters of the other. Rehearsal cases: "review B's header with a short commit" (PASS), "a bold field Head reviewed" (PASS), "a short commit of another head" (FAIL) |
